About 0.42 / 0.53 MB of it is KEM code suite 0x0003 never runs (P-256 and ML-KEM-512/1024, linked because `hpke-rs-libcrux` dispatches over every KEM at run time; Q16); `openmls_memory_storage` is dropped by the linker. **Budget: ≤ +3 MiB (3 145 728 B) per ABI, enforced per ABI.** It holds, but x86_64 has only **229 344 B (7.3 %)** of headroom, and the measured probe lacks most of `ghost-client-mls`'s own code (the 20 JNI functions, the seal, the codecs, the reveal): **S2's exit re-measures with the real crate by the V10-3 method and fails above the budget on either ABI**, and every OpenMLS bump re-measures. `apk-size:` reports the library per ABI.
- **Portable build (V10-7, S0-8).** Of the libcrux crates the provider adds (`libcrux-aead`, `libcrux-aes`, `libcrux-chacha20poly1305`, `libcrux-poly1305`, `libcrux-ed25519`, `libcrux-hkdf`, `libcrux-hmac`, `libcrux-sha2`, `libcrux-hmac-drbg`), only `libcrux-aes` has a build script with SIMD switches (and the stale-cfg hazard, reproduced); no provider crate has SIMD or arch code, and every Android build of S0 printed zero `cargo:rustc-cfg` lines for the four SIMD-capable packages. The rule of F9's G-R3 extends to them through the extracted script of G-M9.
- **Reproducibility.** T12's same-path identity and `native-libs.sha256` cover the grown library (checked in S0: the `mls-probe` variant rebuilt at the same source path in a fresh target directory is byte-identical on both ABIs, evidence §3).
- **Canonical leaves freeze the shape (S0-4, S0-5).** Every KeyPackage and leaf of a channel has one canonical form per suite (§6.2.4 (B)), with no GREASE values (RFC 9420 §13.5 recommends them). Any new capability or extension type is therefore a flag day — every member must first run a client that accepts the new canonical form, which CO-2 then admits — or a new channel incarnation (§4.4); a PQ suite has its own canonical form (2 765 B with X-Wing, V10-9).
- **Bumps.** Exact pins; a bump is its own PR running golden channel states per pin (`client-core/mls/tests/golden/openmls-<version>/`) with an upgrade test (load the previous pin's states, continue every group), G-M7 (with the golden sizes of §5.7: a bump that eats the 172-byte margin fails), G-M8, the WG vectors, OpenMLS's suite at the new tag with every suite, the size re-measure above, the GHSA check of G-M1, the full `channel-exit-gate`, and a CHANGELOG review of storage format, wire format and error semantics.

### 3.9 Licensing (normative; ADR-37)

The OpenMLS stack adds **three MPL-2.0 crates** (`hpke-rs`, `hpke-rs-crypto`, `hpke-rs-libcrux`), required by both OpenMLS crypto providers. ADR-37 point 2 allows MPL-2.0 unmodified with the exact source published on the distribution channels and a note in the licence screen; the two existing MPL packages (`option-ext`, `priority-queue`) already follow that rule through G-L4 (`mpl-sources.sh`). Decision (Q16): accept the three crates unmodified, add them to G-L4 and the notices, no `[patch]`. The alternative — a GHOST `OpenMlsCrypto` over RustCrypto's `hpke` (MIT/Apache-2.0; already pulled, linker-dropped, by vodozemac) — removes MPL but adds a GHOST-written crypto provider to the audit scope; it would also drop the 0.42 / 0.53 MB of KEM code suite 0x0003 never runs (§3.8, V10-3). Everything else in the graph is MIT, Apache-2.0 or dual — **no strong copyleft; the three MPL-2.0 crates are file-level copyleft** (V10-1: no GNU text, GNU SPDX identifier, MPL header or "Signal Messenger" copyright in any of the 566 source files of the 22 added crates); `cargo deny check licenses` must stay green (G-M1, green in S0). **Notices (V10-1):** none of the OpenMLS, `hpke-rs` or libcrux `.crate` archives ships a licence file, so the notices generator takes the MIT text with OpenMLS's copyright line ("Copyright (c) 2020 OpenMLS Authors", repository `LICENSE` at `3a3e35de`) and the MPL-2.0 and Apache-2.0 texts from the SPDX snapshot, as Phase 9 did for libcrux.

---

## 4. Post-quantum posture (normative; ADR-12 applied, CP-10)

### 4.1 Facts (checked 2026-09-25)

- **draft-ietf-mls-pq-ciphersuites-06** (2026-07-21): 11 suites, code points **TBD1–TBD11**, state "Waiting for WG Chair Go-Ahead" with "Revised I-D Needed – Issue raised by WG"; its hybrids give "authenticity against classical attacks only".
- **X-Wing** (draft-connolly-cfrg-xwing-kem-11, 2026-09-23): an Independent Submission; HPKE KEM value **25722 (0x647A) requested**.
- **OpenMLS 0.9.0 / openmls_traits 0.6.0**: `MLS_256_XWING_CHACHA20POLY1305_SHA256_Ed25519 = 0x004D` (an OpenMLS-specific code point, doc comment "X-WING KEM draft-01") and the WG provisional `MLS_128_MLKEM768X25519_AES128GCM_SHA256_Ed25519 = 0x004F` ("TBD1 (provisional code point)"), both behind `draft-ietf-mls-pq-ciphersuites`, both mapped to the HPKE KEM `XWingKemDraft6` (`openmls_traits-0.6.0/src/types.rs:449-472, 789-793`). The libcrux provider lists 0x0001, 0x0003 and 0x004D as supported (`crypto.rs:88-96`); its `supports()` checks the AEAD, the signature scheme and the hash, **never the KEM** (`crypto.rs:64-86`). **Measured (V10-9, S0-10):** 0x004D, 0x004F and 0x004E (TBD2, also in `types.rs`) are all mapped to the same HPKE KEM `XWingKemDraft6` (`types.rs:788-794`); the provider does not list 0x004F or 0x004E but accepts them, and groups on all three work end to end at 20 and 32 members — one implementation talking to itself: conformance to the KEMs the WG draft assigns to its code points was not tested.
- **Sizes (measured with GHOST's extensions, V10-9; blank tree, bytes):** X-Wing KeyPackage 2 765; at 20 members a Remove 28 842, a PCS commit 30 006, an Add with its evidence 33 018 and its Welcome 37 986; at 32 members 42 896 / 44 060 / 48 291 / 56 447 (0x004E with AES-256 and SHA-384: 0.3–1.2 % larger). Every PQ blob needs the 64 KiB bucket from 20 members; each member adds about 1.17 KB to a blank-tree commit, so C(65536) caps a channel near 46 members for Adds and 38 for Welcomes. Classical 0x0003 with GHOST's extensions: §5.7. (The research probe's figures — at 100 members a sparse X-Wing remove of 125 KB, at 1 000 members 1.18 MB — stay as the order of magnitude beyond the caps.)
- **No suite migration API**: RFC 9420's ReInit is not exposed (`om:src/group/mls_group/proposal_store.rs:608-609`, "TODO #751"); changing a channel's suite means a new group.

### 4.2 Decision

**P0 channels use 0x0003 only.** ADR-12's clause for MLS is kept literally: the hybrid suite is adopted "when stable", and by every available criterion it is not (TBD code points, an experimental OpenMLS code point, no audit of that path). Choosing 0x004D now would make every channel created in Phase 10 depend on a code point that neither the IETF nor the OpenMLS maintainers treat as final, with 64 KiB blobs from 20 members and a cap near 38–46 members (V10-9), and the choice cannot be migrated in place.

### 4.3 What is and is not protected (normative wording for LIMITE L8-C1)

| Layer | Against a harvest-now-decrypt-later adversary (records relay blobs now, breaks X25519 later) |
|---|---|
| Channel posts, commits, Welcomes | **not protected** (MLS key schedule from X25519 HPKE; the outer seal derives from the MLS exporter) |
| Reveal payloads (§6.9) | not protected (X25519 HPKE) — consistent with PQ-R8 (cards travel on classical channels) |
| Relay credentials, namespaces | not confidential material against that adversary (the relay already sees them) |
| DMs (Phase 9) | protected at the seal layer (ADR-36), unchanged |

### 4.4 Triggers and the migration path

Adopt a hybrid suite when all hold: (1) the WG assigns final code points or OpenMLS declares a suite non-experimental; (2) the external review covers OpenMLS's hybrid HPKE path and GHOST's use; (3) S0-style probes show commits within the class floors at the class caps, or GHOST adds fragmentation (Phase 11's blob fragmentation is a candidate); (4) the suite's canonical KeyPackage and leaf are defined and measured like 0x0003's (the canonical form is per suite, S0-4), and the implementation is checked against the WG draft's code points by a second implementation or vectors (V10-9 tested OpenMLS only against itself). Migration: the owner creates a new channel on the new suite ("incarnation"), members are invited with the invite flow of §6.2 (a new `channel_id`, hence new pseudonyms — the UI must say so), the old channel is left. The invite carries the suite (§5.9), so clients refuse a suite they do not support.

### 4.5 CP-10 wording

Forbidden for channels: "post-cuantic", "quantum", "PQ", any comparison implying DM-level protection. The Phase 13 copy states: "Mesajele din canale folosesc criptografie clasică (X25519); spre deosebire de mesajele directe, nu sunt protejate împotriva unui viitor calculator cuantic."

---

## 5. Channel model and data formats (normative)

### 5.1 Objects and identifiers

| Object | Definition | Who knows it |
|---|---|---|
| `channel_id` | 32 random bytes from the creator's CSPRNG (Rust); also the MLS `group_id` | members, invite holders, removed members |
| pseudonym seed `ps` | `HKDF-SHA256(ikm = root entropy, salt = ∅, info = "ghost/v1/channel-pseudonym" ‖ u8(32) ‖ channel_id, L = 32)` (`KeyDerivation.kt:24-30`; unchanged) | the member; derivable from the seed |
| pseudonym key `P` | Ed25519 public key of `ps` (RFC 8032); the leaf signature key and the `BasicCredential` identity | members |
| box key | X25519 key pair drawn from the CSPRNG (Rust) for every new leaf of the member — its KeyPackage and each update path it commits; `b_pk` published in that leaf (§5.4), `b_sk` stored wrapped in `ch_box_key` and deleted with the retention of the last epoch whose tree held that leaf (§19.17; round 10b's seed-derived `b_sk` gave reveals no forward secrecy) | members (`b_pk`); the member (`b_sk`) |
| `op_id` | 16 random bytes per post, reaction or request (the spec's "client operation ID") | members |
| invite | §5.9; `invite_id` 16 random bytes; invite signing key from a 32-byte random seed | sponsor, invitee (secret); all members (the public key, via `INVITE_ISSUED`) |
| generation g | counter of generations; `G_g` its secret (§5.2) | members of any epoch of g |

### 5.2 Derivations (normative; labels are part of the compatibility contract)

`Exporter_e(label, context, L)` is `MlsGroup::export_secret` at epoch e (RFC 9420 §8.5). `HKDF(ikm, salt, info, L)` is HKDF-SHA256.

| Value | Derivation | Held by |
|---|---|---|
| epoch key `K_ep(e)` | `Exporter_e("ghost/v1/ch/epoch", channel_id, 32)` | members of epoch e |
| epoch recognition key `R_ep(e)` | `HKDF(K_ep(e), ∅, "ghost/v1/ch-recognition", 32)` | the same; Kotlin holds only this one (CS-S6) |
| generation secret `G_g` | `Exporter_{E_g}("ghost/v1/ch/generation", channel_id, 32)`, where `E_g` is the epoch whose commit opened generation g (epoch 0 for g = 0) | members of g; joiners get it in the bootstrap (§5.4) |
| namespace `ns_g` | `HKDF(G_g, ∅, "ghost/v1/ch/ns", 32)` | members of g, invite holders of g |
| read base `RB_g`, deposit base `DB_g` | `HKDF(G_g, ∅, "ghost/v1/ch/read-base", 32)`, `HKDF(G_g, ∅, "ghost/v1/ch/deposit-base", 32)` | the same |
| per-slot secrets `RS_{g,s}`, `DS_{g,s}` | `HKDF(RB_g, ∅, "ghost/v1/ch/read" ‖ u8(s), 32)`, `HKDF(DB_g, ∅, "ghost/v1/ch/deposit" ‖ u8(s), 32)` | the same |
| credentials | bearer credential v3 of F9 §4.3, unchanged: `3 ‖ kind ‖ u8(s) ‖ ns_g ‖ RS_{g,s}` (kind 0x01 read) and `… DS_{g,s}` (0x03 deposit); verifiers `SHA-256(label(kind) ‖ u8(s) ‖ ns_g ‖ secret)` | presented to relays |
| join key `JK_g`, recognition `R_join(g)` | `HKDF(G_g, ∅, "ghost/v1/ch/join-key", 32)`; `HKDF(JK_g, ∅, "ghost/v1/ch-recognition", 32)` | members of g, invite holders of g, members removed at the end of g |
| invite key | Ed25519 from `invite_seed` (32 random bytes in the invite) | invitee (secret), all members (public, via `INVITE_ISSUED`) |
| committers digest (S0-15; the set, §19.27 TR-6) | `SHA-256("ghost/v1/ch/committers" ‖ channel_id ‖ u8(k) ‖ c_0 ‖ … ‖ c_{k − 1})` where `c_0 < … < c_{k − 1}` are the `committers` of `GhostChannelContext` (§5.3) **sorted bytewise ascending**, so a reorder (a transfer of rank 0) leaves it unchanged and an add or removal changes it; a public commitment, not a key (rule 1 does not apply) | members; invite holders (in the invite, §5.9) |
| invite seal key (§19.28.3, corrected §19.29.3) | `HKDF(ikm, salt, "ghost/v1/ch/invite-seal" ‖ kdf_code, 32)` with `ikm = Argon2id(password, salt, profile(kdf_code))` for a password invite and `ikm = salt` for an open one | whoever holds the link (and, for a password invite, the password) |

**Rules.** (1) Every derivation takes a secret key input (an exporter output, `G_g`, `ps` or a random seed); none takes `channel_id`, a pseudonym key or a KeyPackage as its key input (T36; gate G-M3 is the static half, the F9 G-DM3 pattern). **The one declared exemption (§19.29.3):** the open invite's seal key derives from the salt printed in the link, a public value; that seal gives open and password invites one format, not confidentiality — the link itself is the bearer secret (§5.9) — and G-M3 admits it only through the typed input `OpenInviteSalt`. (2) The generation that follows a rotating commit is derived from **that commit's new epoch**, which a removed member cannot compute (RFC 9420: the path secrets are encrypted to the remaining members' nodes). (3) The invite carries `ns_g`, `RB_g`, `DB_g` and `JK_g`, never `G_g`. (4) Vectors: `protocol/test-vectors/ch_derive.txt`, replayed by Rust and by an independent JDK implementation (the F9 §15.2 practice).

### 5.3 The GHOST GroupContext extension 0xF0A1 `GhostChannelContext` (TLS presentation language, RFC 9420 §2.1)

```
struct {
    uint8   version;               // 1
    opaque  channel_id[32];        // equal to group_id
    uint8   size_class;            // 0 small: <= 32 members, floor 4 KiB; 1 large: <= 100 members, floor 16 KiB
    uint8   timer_code;            // the F9 §10.1 codes: 0 off, 1 5 min, 2 1 h, 3 1 d, 4 7 d (default), 5 30 d
    uint16  timer_version;         // +1 at every timer change
    uint8   history_policy;        // 0 = none (the only value in P0)
    uint8   flags;                 // bit 0: members may invite; bit 1 reserved (sponsor confirms, Q30; zero in P0);
                                   // bit 2: joining requires a password (§19.28); bits 3-7 zero
    uint16  idle_evict_days;       // 35..70, default 63 (§19.4)
    uint16  pw_version;            // 0 until a password is first set; +1 at every set or change (§19.28.3, §19.29.3)
    opaque  name<V>;               // UTF-8, 0..48 bytes (RFC 9420 §2.1.2 variable-length vectors)
    opaque  committers<V>;         // 1..8 pseudonym keys, 32 bytes each, distinct; rank = index; [0] = owner
    opaque  funders<V>;            // 0..3 pseudonym keys, 32 bytes each, a subset of the members
} GhostChannelContext;             // at most 448 bytes (varint length headers: 1 byte for name, 2 for the key lists)
```

- Extension type 0xF0A1 is in RFC 9420's private-use range (0xF000–0xFFFF). The group's `RequiredCapabilities` lists 0xF0A1 and 0xF0A2, so a KeyPackage that does not support them cannot be added (RFC 9420 §11.1).
- **Validity (checked by every member on every commit, after `process_message`, before any merge; §6.4 CO-2; every rank is read from the `committers` of the context **before** the commit, §19.13):** canonical encoding; every committer and funder key is the signature key of a current leaf after the commit; `history_policy = 0`; `timer_code ≤ 5`; `size_class ≤ 1` and the member count after the commit ≤ the class cap; `35 ≤ idle_evict_days ≤ 70`; reserved flag bits (1, 3–7) zero; **the password policy (§19.28, corrected §19.29.3):** `pw_version` never decreases and grows by at most one per commit; setting flag bit 2 (0 → 1) increases it by one, clearing bit 2 leaves it unchanged, and an increase while bit 2 stays set is a password change; only rank 0 changes bit 2 or `pw_version`; with Q46 (b), a context with both bit 0 and bit 2 set is invalid (in a password channel only committers invite). **The joiner checks the same validity on the joined context** (`welcomeJoin`, §6.2.5): in particular every committer is a current leaf, which a forger's fresh group naming the real committers cannot satisfy (E-71).
- **The generation number is not in the context.** It is a deterministic count every member keeps (§6.5) and joiners receive in the bootstrap; keeping it out means a rotation needs no GroupContextExtensions proposal and no extra bytes.

### 5.4 Leaf extension 0xF0A2 and GroupInfo extension 0xF0A3

```
struct { uint8 version; opaque box_pk[32]; uint64 refresh_epoch; } GhostLeaf;  // 41 bytes, in every LeafNode (§19.5)
struct { uint8 version; uint32 generation; uint64 open_epoch; opaque G[32]; } GhostBootstrap;   // 45 bytes
struct { uint8 version; opaque channel_id[32]; uint8 purpose; uint32 day; } GhostKpBind;       // 38 bytes, KeyPackage extension 0xF0A4 (§19.3)
```

- `GhostLeaf` is covered by the leaf signature (RFC 9420 §7.2) and is the leaf's **only** extension, under exactly the canonical capabilities (CO-2, S0-5). A leaf whose `box_pk` is a low-order or non-canonical X25519 point is refused (the F9 §22.8.2 rule). `box_pk` is fresh in every new leaf of the member (§5.1, §19.17); it may therefore change only in the member's own update-path leaf, which also keeps its signature key and credential (CO-2, §19.14). `refresh_epoch` is 0 in a KeyPackage leaf and, in the leaf of a commit's update path (set through `CommitBuilder::leaf_node_parameters`, `commit_builder.rs:359`), the epoch the commit is for; CO-2 checks both (§19.5).
- `GhostKpBind` (a KeyPackage extension, signed with the KeyPackage; never in the tree) binds a KeyPackage to one channel, one purpose (1 join, 2 rejoin, 3 restore) and the creator's trusted day; admission refuses a KeyPackage bound to another channel or purpose, or whose day is more than 3 days after the committer's day (σ of Phase 7) or more than 7 days before it (a request's pending window) (§19.3).
- `GhostBootstrap` gives a joiner the current generation. **It travels only in the Welcome's encrypted GroupInfo** through `CommitBuilder::create_group_info_with_extensions` (`om:src/group/mls_group/commit_builder.rs:569`), followed by `create_group_info(false)` so that no exported GroupInfo is built (V10-8, S0-9: the 45-byte extension reaches the Welcome's GroupInfo, the joiner reads it from `ProcessedWelcome::unverified_group_info()` and uses it only after `JoinBuilder::build()` succeeded, and a Welcome whose GroupInfo signature is wrong is refused with `InvalidGroupInfoSignature`). Round 10c's fallback under `K_boot(e)` and the `boot_flag` of the WELCOME body are **withdrawn** (V10-8 made them unnecessary). It must never be readable under `JK_g` alone (P-C4(b); mutant PC-11). **The GroupInfo signature is not enough (V10-8, S0-15):** RFC 9420 §12.4.3.1 verifies it against the `signer` leaf of the ratchet tree the same Welcome carries, so it proves only that someone holding a leaf of *that* tree signed. A holder of `JK_g` who is not a committer — an invite holder, a member removed at the end of g, a current member — can open a join request, build its own group with `group_id = channel_id` and a well-formed `GhostChannelContext`, add the joiner's KeyPackage and deposit the Welcome; OpenMLS accepts it (measured), and a join that is applied consumes the KeyPackage, so the real Welcome then fails (`NoMatchingKeyPackage`, measured). The joiner therefore checks the **committers anchor** before writing anything, on the **unverified** GroupInfo before the ratchet tree is validated (§6.2.5; §19.27 TR-7): for a join or restore, the set digest of the context's `committers` equals the invite's `committers_digest` (§5.9) and the GroupInfo signature verifies under one of them; for a rejoin, the GroupInfo signature verifies under one of the committers of the member's last final epoch (§6.7), with **no digest comparison** (corrected §19.29.2: TR-7's set-digest equality refused legitimate rejoin Welcomes after a committer was added, E-72). The anchor does not stop a committer it names, even one removed since, from forking the real group (L8-C25, §19.29.2). **The joiner checks the bootstrap** too: when it names the generation of the namespace the Welcome arrived in (the invite's for a join or restore), `ns(G)` must equal that namespace; a newer generation is accepted only from an anchored Welcome (mutant PC-17).

### 5.5 Channel content: the MLS application plaintext `ChannelContent` v1

```
ChannelContent = version(1) = 0x01 || kind(1) || op_id(16) || author_minute(u32_be) || timer_code(1) || tlv*
tlv            = type(u16_be) || len(u16_be) || value
```

- `author_minute`: whole minutes since 1970-01-01T00:00Z on the author's trusted clock (FR-3.4, T13); there is no finer time anywhere in the content.
- Canonical form: TLVs strictly ascending by type, no duplicates, only the TLVs listed for the kind, lengths within bounds, no trailing bytes; otherwise `NON_CANONICAL` and the post is dropped and counted.
- The spec's binding of "channel ID, epoch, content type and client operation ID" (spec §7.3): the channel and epoch are bound by MLS (group id and key schedule), the content type by `kind`, the operation id by `op_id`, all inside the encrypted content. MLS `authenticated_data` is left in clear inside the PrivateMessage by RFC 9420 §6.3 and hidden from relays by the outer seal (§5.6); it is authenticated by MLS (signed in the framed content and bound by the AEAD) and readable by every member of the epoch. **Application messages** carry `ChannelOrder = version(1) = 1 ‖ sender_leaf(u32_be) ‖ seq(u32_be)` there (9 bytes, set through `MlsGroup::set_aad`, which the next `create_message` consumes and resets, `om:src/group/mls_group/mod.rs:326-331`, `application.rs:96,113`): the author's own ordering claim, which a receiver reads before decrypting (`PrivateMessageIn::aad`, `om:src/framing/private_message_in.rs:59`) and which nobody else can alter (§6.3.3, §19.18). **Commits** carry `GhostCommitAad` there (§19.2).

| kind | Name | TLVs | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x01 | POST | 0x0001 `text` (1 … 16 000 bytes UTF-8; with `media_ref`: the caption, optional, ≤ 1 024 bytes of strict UTF-8), 0x0002 `reply_to` (16, optional), 0x0003 `media_ref` (exactly 84 bytes: the fixed part of Phase 11's media descriptor, `faza11-media.md` §4.1 offsets 0 … 83, with `key_slot` zero outside Rust; **refused (`NON_CANONICAL`) until the channel-media slice, §5.10, Q38**) | replies are posts; a POST carries `text`, `media_ref` or both |
| 0x02 | REACTION | 0x0004 `target` (16), 0x0005 `grapheme` (0 … 32 bytes; empty withdraws) | one reaction per (author, target) |
| 0x03 | INVITE_ISSUED | 0x0010 `invite_id` (16), 0x0011 `invite_pk` (32), 0x0012 `expiry_day` (u32, days since 1970), 0x0013 `sponsor_sig` (64: the sponsor's pseudonym signature over `"ghost/v1/ch/invite" ‖ channel_id ‖ invite_id ‖ invite_pk ‖ u32(expiry_day)`, §19.2), 0x0014 `committers_digest` (32: the digest of §5.2 over the committers when the invite was issued; authenticated by the post's MLS signature; S0-15), 0x0015 `pw_version` (u16 ≥ 1: the context's `pw_version`, present exactly when the sponsor issued in a password channel, absent otherwise, the one encoding of "open"; §19.28.3, corrected §19.29.3) | the sponsor's record (§6.2) |
| 0x04 | LEAVE | none | asks a committer to remove the author (§6.6) |
| 0x05 | REVEAL | 0x0020 `hpke_enc` (32), 0x0021 `ciphertext` (≤ 512) | §6.9 |
| 0x06 | FLAG | 0x0030 `target` (32, a pseudonym key), 0x0031 `reason` (u8) | **reserved** (ADR-13, P1); refused in Phase 10 (Q15) |
| 0x07 | PIN | — | reserved (FR-3.7, P2) |

- **Timer.** A receiver deletes a post after `min(its timer_code, the channel timer of the post's epoch)`, so a member cannot lengthen a post's life beyond the policy; the author encrypts with the current policy timer (encrypt-at-session, §6.3.1).
- **Size.** MLS adds about 146 bytes per application message with 0x0003 (measured, `mlsscale` run: 0 → 145, 100 → 246, 3 000 → 3 146 bytes), plus the 9 bytes of `ChannelOrder`. **Measured with `ChannelOrder` (V10-4):** the longest text post (16 000 bytes of text, 16 027 bytes of content) is a 16 198-byte MLS message, 118 bytes under C(16384) = 16 316 (98 bytes with `reply_to`); posts never need 64 KiB, and G-M7 pins the size (a bump that eats the margin fails). A media POST (84-byte `media_ref`, caption 1 024 bytes) is 1 310 bytes and so always takes the class floor, like a short text (Phase 11's R-D1 for DMs, §5.10).

### 5.6 The outer seal (normative; the DM shape of F9 §6.2 with channel labels)

```
blob(B) = salt(16) || hint(16) || ChaCha20-Poly1305(k, nonce = 0^12, AD, P)       |P| = B − 48,  B ∈ {4096, 16384, 65536}
hint    = HMAC-SHA256(R, "ghost/v1/ch-hint" || mode(1) || salt)[0..16]
k       = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = K, salt = salt, info = "ghost/v1/ch-seal" || ns(32), L = 32)
AD      = "ghost/v1/ch-seal" || 0x01 || ns(32) || u32_be(B) || mode(1)                 (not transmitted)
P       = mode(1) || body_len(u24_be) || order(16) || body || zeros                    capacity C(B) = B − 68
order   = epoch(u64_be) || sender_leaf(u32_be) || seq(u32_be)      (mode 0x11; zeros in the other modes; for an application
          message it must equal the epoch of the PrivateMessage header and the ChannelOrder of its MLS AAD, §19.18; for a commit
          sender_leaf and seq are zero)
```

| mode | Key K (recognition R) | Body |
|---|---|---|
| 0x11 EPOCH | `K_ep(e)` (`R_ep(e)`) | an `MlsMessageOut` (PrivateMessage: application message or commit) |
| 0x12 JOIN | `JK_g` (`R_join(g)`) | `JoinRequest` (below) |
| 0x13 WELCOME | `JK_g` (`R_join(g)`) | `WelcomeBlob` (below) |

```
JoinRequest = version(1) = 1 || type(1) ∈ {0x01 join, 0x02 rejoin, 0x03 restore}                  (§19.3)
            || kp_len(u16_be) || key_package (an MlsMessage carrying the canonical KeyPackage of §6.2.4 (B);
                                               kp_len = 395 for suite 0x0003, any other length refused, S0-4)
            || auth
auth        = invite_id(16) || invite_sig(64)                                          (join, restore)
            | day(u32_be) || last_epoch(u64_be) || last_hash(32) || rejoin_sig(64)       (rejoin)
invite_sig  = Ed25519(invite key, "ghost/v1/ch/join" || channel_id || invite_id || SHA-256(key_package))
rejoin_sig  = Ed25519(pseudonym key, "ghost/v1/ch/rejoin" || channel_id || u32_be(generation) || u32_be(day)
                      || u64_be(last_epoch) || last_hash || SHA-256(key_package))
              // generation: of the namespace the request is sealed into; last_epoch, last_hash: the requester's
              // last final epoch and its winner hash; day: the requester's trusted day
WelcomeBlob = version(1) = 1 || kp_ref_len(u8) || kp_ref (the joiner's KeyPackageRef)
            || welcome_len(u24_be) || welcome (an MlsMessage carrying a Welcome, TLS)
              // the bootstrap travels only in the Welcome's GroupInfo (§5.4; V10-8, S0-9: round 10c's
              // boot_flag and K_boot(e) fallback are withdrawn)
```

- **CS-S1 (T9).** `|blob| = B` exactly; every length is inside P; the bytes are uniform (salt random, hint a PRF output, key fresh per salt). **Channel blobs and DM blobs have the same shape per blob**; a relay cannot tell them apart by bytes. It can tell **namespaces** apart (declared, §19.22): a channel namespace has as many listeners as fetching members (LC-2), and a large-class namespace holds no blob below 16 KiB (LC-1).
- **CS-S2 (canonical).** A known mode for the key class that matched, lengths within P, all padding zero; otherwise consumed, dropped and counted.
- **CS-S3 (binding).** `k` and `AD` bind the namespace and the bucket: a copy into another namespace or a truncation does not open (the relay store can hold one ciphertext under several namespaces, `relay/crates/storage/src/lib.rs:314-420`).
- **CS-S4 (nonce).** `k` is fresh per salt, so the zero nonce never repeats under one key.
- **CS-S5 (one attempt).** The receiver computes the hints of its candidate (R, mode) pairs and opens with the one that matches; a match whose AEAD fails is a forgery, dropped and counted. Candidates per channel: `R_ep(e)` with mode 0x11 for the current epoch and every retained epoch (≤ 17, §8.3; S0-6), and `R_join(g)` with modes 0x12 and 0x13 for each listened generation (≤ 3). No trial decryption across keys (the partitioning-oracle argument of F9 R-S5).
- **CS-S6 (recognition vs opening).** Kotlin stores and matches recognition keys only; `K_ep`, `JK_g` and `G_g` are stored wrapped under the channel state key and handed to Rust with the blob.
- **CS-S7 (order is a hint; corrected §19.18).** `order` is authenticated by the seal, but every holder of `K_ep(e)` can seal, so the seal says nothing about who chose it: a member could re-seal another member's MLS message under a false `order`. The authoritative ordering key is therefore the `ChannelOrder` in the message's MLS AAD, which only its author can set. `open` returns both; a blob whose `order` differs from its header epoch or its `ChannelOrder` is dropped and counted before any decryption, and after decryption a `ChannelOrder.sender_leaf` different from the MLS sender (`ProcessedMessage::sender`) drops the message. OpenMLS does not expose the ratchet generation of a received message (`ProcessedMessage` carries group id, epoch, sender, AAD, content and credential, `om:src/framing/validation.rs:500-530`), so `seq` is the author's authenticated claim, not checked against MLS; a lying `seq` misorders only its author's own messages. `order` serves peek ordering only (§6.3.3); it is visible only to holders of `K_ep(e)`, who learn the sender anyway on decryption.
- **Welcomes are "encrypted individually" (spec §7.3):** each joiner gets its own sealed copy (its own salt) of the commit's Welcome, whose group secrets are HPKE-encrypted to that joiner's KeyPackage by MLS.

### 5.7 Size classes and buckets (normative)

`bucket(len) = min{ B ∈ {4096, 16384, 65536} : B ≥ floor(class) ∧ C(B) ≥ len }`; a body larger than C(65536) = 65 468 bytes is refused (`NO_FIT`), which bounds every OpenMLS object GHOST accepts to one blob. That bound does **not** bound SRLabs S0-1 (V10-4: leaves inflated within it made every later Welcome of a 100-member channel too large for any blob); canonical KeyPackages and leaves do (§6.2.4, CO-2, S0-5).

**Measured sizes (normative evidence; V10-4, `ghost/client-core/mls-spike/core/tests/v10_04_sizes.rs`, 2026-09-25/26; S0-4).** Suite 0x0003, OpenMLS 0.9.0, GHOST's extensions and evidence (`GhostLeaf` with `refresh_epoch` in every update-path leaf, `GhostCommitAad` with `AdmitEvidence` or `SwapEvidence`, proposals by value), every commit with a forced path, the canonical KeyPackage (395 B); bytes of the `MlsMessageOut` the seal carries. *Blank* = only the committer's direct path set, so its update path carries all n − 1 path-secret ciphertexts, the maximum in any shape (random histories with blank parents and unmerged leaves stayed below it); *populated* = every parent set; *width* = the tree's leaf count, which the member count does not fix: a small channel reaches 64 or 128 leaves after a class change from large (the tree keeps its width while its rightmost leaf is occupied), and 128 is the widest a class-capped channel can be; each extra level adds one update-path node, **+35 B**.

| Commit (margin to the floor) | small, 32 members, blank: 32 leaves / 64 / **128** | small, populated (32 leaves) | large, 100 members (128 leaves): blank / populated |
|---|---|---|---|
| PCS commit (empty, with path) | 3 185 / 3 220 / 3 255 | 1 053 | 8 831 / 1 287 |
| Remove 1 | 3 110 / 3 145 / 3 180 | 1 306 | 8 756 / 1 458 |
| swap with rejoin evidence | 3 653 / 3 688 / 3 723 | 1 849 | 9 299 / 2 001 |
| **swap with restore evidence** | 3 786 / 3 821 / **3 856 (margin 172)** | 1 982 | **9 432 (margin 6 884)** / 2 134 |
| Add bringing the channel to its cap (31 → 32; 99 → 100) | 3 745 / 3 780 / 3 815 (margin 213) | — | 9 391 / — |
| context change: 8 committers, 3 funders, 48-byte name | 3 653 / 3 688 / 3 723 | 1 521 | 9 299 / 1 755 |
| Remove 1 + that context change (one unit under the rule below) | 3 578 / 3 613 / 3 648 (margin 380) | 1 774 | — |
| composite: context change + Remove + restore swap | 4 178 / 4 213 / 4 248 (**split** by the rule) | 2 374 | 9 824 / 2 526 |
| rank-255 empty self-update | 1 955 / 2 072 / 1 990 | 1 053 | 3 560 / 936 |

| Other object | Size | Bucket |
|---|---|---|
| canonical KeyPackage (MlsMessage) | 395 B, on every day (golden) | 4 KiB (join request) |
| Welcome for one joiner, tree included | small: 8 818 (32 leaves) … 9 111 (128 leaves) blank, 10 824–10 896 populated; large: 25 894–26 523 blank, 33 612–33 684 populated | 16 KiB small, 64 KiB large |
| application message | +146 B over the content, +9 B `ChannelOrder`; a 16 000-byte text post is 16 198 B (margin 118 to C(16384); 98 with `reply_to`) | by length |
| **with round 10c's 1 024-byte KeyPackage bound (Q3's option (b))** | Add 31 → 32: 4 374 (32 leaves) / 4 444 (128 leaves), **above C(4096)**; the largest small class in which that Add fits: **27** members (32-leaf tree), **26** (128-leaf tree, 25 → 26: 3 952 B); at the large cap 10 020 B (fits) | — |
| **without canonical leaves (why S0-5 is needed)** | one joiner's Welcome over 30 KeyPackage leaves inflated to 1 024 B: 27 696 B at 32 members (64 KiB) and over 98 such leaves 87 536 B at 100 (**NO_FIT**); four rank-255 members each adding ≈ 12.8 KB to their own leaf in a self-update within C(16384) make the next Welcome 77 625 B (**NO_FIT**: nobody can join or rejoin) | — |

- **Rule (normative; revised §19.18 and S0-16):** a committer builds its commit, and while the commit's body exceeds `C(floor)` it defers, in this order: Add proposals from the end of the pass's list; then swaps; then the context change, **except** the part that drops from `committers` and `funders` the keys of pseudonyms the commit still removes — a Remove of P and the drop of P's key are **one unit** (CO-2 and §5.3 require the key to leave the lists in the commit that removes P's leaf, so deferring the drop alone would leave an invalid commit); then Removes beyond the first, each with the drop of its key. Each deferred item waits for the next pass; a removal hold (CO-13) applies only to what the commit carries. Round 10c deferred the context change before swaps, which could strand exactly that unit (V10-4 measured the unit at 3 578–3 648 B, within the floor). A commit with **one** proposal (or none, with a path) that still exceeds the floor is a class violation: **V10-4 rules it out at both caps, in every width, with canonical KeyPackages** (the table: the tightest is the restore swap at 32 members in a 128-leaf tree, 172 B under the floor); the composite of a context change, a Remove and a swap is 4 178–4 248 B at 32 members and is split by this rule. A commit received above the floor is invalid (CO-2). G-M7 pins the golden sizes of this table; a bump that eats a margin fails the gate. Q3 is re-asked with both options (§17).
- Consequence (P-C3): in a generation namespace every commit and every short post share the floor bucket, and only Welcomes and long posts use a larger one.
- **The password field (§19.28, §19.29.5):** with `pw_version` in the context (§5.3, 448 bytes), every row above that carries a context change, and every Welcome, grows by exactly 2 bytes (one `uint16` inside an opaque vector whose varint length prefix stays 2 bytes); the spike's encoder predates the field, so these rows stay as V10-4 measured them and G-M7's goldens are set in S2 with the real crate. The tightest margin (the restore swap, 172 B) carries no context change and is unchanged; the context-change unit keeps 378 B of margin.

### 5.8 TTL per blob (normative; ADR-27 point 11, ADR-32, deviation X-7)

| Channel timer | off | 5 min | 1 h | 1 d | 7 d (default) | 30 d |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Post TTL (mode 0x11 application) | 2 592 000 | 3 600 | 3 600 | 86 400 | 604 800 | 2 592 000 |
| Handshake TTL (commits, JOIN, WELCOME, `INVITE_ISSUED`, `LEAVE`) | 2 592 000 | **604 800** | **604 800** | **604 800** | 604 800 | 2 592 000 |
| Op deadline | author + 7 d | author + 1 h | author + 1 h | author + 1 d | author + 7 d | author + 7 d |

- Posts follow ADR-27 point 11 exactly. The op deadline is ADR-32 point 3 (`author + min(bucket, 7 d)`); a handshake op's deadline is `author + 7 d`.
- **X-7.** In channels with timers ≤ 1 d, handshake blobs live 7 days so that a member offline longer than the timer can still catch up on membership (otherwise it could never rejoin in-band either); relays can tell those blobs from posts by their TTL there (LC-1). In channels with timers 7 d, 30 d or off, every blob uses one TTL (P-C3). The alternatives (whole channel at 7 d; handshake at the post TTL) are Q12's.
- A timer change moves the post TTL at the change: the first post under the new timer marks it at the relays (as DMs, F9 §11.2 "Expirare / schimbare timer"); between 7 d, 30 d and off this also moves the handshake TTL, so "one TTL per generation namespace" holds per **timer version** (JC-4 keys by it, §19.22). A join request uses the handshake TTL frozen in its invite (§5.9, `handshake_ttl_code`): under an invite issued before a timer change it carries the old TTL, which marks it at the relays as a join under an older invite (declared, LC-1).
- **Administrative posts** `INVITE_ISSUED` and `LEAVE` are application messages that committers need for days (an admission may come up to 7 days after the invite; a leave waits for a pass). They use the **handshake** TTL, not the post TTL (in channels with timer 7 d, 30 d or off the two are equal; in channels with timers ≤ 1 d this extends X-7 to them, so relays can tell them from posts there, L8-C14). Round 10b gave them the post TTL, so in a 5-minute or 1-hour channel an invite or a leave request stayed on relays one hour and a committer that did not list within that hour never admitted the invitee or removed the leaver (§19.21).

### 5.9 Channel invite v1 fields (`ghost://channel/`; ADR-05's link and QR rules)

**Superseded as a link format by `ChannelInviteV2` (§19.28.3, corrected §19.29.3).** Every invite, open or password-sealed, is a V2 link of **335 bytes** (552 characters with the prefix); the V1 fields below from `suite` to `name` are its sealed body, zero-padded to 301 bytes, and V1 links are never issued. The layout, the field rules and the sizes below describe that body.

```
ChannelInviteV1 = version(1) = 0x01 || suite(u16_be) = 0x0003 || size_class(1) || handshake_ttl_code(1)
                || channel_id(32) || generation(u32_be) || ns(32) || read_base(32) || deposit_base(32) || join_key(32)
                || invite_id(16) || invite_seed(32) || committers_digest(32) || expiry_day(u32_be)
                || name_len(1) || name(0..48)                                                                 // 254..302 bytes
link = "ghost://channel/" || base32(ChannelInviteV1), lowercase, unpadded       // 16 + 407..484 characters
```

- No web host, no identity key in clear, no payment address (T16's rules for invitations applied to channel invites); an invite is single-use (§6.2.4) and expires at `expiry_day ≤ issue day + 7`.
- **`committers_digest` (S0-15)** is the digest of §5.2 over the channel's `committers` when the sponsor issued the invite; the joiner accepts only a Welcome whose context has those committers and whose GroupInfo one of them signed (§5.4, §6.2.5). Consequence: a commit that changes the **set** of `committers` (a new moderator, a resignation, a committer's removal; since §19.27 TR-6 a transfer of rank 0, a reorder, does not) voids every unconsumed invite of the channel at its settle (CO-5), the sponsor's client tells its user so (§19.29.2), and the sponsor issues new ones; an invite is admitted only by a commit that leaves `committers` unchanged (CO-2).
- The invite is a **bearer secret for one generation**: whoever holds it first **joins the channel** (admission is automatic; single use means only that the first presenter wins) and every holder can list and store in `ns_g` and open join traffic until the generation rotates, which §19.7 bounds by rotating after an unconsumed invite expires (L8-C4). QR in person is the recommended path; a link sent inside a GHOST DM is end-to-end encrypted (ADR-05's warning about other messengers applies).
- **The channel name is not carried by default** (Q29, §19.7): `name_len = 0` (the body is then 253 bytes; round 10c's V1 link was 222 bytes, 254 with S0-15's digest) and a leaked link does not name the channel; the joiner learns the name from `GhostChannelContext` after admission. Since V2 pads the body to 301 bytes, a carried name no longer changes a link's size; if the owner chooses to carry it, it is readable by any holder of an open link (whose seal key comes from the link's own salt) and of a password link together with its password, and is declared (L8-C4).

### 5.10 Channel media: the Phase 11 contract (reserved; §19.24, Q38)

Spec FR-3.4 requires posts with "media up to 50 MiB" (P0), and the Phase 11 design (branch `p11-design`, `docs/design/faza11-media.md` at `116cfcd`, §4.3, §5.6, ADR-45) states what it requires of Phase 10. What Phase 10 fixes **now**, so that no format changes later:

1. **The pointer.** POST TLV 0x0003 `media_ref` is exactly the 84 fixed bytes of Phase 11's descriptor (`d_version`, `kind`, `flags`, `class_code`, `object_id`, `key_slot`, `manifest_hash`, faza11 §4.1 offsets 0 … 83); the POST's `text` is the caption (≤ 1 024 bytes of strict UTF-8, Phase 11's R-D3) and is optional when `media_ref` is present (§5.5). The descriptor's parsing rules (R-D3) apply unchanged.
2. **The size.** A media POST fits the class floor (§5.5 "Size"), so P-C3 is unchanged: the pointer post is indistinguishable from a short text. The object's fragments are 64 KiB blobs and never share a namespace with the pointer's generation namespace unless Q38's slice decides so (below).
3. **The key slot** (Phase 11 §4.2 applied to MLS). `MlsNative.encrypt` gains an optional `mediaKeyEnv` and the DM context handle that holds its wrap key: for a POST with `media_ref`, Rust checks that `key_slot` is zero, unwraps `key_env` under `(out, dest_id, object_id, manifest_hash)` with the last two read from the same content, writes `K_f` into the slot, MLS-encrypts and zeroises; `receive` of such a POST wraps `K_f` into a new `key_env` bound to `(in, dest_id, object_id, manifest_hash)`, zeroes the slot and returns the content and `key_env`. The file key never reaches Kotlin. `dest_id` of a channel is `SHA-256("ghost/v1/ch/media-dest" ‖ channel_id)[0..16]` (local only: it appears in no relay-visible value). These two arguments are **not** in P0's closed list (G-M6); they enter it with the slice.
4. **TTL and deadline**: the channel's post TTL for every blob of an object (Phase 11's R-MT1 and R-MT4 with the channel timer version fixed at commit), and the post deadline of §5.8.
5. **What a removed member keeps**: the `key_env` of every object it received before its removal (declared with L8-C18's residue; no object sent after its removal reaches it, P-C4).

**Left to the channel-media slice (Q38), with the constraint each choice meets.** (a) Where fragments live: a per-generation media namespace derived from `G_g` (label `"ghost/v1/ch/media-ns"`, with its own per-slot READ and DEPOSIT secrets, funded like `ns_g` and rotated with it) keeps Phase 11's media lane and fairness rules, which key on `Consumer.MEDIA` namespaces, but adds 3 read pairs per channel (6 while draining), so the 9-channel limit of Q4 would fall to about 5 under `maxReadPairs = 64`; fragments in `ns_g` add no pair but mix 64 KiB fragment bursts and `FETCHED_CAP = 256` pressure with posts. (b) The funders' quota: 256 MiB per registering capability per relay per week (F8:417) is about five 50 MiB videos per channel per week. (c) Relay egress of fan-out (Phase 11's F-M5: a 50 MiB post read by 100 members is about 5 GB). (d) The T25 channel-media cases and FR-3.4's "boundary-size, invalid-media" acceptance tests.

---

## 6. Protocol and state machines (normative)

### 6.1 Channel creation (transaction `CH-CREATE`)

1. The creator's client draws `channel_id`, obtains `ps` from `:identity`, and calls `MlsNative.groupCreate` with the policy (`committers = [P]`, `funders = [P]`, timer 7 d unless chosen, class small, `members_may_invite = 1`, `idle_evict_days = 63`, name).
2. The call returns the change-set, `K_ep(0)`, `G_0` and the derived generation values; in one transaction Kotlin writes `ch_channel`, `ch_generation(0)`, `ch_epoch(0)`, the `mls_kv` rows, registers `ns_0` (`Namespaces.register(ns_0, CHANNEL, set(ns_0, w), listen = true, LOCAL)`), installs READ and DEPOSIT credentials for every relay slot of the set, marks `ns_0` funded (`Funding.set`, §7.5) and requests READ and DEPOSIT registrations for `{c, c + 1}` of the capability the planner will redeem (the F9 §4.8 `REGROWS` rule).
3. No traffic follows from the creation except the funder's redemptions and registrations (Q31's rule of F9: pending registrations count as write work, so the planner redeems as soon as an eligible token exists).

### 6.2 Invitation and admission

#### 6.2.1 Sponsor (`CH-INVITE`, a user action)

A member may invite if `members_may_invite = 1` or it is a committer (with Q46 (b), only a committer in a password channel, §5.3). The client draws `invite_id` and `invite_seed`, computes `committers_digest` over its current context's `committers` (§5.2, S0-15), builds the invite (`ChannelInviteV2`, §19.28.3: in a password channel sealed under the password the sponsor types, which nothing can check, §19.29.3), signs `sponsor_sig` with its pseudonym key and, in the same transaction, encrypts and enqueues an `INVITE_ISSUED(invite_id, invite_pk, expiry_day, committers_digest, sponsor_sig[, pw_version])` post; the invite link is returned to the UI. An open invite's link stays in `ch_invite_own` (sponsor side) only until `expiry_day`, for the UI; a password invite's link is shown once and not kept (Q50 (a), §19.29.3); every member, the sponsor included, records the `INVITE_ISSUED` in `ch_invite_issued`. When a settle voids one of its invites (a change of the committer set, CO-5, or of the password, §19.29.3), the sponsor's client tells its user, so the invitee can be told not to act on the old link (§19.29.2).

#### 6.2.2 Invitee (`CH-ACCEPT`, a user action)

1. Parse strictly (the V2 header first: a `kdf_code ≥ 1` link asks for the password, and a wrong password or a tampered link is `WRONG_PASSWORD` with nothing written, §19.28.4 step 3); refuse an unknown suite (only 0x0003 in P0), an expired invite, a `channel_id` the client is already a member of (unless in `RESYNC_NEEDED`, `REINVITE_NEEDED` or `REPLACED`, where the invite serves a restore, §6.7, §6.12; a member captured in a forger's fork is `active` and cannot tell, so it returns only by leaving that copy, Q49, §19.29.2). The client keeps no list of channels it left (T33), so it cannot tell a **clone** — a new group that reuses a known `channel_id`, created for example by a member removed from the original — from a re-invitation: in either it appears with the pseudonym ADR-04 derives from that id (D12). The accept screen says so (Phase 13 copy: "you appear under the same pseudonym as in any earlier channel with this identifier"); declared, L8-C22 (§19.16).
2. Derive `ps` for `channel_id`; `MlsNative.keyPackage` creates the canonical KeyPackage of §6.2.4 (B) (the constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]` of Q42 (a), §19.29.1; `GhostLeaf`, `GhostKpBind(channel_id, purpose, day)`, the canonical capabilities; 395 bytes) and returns its global change-set (the init and encryption private keys, wrapped). The purpose is **restore** (3) when the client is in `RESYNC_NEEDED` or `REINVITE_NEEDED` for this channel or has no state for a channel whose pseudonym it may still hold (a restored device, §6.12), otherwise **join** (1). (A restore-purpose KeyPackage is admitted only as a swap of a current leaf, CO-2 (A), so a pseudonym that never was a member of the real channel — a captured joiner — is refused; how such a member returns is Q49, §19.29.2.)
3. In one transaction: `ch_channel(state = joining)`, `ch_join(invite_id, generation, expiry_day, kp_ref)`, register `ns_g` (listen, LOCAL) with READ and DEPOSIT credentials, and enqueue the `JoinRequest` sealed in mode 0x12 with the invite's handshake TTL and the floor of its class.
4. The invitee lists `ns_g` like any member; it opens only mode 0x12 and 0x13 blobs (it holds `R_join(g)` and no epoch key). At `expiry_day + 1` without a Welcome it deletes the join state and the KeyPackage and stops listening (`JOIN_EXPIRED`).

#### 6.2.3 Committer (`CH-PASS`, §6.4)

At a pass, for each pending join request of the current or a draining generation, in arrival order: check (§6.2.4); if valid, propose the Add by value (a restore or rejoin: a swap of the pseudonym's current leaf) and add its evidence to the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (§19.2); build the commit; seal one WELCOME blob per joiner in the namespace the request came from and **keep them wrapped in the committer's own candidate row**; enqueue only the commit in the pass transaction. **The WELCOME blobs are enqueued in `CH-SETTLE`, after `merge_pending_commit` of the committer's own winning commit**, and the invites are marked consumed there (§19.2; RFC 9750 §5.2.3). A committer whose commit loses deletes the Welcomes and keeps the requests pending for its next pass (their KeyPackages are intact: no joiner processed a Welcome).

#### 6.2.4 Admission checks (normative; close SRLabs S0-1)

**The canonical GHOST KeyPackage (suite 0x0003; S0-4, S0-12, §19.29.1; one definition, used by `keyPackage`, the admission policy, CO-2 (A) and the `JoinRequest` codec).** An `MlsMessage` carrying a KeyPackage with: protocol version mls10; cipher suite 0x0003; X25519 `init_key` and leaf `encryption_key`; leaf `signature_key` = the pseudonym key and a `BasicCredential` whose identity is that key; **leaf capabilities exactly** versions [mls10], cipher suites [0x0003], extensions [0xF0A1, 0xF0A2, 0xF0A4], proposals [] (the encoded list is empty: RFC 9420's default proposal types need no listing), credentials [Basic], in that order, **no GREASE values**; leaf node source `key_package` with the **constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]`** (Q42 (a), §19.29.1; round 10c's day-aligned `[day_start(d) − 4 d, day_start(d) + 80 d]` is Q42's option (b)); **leaf extensions exactly** [`GhostLeaf` with a valid `box_pk` and `refresh_epoch = 0`]; **KeyPackage extensions exactly** [`GhostKpBind(channel_id, purpose, d)`], whose day d is the KeyPackage's only date. Its encoding is **395 bytes** (measured on every day, V10-4): the size follows from RFC 9420's encoding of these values, and G-M7 checks at every OpenMLS pin that `keyPackage` still produces exactly it. A KeyPackage of any other form or size is refused. (Round 10c's "exactly one leaf extension, total size ≤ 1 024 bytes" let a single Add exceed C(4096) from 27 members and let leaves inflated within the bound make a 100-member tree unwelcomable, V10-4; Q3 offers keeping that bound with a smaller small class instead.)

The checks split in two (§19.2). **(A) Evidence, checked by every member as part of CO-2** — a function of the commit, the pre-commit group state and the commit's `GhostCommitAad` only: for every Add, one evidence entry names its `kp_ref`; for a join, `sponsor` is the signature key of a current leaf that this commit does not remove and may invite (`members_may_invite` or a committer; with Q46 (b), in a password channel only a committer, §5.3), `sponsor_sig` verifies under it, `invite_sig` verifies under `invite_pk` over the KeyPackage, no two Adds of the commit share an `invite_id`, and the pseudonym is not a current member; for a swap (Remove of leaf L and Add of the same pseudonym), a restore carries join evidence as above and a rejoin carries `(day, last_epoch, last_hash, rejoin_sig)` with `rejoin_sig` valid under L's signature key; the KeyPackage is **the canonical KeyPackage** above (its form, and its day d within `[commit_day − 7, commit_day + 3]`, `commit_day` being the day of the commit's `GhostCommitAad.commit_minute`: the admission window of (B) seen from the commit, the same for every member, so no member's clock enters CO-2; S0-12, §19.29.1), with `GhostKpBind` for this channel and the purpose matching the evidence kind. **(B) Admission policy, applied by the admitting committer only** (other members do not repeat it; it depends on the committer's history and clock): the committer **holds the `INVITE_ISSUED`** (it was a member when the invite was issued, so it has processed every later Add of its branch and knows every consumption); the invite is unexpired on the committer's trusted day and not consumed on its branch (`ch_invite_issued.consumed`, set at settle from the AAD of every merged Add); the sponsor is still a member (a merged commit that removes P voids P's unconsumed invites, §19.7); the invite's `committers_digest` equals the digest of the committer's pre-commit `committers` (S0-15; a changed list voids the invite, CO-5); the KeyPackage is canonical, `GhostKpBind.day` within `[day − 7, day + 3]` of the committer's trusted day, and its `kp_ref` and init key were never seen before in this channel (`ch_kp_seen`, kept until `GhostKpBind.day + 11`: the last day the window admits it, d + 7, plus σ and one day; §19.29.1 — "until the KeyPackage's `not_after`" would be forever under the constant lifetime); **in a password channel** (flag bit 2 set at admission) `INVITE_ISSUED` carries TLV 0x0015 equal to the context's `pw_version`, and in an open channel no password check applies (§19.28.4, corrected §19.29.3); the member count after the commit stays within the class cap. A join-type request whose pseudonym **is** a current member is not refused: with purpose restore it becomes an invite-authorized swap (§6.12). A rejoin request is admitted only under the freshness rules of §6.7. A second join request for the same invite is ignored. A committer admits at most the number of Adds that keeps the commit within the floor (§5.7). Because every KeyPackage an Add or a swap carries and every leaf a commit installs is canonical (CO-2, S0-5), no oversized or unknown extension — the "50 MiB extension" of SRLabs S0-1 — enters a GHOST channel's tree through an Add or an update path; a malicious **member** can still send large valid posts up to 64 KiB per blob (declared, L8-C11).

#### 6.2.5 Joiner (`CH-JOIN`, on a WELCOME blob)

The invitee (or rejoiner) opens a WELCOME blob with `JK_g`, matches `kp_ref`, runs `MlsNative.welcomeJoin`: after `ProcessedWelcome::new_from_welcome` and **before** `JoinBuilder::build()` it checks **the committers anchor** on the unverified GroupInfo (S0-15, §19.27 TR-7, corrected §19.29.2: for a join or restore, the set digest of the context's `committers` equals the invite's `committers_digest` and the GroupInfo signature verifies under one of them; for a rejoin, the signature verifies under one of the committers of the member's last final epoch, with no digest comparison, E-72); only then does it run OpenMLS's `JoinBuilder` with `skip_lifetime_validation()` (`om:src/group/mls_group/creation.rs:1313`) and GHOST's structural check that every KeyPackage-source leaf carries the constant lifetime (§19.29.1); it verifies `GhostChannelContext` with the validity rules of §5.3 (every committer and funder a current leaf, among them) and the bootstrap (namespace-checked, §5.4), derives `K_ep(e)` and the generation values, and in one transaction writes the group rows, `ch_channel(state = active, joined_epoch = e)`, the generation rows, and deletes the join state. If the bootstrap names a newer generation than the invite's, the namespace change is **not** made here: `CH-SWITCH` makes it at the joiner's next session start (§19.8; `CH-JOIN` calls no `Namespaces`, `Outbox`, `Registrations`, `Funding` or `Capabilities` method, G-M5). No store results (T40): the joiner's first store is its user's first post or its scheduled self-update; its new listening is LC-5. **A Welcome that fails the anchor** is `INVALID_WELCOME`: nothing is written, so the KeyPackage and its private keys stay and the joiner keeps waiting for the real Welcome (V10-8 measured the alternative: a forged Welcome that is applied consumes the KeyPackage, and the real one then fails with `NoMatchingKeyPackage`); the blob is consumed and counted. **What the anchor does not stop (§19.29.2):** a committer it names — even one removed since, which keeps its last state — forks the real group and Welcomes the joiner into the fork; that Welcome passes the anchor and §5.3's validity (E-71), and the forger can keep the joiner live, so the joiner cannot detect it in-band (L8-C25, Q49). **Rejoin and restore Welcomes** (the client still holds rows of the channel's old group): Kotlin passes `welcomeJoin` only the global rows of the KeyPackage, none of the old group-scoped rows, so OpenMLS's `GroupAlreadyExists` check (`MlsGroup::load` finds nothing, `creation.rs:305-312`) does not fire and `JoinBuilder::replace_old_group()` (`creation.rs:1304`) is not needed; the same transaction deletes the old `mls_kv` rows and writes the change-set (§19.16).

### 6.3 Posting and receiving

#### 6.3.1 Compose and encrypt-at-session (`CH-POST`)

A post is encrypted **only when the channel is current**: the client has, in the present session, completed one listing pass over every relay of the current generation's set and processed every openable blob of its current epoch; it holds **no valid candidate for its current epoch that removes a pseudonym** (Remove, leave, eviction or swap: the **removal hold**, CO-13, §19.6; RFC 9750 §5.2.2 option 1 pauses sending for every candidate, GHOST pauses only where a departing member could read); and it is not `resync_needed` or switching generation (§19.8). (Round 10c's `clock_blocked` condition is withdrawn with Q42 (a), §19.29.1: under round 10d's rule any member could hold every member there, and with it every pass, CO-7, E-70.) **A committer's own pending commit holds its posts only when that commit removes a pseudonym** (revised §19.13; round 10b held every own pending commit, which cost owners and moderators at least 20 minutes of posting after every pass and bought nothing: a post in epoch e while a non-removing commit is pending is a late post of e for members that settled, decrypted from the retained epoch). OpenMLS's `create_message` checks only `is_active()` and the proposal store (`om:src/group/mls_group/application.rs:89-94`) and does **not** call `is_operational` (`mod.rs:1115-1121`), so it would encrypt in epoch e even while a removing commit is pending (E-5 corrected, §19.6); `MlsNative.encrypt` therefore refuses with `PENDING_COMMIT` when the own pending commit removes a pseudonym, as a native backstop of CO-13. Otherwise the post waits as plaintext in `ch_pending_post` (under the content key, the F9 §13.8 pattern) and is encrypted and enqueued in the first transaction where the channel is current. Consequences: posts are almost always encrypted in the epoch their receivers hold, so late posts come only from store delays, not from offline composition; the store minute of a post composed offline depends on the session's catch-up time (declared in LC-7). A pending post that reaches its op deadline without being encrypted fails (`FAILED`, the user may retry).

The post's op: namespace `ns_g` of the current generation, bucket by §5.7, TTL by §5.8, deadline `author + min(bucket, 7 d)`, relays `set(ns_g, week(now))`, the Phase 7 send delay (`SendDelay.OFF` refused for `Consumer.CHANNEL`, as for DMs).

#### 6.3.2 Receive (`CH-RECV`)

The messaging actor drains `Consumer.CHANNEL` like `Consumer.DM` (the listener and the consumer tail are the existing single slots, wired to the one actor, D16). Per channel it `peek`s the fetched rows of its namespaces and classifies each blob by the hint that matches (CS-S5):

| Opened as | Action |
|---|---|
| no match | if some candidate could still appear (a future epoch of this channel, or a Welcome for a pending join): `Inbox.defer(60 s … 6 h, backing off)`, re-offered at once by `Inbox.reofferDeferred` after every settle and join (§7.5, §19.1); counted as *unopenable foreign* for CO-11 only in the **current** generation's namespace and unless it is the client's own (§19.15); at the blob's relay expiry: consume and drop (`unopenable`, counted) |
| 0x11, epoch = current | application message: `order` checked against the header and `ChannelOrder` (CS-S7), decrypt in peek order (§6.3.3) in batches of up to 32 messages of the epoch, one `receive` call and one transaction per batch (S0-17), apply content, mark consumed; commit: validation run (§6.4 CO-2), record as candidate with its store-hour anchor (CO-4), mark consumed; **proposal** (header content type): dropped before `process_message` and counted — GHOST never calls `store_pending_proposal` (`om:src/group/mls_group/processing.rs:398`), because one stored proposal would make OpenMLS refuse every post until the next commit (`application.rs:92-94`) (§19.16); a result OpenMLS reports as `OwnPrivateMessage` is never evidence of anything — its sender claim is protected only by the epoch's sender-data key, which every member holds (`om:src/framing/validation.rs:158-172`, `processing.rs:251-277`) — so a blob so reported whose hash is not one of the client's own ops is a forgery, dropped and counted (§19.13) |
| 0x11, epoch < current, retained | application message: decrypt with the past epoch (late post); commit (PrivateMessage header content type): **late candidate** — record a fork signal (hash, day), consume; never processed (OpenMLS rejects non-current-epoch handshake messages before decryption, `om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:53-75`, valn1307) (CO-6) |
| 0x11, epoch older than retention | impossible (no key); falls under "no match" |
| 0x12 | committers: record a pending join, restore or rejoin request; others: consume, ignore (they need nothing from it: the admitting commit carries its evidence, §19.2) |
| 0x13 | joiners: §6.2.5; others: consume, ignore |

#### 6.3.3 Peek ordering (the F9 §7.7 idea for MLS)

Within an epoch, OpenMLS decrypts a sender's application messages out of order within the sender ratchet's tolerance (16). Blobs are fetched in blob-hash order (Phase 7), so a burst of more than 16 posts from one sender could be decrypted out of range. GHOST therefore decrypts the openable application messages of an epoch sorted by `(epoch, sender_leaf, seq)` — the `ChannelOrder` its author put in the MLS AAD, which the seal's `order` must repeat (CS-S7, §19.18) — and holds a gap (a missing `seq` of a sender) for at most 12 minutes before decrypting past it (F9's measured hold). **Corrected (§19.18):** round 10b took the key from the seal alone, which any holder of `K_ep(e)` can re-seal with a false `order` — moving a victim's late post to the front would push its earlier posts past the tolerance, a loss of another member's posts. With the key in the MLS AAD, a re-sealed copy with a false `order` is dropped at `open`, and a lying `seq` only misorders the liar's own messages. A relay that withholds part of a burst past the hold can still push the withheld posts out of range (declared, §6.3.6).

#### 6.3.4 Dedup and replay (normative; the four layers)

1. Phase 7 `(namespace, hash)` tombstones (IN-2).
2. MLS: a replayed PrivateMessage fails decryption once its generation key was used (RFC 9420 §9.2 deletion schedule); a resurrected commit of a finalized epoch has the hash already merged and is ignored; a commit of an older epoch without a retained key does not open.
3. The seal binds namespace and bucket (CS-S3): a copy into another namespace, including after a namespace is removed and re-added or a relay re-added (F7:47-52), does not open.
4. `ch_seen(op_id)`: an `op_id` already displayed is dropped (a re-encryption after FAILED keeps its `op_id`), retained as `dm_seen` (F9 §7.6) with the channel's bucket.

#### 6.3.5 Bounds (CS-C8)

Past epochs ≤ 16 (S0-6) and ≤ the retention of §8.3; commit candidates ≤ 8 per epoch, at most one per sender (the lowest hash of that sender), the best 8 by (rank, hash), plus the own commit; fork signals ≤ 16 per channel (the oldest dropped), deleted with their epoch's key; pending join, restore and rejoin requests ≤ 64 per channel, each until its invite's expiry or 7 days after its `day`; `ch_kp_seen` ≤ 1 024 per channel, each until its `GhostKpBind.day + 11` (§6.2.4 (B), §19.29.1); pending posts ≤ 256 per channel; own box keys ≤ 18 per channel (the current leaf's, the pending commit's and one per retained epoch, §19.17); peek limit 128 per pass, decrypted in batches of ≤ 32 (S0-17); trial settles ≤ 8 per settle, each **one** `trialCount` call over ≤ 32 held blobs (CO-3, S0-17); `ch_seen` rows bounded by retention.

#### 6.3.6 Declared losses (CS-C5)

A post is lost for a member when: its blob expired at the relays before the member listed it (offline beyond the TTL); its epoch's keys were deleted before it arrived (a store delayed beyond §8.3's retention, or an epoch more than 16 epochs behind the member's current one, the cap of §3.5, S0-6); its sender was on another branch (§6.7); it is a pending post that reached its deadline; it was stored in a draining generation older than the newest draining one after a second rotation (§6.5); the member rejoined (posts of the epochs it missed on the dead branch or while `RESYNC_NEEDED` are not recovered); or **it fell out of the sender ratchet's range** (§19.18): after the 12-minute gap hold the member decrypts past a missing `seq`, and a post that arrives more than 16 generations behind its sender's newest decrypted post no longer decrypts — a relay can cause this by withholding part of a burst of more than 16 posts from one sender until the hold ends.

### 6.4 Commit arbitration (normative; ADR-42)

*(Rewritten by the review rounds: CO-2 … CO-7 and CO-10 revised, CO-11 … CO-14 added in round 10b; CO-1 … CO-4, CO-8, CO-10 … CO-12 revised in round 10c; the defects and the reasons are in §19.1, §19.2, §19.5, §19.6, §19.13 … §19.15.)*

- **CO-1 (who may commit).** A commit is a candidate only if its sender is a member. Rank = the sender's index in `committers` (0 … 7) or 255 otherwise, **always read from the group context before the commit** (a commit that changes `committers` is judged, and ranked against its competitors, by the ranks it found; §19.13). A rank-255 commit is valid only if it covers **no proposal** (an empty commit with a path: a self-update) and satisfies CO-14. External commits (sender type `new_member_commit`) are dropped before processing.
- **CO-2 (validity: a function of the commit, the pre-commit group state and the commit's `GhostCommitAad` only — no clock; revised §19.29.1; checked on a validation run whose change-set is discarded).** **KeyPackage lifetimes first (normative; Q42 (a)):** every KeyPackage an Add or swap carries has the constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]` (part of its canonical form, §6.2.4 (B)). OpenMLS checks a received Add's lifetime against the device clock before the update path, the proposal set and every GHOST check, so its refusal masks any other defect (V10-11); with the constant lifetime no clock ever refuses a canonical KeyPackage, so a `process_message` refusal with `KeyPackageVerifyError::LifetimeError` means a non-canonical KeyPackage and **the commit is invalid** (dropped and counted, never a candidate); on the accept branch the member reads each Add's `KeyPackage::life_time()` and any other lifetime is invalid too — **one verdict on both branches, the same for every member whatever its device clock** (measured, E-69, E-70). *Withdrawn (§19.29.1):* S0-12's trusted-day predicate with its lifetime-pending state and `clock_blocked`, and round 10d's refusal-branch rule (a `LifetimeError` candidate ranked lowest, blocking the settle only as the sole candidate). The refusal branch hides the candidate's rank (TR-1, E-61), so with a finite lifetime a member can only wait or choose without it: waiting let any member freeze a channel with one commit carrying an expired KeyPackage (every correct clock refuses it; as the sole candidate it blocked every member, and CO-7 then forbade every pass, the owner's Remove of the attacker included, E-70), and choosing forked honest members whose device clocks straddled an honest KeyPackage's bound (E-69). Finite lifetimes are Q42's option (b), for which no convergent rule is known. A monotonic `commit_minute` (each commit's minute ≥ the previous epoch's) was considered and is **not** adopted: a committer that dates one commit into the future would then push every later honest commit's minute forward and, through CO-2 (A)'s window, make every honest KeyPackage invalid until real time caught up — an admission freeze. Then: OpenMLS accepts the commit at the current epoch; its body fits `C(floor)`; its authenticated data is a canonical `GhostCommitAad` (§19.2) whose evidence satisfies §6.2.4 (A) for every Add and swap; every commit with a path carries an update-path leaf whose `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch` equals the commit's epoch (§19.5); **every leaf the commit installs is canonical** (S0-5): an update-path leaf has exactly the canonical capabilities of §6.2.4 (B) and exactly one extension, `GhostLeaf` (V10-4: four rank-255 self-updates each adding 12.8 KB to their own leaf, every one within C(16384), made the next 100-member Welcome 77 625 B, beyond any blob), and a KeyPackage an Add or a swap carries is the canonical KeyPackage; **a commit that carries an Add or a restore swap leaves `committers` unchanged** (S0-15: its Welcomes are anchored to that list); **every leaf the commit installs keeps its member's identity**: the update-path leaf has the committer's pre-commit signature key and a `BasicCredential` whose identity equals that key (RFC 9420 §5.3.1's successor rule, which OpenMLS leaves to the application, `public_group/validation.rs:890-895`), and a swap's new leaf has the removed leaf's signature key (§19.14); a Remove by rank r targets only non-committers or committers of rank > r, **except a swap** — a Remove of leaf L and an Add of L's own pseudonym with rejoin or restore evidence, which any committer may commit for a member of any rank (the pseudonym keeps its place in `committers` and `funders`, its key being unchanged; §19.13); a GroupContextExtensions change comes from rank 0, except that any committer may drop from `committers` and `funders` the keys its commit removes, and any committer may drop **its own** key from them (a resignation), provided `committers` stays non-empty (§19.13); rank 0 may reorder `committers` (a transfer of rank 0); the rotation flag is set only by a committer (§6.5); the resulting `GhostChannelContext` is valid (§5.3). **Nothing in CO-2 reads the member's history, its `INVITE_ISSUED` records or any clock**: expiry of invites, single use and freshness are the admitting committer's policy (§6.2.4 (B), §6.7), and the admission window of CO-2 (A) is relative to the commit's own `commit_minute`, so members admitted at different times, and members whose clocks differ, judge every commit alike (RFC 9750 §5.3: honest clients "arrive at the same 'first valid Commit'"; §19.29.1).
- **CO-3 (winner).** Among the valid candidates for epoch e held at settle, excluding **abandoned** ones (a candidate whose own committer has an authenticated message that opens under another candidate's trial state: that committer moved on), the winner **maximises the number of successor senders**, then minimises `(rank, SHA-256(MLS commit bytes))`. The successor senders of candidate X are the distinct authenticated MLS senders — other than X's committer and the member itself — of the held blobs (up to 32, not the member's own) that open under `R_ep(e + 1)` of a **trial settle** of X: **one `MlsNative.trialCount` call** (§10.2 fn 20, S0-17) loads the rows once, settles X in memory, tries every held blob under the trial state (restoring the post-merge checkpoint after each, so no blob advances a ratchet), and returns the successor senders and whether X's own committer has a message among them (abandonment); nothing is written. (Round 10c composed a trial change-set with the stored rows in Kotlin and ran `receive` per blob: at 100 members with 64 retained epochs a trial settle alone cost 300 ms on the host, and up to 8 candidates × 33 full reloads per settle, V10-5.) The trial runs only when more than one valid candidate is held and some held blob does not open under the member's current keys; otherwise every count is 0 and CO-3 is the `(rank, hash)` rule. **A committer's own candidate without a verified copy** (CO-4) ranks below every held foreign candidate unless it has more successor senders than that candidate: other members may never have seen it (§19.13). A candidate that lost is kept only until the settle transaction ends.
- **CO-4 (settle point).** A member settles epoch e at the first actor step where (i) `now ≥ anchor(e) + 20 min`, with `anchor(e)` the earliest, over the held candidates, of min(the member's first sighting, the copy's **relay store hour** + 60 min), where the store hour is `relayExpiryHour − handshake TTL` of the copy it fetched, when the inbox row carries an expiry (otherwise only the sighting counts) (the relay rounds the expiry up to the hour, F7:485, so the store happened no later); and (ii) after (i) held, the member completed one listing pass over every relay of the current generation's set, so it holds every candidate stored before that pass. For its **own** commit the committer also requires a verified copy (the op's outcome is SENT or DEGRADED); if another candidate wins, it clears its pending commit (`clear_pending_commit`) and merges the winner; if its op ends FAILED (no copy can exist, F7's contract), it clears the pending commit. **Revised (§19.13): an own commit that may have reached a relay is never cleared on its own.** While the op is INDETERMINATE, and after it closes INDETERMINATE (Phase 7 resends the identical bytes at most twice), the committer keeps the commit pending, keeps listening, and at each settle point (a) merges it if it wins with a verified copy or with at least one successor sender in its trial settle (CO-3; for an unverified own commit the trial runs whenever a held blob does not open, even if it is the only candidate) — other members merged it and continued, which proves a copy existed; (b) clears it when another candidate wins at a settle point; (c) clears it when no copy can remain on any relay: the op's deadline plus the handshake TTL has passed with neither. Until then its own passes wait (CO-7) — the next rank picks up the pending work under CO-8's work-age rule — and its posts wait only if the commit removes someone (CO-13); the wait is rare, because Phase 7 closes a store INDETERMINATE only when no receipt arrived after its resends. Round 10b cleared it when the op closed without a verified copy; a copy others had merged then stranded the committer, because OpenMLS cannot open the author's own PrivateMessages (`OwnPrivateMessage`, `om:src/framing/validation.rs:158-172`) and so could never adopt its own winning commit later. The committer recognises its own blobs by their hashes (`ch_candidate.hash`, `ch_outbound_op`), never through `process_message`. No candidate blocks a settle for a lifetime reason: a lifetime-refused commit is invalid (CO-2; S0-12's and round 10d's blocking lifetime-pending candidates are withdrawn, §19.29.1). **Catch-up:** a returning member's anchors lie in the past, so each epoch settles right after the session's listing pass and the re-offer of deferred blobs (§7.5): N missed epochs cost N settles in one session, not N × 20 minutes.
- **CO-5 (merge).** The settle transaction (`CH-SETTLE`, time-triggered, local) re-processes the winner with persistence and merges it (`merge_staged_commit` or `merge_pending_commit`), derives `K_ep(e + 1)`, records `ch_epoch(e + 1, winner hash, epoch_authenticator)` and the epoch log, applies the rotation rule of §6.5 (the namespace switch itself is `CH-SWITCH`, §19.8), marks consumed invites and sponsors from the winner's `GhostCommitAad`, **voids every unconsumed invite whose `committers_digest` no longer matches the new `committers`** when the winner changed them (S0-15; the sponsor's UI tells its user and offers a new invite, §19.29.2) **and every unconsumed invite of an older `pw_version`** when the winner bumped it (§19.29.3), re-offers the channel's deferred blobs (`Inbox.reofferDeferred`), and deletes the other candidates. If the winner is the member's **own** commit, it enqueues that commit's WELCOME blobs in the same transaction (§6.2.3, §19.2). Epoch e is then **final** at this member.
- **CO-6 (late candidates; finality is sticky).** A member never leaves a final epoch because of a later commit. A commit-typed blob that opens under the seal of a final epoch e with a hash different from its winner is a **late candidate**: recorded as `ch_fork_signal(e, hash, first_seen_day)` and consumed. It is never validated, ranked or merged: OpenMLS rejects a handshake message whose epoch is not the current one before decrypting it (`validate_framing`, `om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:53-75`, valn1307), so its sender and validity cannot be known without a copy of the previous group state, which GHOST does not keep (§0.4). Anyone who held `K_ep(e)` — a member removed at e + 1 included — can forge one; fork signals therefore feed only diagnostics (`status()`), never a state change.
- **CO-7 (pass preconditions).** A committer runs a pass only when the channel is current (§6.3.1), it holds no pending commit, and it holds **no candidate of rank ≤ its own** for its current epoch (a rank-255 candidate does not block a pass: the committer's commit outranks it, §19.5).
- **CO-8 (pass schedule; D9; revised §19.13).** Every committer runs a pass at its own membership or policy actions (immediately, a user action, whatever its rank: a moderator's Remove does not wait for the owner), at each foreground session after catch-up plus U[0, 5 min] at most once per 6 h, and in a background job at most once per 24 h. Rank 0's scheduled pass commits **if work is pending** (join, restore and rejoin requests, leave requests, evictions due, a queued removal or policy change, a class change, a due rotation of §6.5), and otherwise only if its last commit is ≥ 7 days old (the PCS commit, empty with a path; **≥ 1 day in channels with timers ≤ 1 d**, Q36, §19.19). A committer of rank r ≥ 1 includes in a scheduled pass only **work it first recorded at least `r × 48 h` earlier** (so a moderator handles what the owner left pending for 48 h, the next moderator what both left for 96 h), plus its own PCS commit when its last commit is ≥ 7 days old (the daily cadence of short-timer channels is rank 0's alone: one committer's commit ends the epoch for everyone). Round 10b let rank r pass only while no lower rank had committed in `r × 48 h`; since rank 0 commits only weekly in a quiet channel, rank 1 was "active" about five days in seven and raced the owner on fresh requests routinely, not rarely. The work-age rule needs no liveness signal and races only when the owner returns to work it left for 48 h.
