| T27 | amended (§19.22): "a namespace that is not its own" becomes "a namespace that is neither its own nor a channel namespace it funds"; "redemptions and registrations do not depend on DM activity" gains "nor on channel activity, except the rotating committer's funding of the generation its own won commit opened (LC-3, LC-6)" |
| T28, T31 | T28's wording covers DMs; T40 is its channel twin. T31 already allows reading "canalele"; an invitee reads the invite's namespace until expiry; T31's "own writes at activity-independent times" gains the same exception as T27 (a funder's renewal is activity-independent; a rotating committer's funding follows its own commit) (§19.22) |
| T33 | extended: channel tables; OpenMLS state holds no sub-day time (day rewrite, day-aligned lifetimes), verified by a state scan over every stored `mls_kv` value (ME-C-series mutants); the activity-time columns it allows are exactly those §9 declares (§19.21), and the introspection test lists them (a new time column fails it) |

## 12. Test plan and exit-gate argument

**Lessons from Phase 9 built in (testing research §2).** L-1: counts (seeds, mutants) are pinned only after their cost is measured, and a mutant is registered only once its killer exists; S0 measured the call costs (V10-5, V10-10) but no gate world exists before S10, so **the seed counts are pinned at the S10 and S11 exits** from the measured per-seed time of SCN-M20 and of the privacy world and the dispatch windows of V10-14 (target unchanged: 64 seeds for SCN-M20), and **the mutant count is the registered count at S11's exit** (S0-18). L-2 (revised §19.11): CI restoration (the artifact quota, a KVM runner) is a precondition of the S10 and S11 exits, where the gates first run, not of S0 — S0's checks run locally. L-3 (revised by V10-14, S0-14): the runner budget is 4 runners on the owner's laptop, running only while he is logged in (`docs/ci-self-hosted.md` §1). **Measured by trigger:** on the days without pushes to main the scheduled workflows used 19–21 % of the four runners' time; on development days the runners were busy 87–97 % of the day, 54–67 % of it push-triggered (`t2-exit-gate` pair 0 with its mutants, the 12 `entitlement-exit-gate` shards, `messaging-exit-gate`, `ci`), and queued scheduled jobs waited up to 23 h behind them; scheduled runs are created 275–340 minutes after their cron times (07:18–07:56 UTC, the owner's morning), so "nightly" gates run in the day. The channel gates therefore run **on dispatch at hours the owner chooses and weekly**, never on every push (§13.2). L-4: exact joins and twins carry the privacy proof; statistics over a saturated LC attacker prove nothing, so Phase 10 has none. L-5: every property needs a mutant that can falsify it.

### 12.1 Infrastructure: `ChannelWorld`

- **Clients:** 2–24 per world (60 in the privacy world), each a real `:messaging` + `:crypto-bridge` + `:sync` stack on its own SQLite file (production pragmas, WAL and DELETE journal modes), migrated to v7, driven by the Phase 7 `DeterministicDriver`, sharing the DM harness through the shared-test-source pattern of F9 §15.1.
- **Real OpenMLS** through `MlsNative` loading `ghost-dm-host` (which now links `ghost-client-mls`). **Determinism (corrected §19.10):** the seeded `getrandom` backend F9 designed was **never built** (F9 §22 item 13; l.3862), and OpenMLS draws HPKE randomness from `SysRng` on the `rayon` worker thread (`treekem.rs:75`, `hpke-rs-libcrux/src/lib.rs:355-369`), so the per-actor-thread stream F9 described cannot attribute those draws to a world. S2 builds a **process-global** ChaCha20 stream keyed by the world seed behind getrandom's custom backend (`--cfg getrandom_backend="custom"`, host build only, G-R2), with every native call of a JVM serialised through one lock so the draw order is the call order; V10-6 requires two runs of a scripted world to produce byte-identical commits, Welcomes and blob hashes. **V10-6 confirmed it in a first probe (S0-7):** two processes of an 8-member scripted world (posts, a self-update, removes, an add with its Welcome) printed identical messages, rows and epoch authenticators, under three **preconditions** now normative: (a) `rayon` at **one** worker (with the default pool the commits, Welcomes and state differ between runs); (b) the **`added_at` day rewrite** of §3.4 point 5 (without it the messages are identical but every member's stored rows differ: OpenMLS stamps `SystemTime::now()`), so the rewrite is a determinism requirement as well as T33's; (c) a **single getrandom major** in the host library's linked graph — today getrandom 0.4.3, whose custom symbol is `__getrandom_v03_custom` — which G-R2 checks with `cargo tree -p ghost-dm-host -e normal,no-proc-macro` (a check over every graph would also see getrandom 0.2.17, which the test-only `ghost-client-mls-kat` links through `openmls/test-utils` → `openmls_rust_crypto` and the `openmls_test` proc-macro; that crate is not a determinism subject). If S2's stream still fails V10-6, the harness records and replays native outputs from the start, and crash enumeration uses the recording (sized in §14).
- **Virtual time (§19.4; revised §19.29.1).** OpenMLS checks KeyPackage lifetimes against the host's `SystemTime::now()`, which no world can move. With the constant lifetime of Q42 (a) that check never refuses a GHOST KeyPackage, so worlds run on virtual days without anchoring and SCN-M20's ≈ 80-day bound lifts. (Under Q42 (b), finite lifetimes `[d − 4, d + 80]`, worlds must run in the past — first virtual day ≥ `host day − 79`, last ≤ `host day + 3` — and longer worlds are refused by the harness; measured: KeyPackages made on host − 79 … host + 4 are accepted at the host clock.) Expiry cases (an expired invite, a `GhostKpBind.day` outside the window at a committer) are tested by GHOST's own day checks; OpenMLS's own lifetime site is tested in Rust: a finite-lifetime (non-canonical) KeyPackage refused on the refusal branch and on the accept branch, one verdict for every member (E-69, E-70).
- **`ModelRelay`** (conformance-pinned by `relay_semantics.txt` and `register.txt`) with the Phase 7 and 9 hostile modes plus: **FORK** (serves different same-epoch commits to two subsets), **WITHHOLD_COMMIT** (hides one commit from a subset for a chosen time, or for good), **REORDER_ACROSS_EPOCH** (application messages after the next commit), **RESURRECT_COMMIT**, **DROP_WELCOME**, **EXPIRE_LOG** (drops handshake blobs before an offline member returns), **FLOOD_SELFUPDATE** (a member's modified client floods rank-255 commits), **COPY_JOIN** (a join request copied to another namespace), **LATE_COMMIT** (a valid commit for an epoch stored after the others settled, of a better rank), **GRIND** (a modified member client keeps an old epoch's state and stores a hash-ground self-update for a settled epoch), **SPAM_UNOPENABLE** (a member or invite holder stores random blobs in the current namespace, or a member removed at the end of the old generation in the draining one), **REPLAY_JOIN** (an insider re-wraps an earlier KeyPackage as a rejoin or restore), and, added by §19.13 … §19.18: **RESEAL_ORDER** (a member re-seals another member's post under a false `order`), **PROPOSE** (a member stores an MLS proposal), **FORGE_OWN** (a member forges sender data so that a blob claims the victim's own leaf), **REPLACE_BOOTSTRAP** (a holder of `JK_g` re-seals a WELCOME with its own bootstrap; since S0-9 the bootstrap is inside the signed GroupInfo, so this is a case of the next mode), **FAKE_WELCOME** (added by S0-15: a holder of `JK_g` who is not a committer opens a join request, builds its own group under the channel's id and deposits a Welcome for the joiner's KeyPackage; variants: its own committers list, the real list copied), **NEW_SIGNER** (a member's modified client commits an update path with a new signature key or a mismatched credential), **SILENT_ORPHAN** (EXPIRE_LOG on a rotating commit for one member, after which its old namespace goes quiet), and the Phase 9 harness debts this phase builds (§19.10): the redeem lane inside the world, send delays with HIGH mode (`not_before_minute` honoured), circuit open and close times, refusals.
- **Faults:** `FaultySqlExecutor` at every statement, pre- and post-commit, `SQLITE_FULL` injection; `SimulatedNativeCrash` inside `receive`, `settle` (real and trial), `commit` and `welcomeJoin`; a virtual clock with steps.
- **Oracles:** `oracle_epoch(client, epoch, winner_hash, epoch_authenticator, tree_hash)`; `oracle_displayed(client, op_id)` with a primary key; the **reload oracle** (§3.4 point 6); `oracle_seizure(removed client)` and `oracle_joiner(client)` (§12.8); `oracle_traffic(client, store | redeem | register, cause)` written by every facade call and every scheduled step, so NI-CH1 and G-M5 can map every relay-visible act to a non-inbound cause.

### 12.2 Unit, vector, property and interoperability tests

- **Rust (`cargo test -p ghost-client-mls`, `rust` job):** derivation vectors `ch_derive.txt`; seal vectors `ch_seal.txt` (every mode and bucket, hints, copy and truncation refused); `ch_content.txt`, `ch_invite.txt`, `ch_join.txt`; `GhostKvScopeTest` (all 53 storage methods of the feature set mapped, S0-6); the day rewrite (golden `MessageSecrets`) and the split of past epochs (golden head and per-epoch rows; reassembly byte-identical to the unsplit store); change-set atomicity (a call that fails after partial writes returns nothing; a batched call rolls back a failed item, S0-17); `rayon` single worker; the admission checks of §6.2.4 (each refusal; the canonical KeyPackage's 395 bytes); CO-2 validity (each rule, the lifetime predicate on both branches, canonical leaves); the committers anchor of `welcomeJoin` (S0-15; the V10-8 forged-Welcome test and the corrupted-GroupInfo-signature test move here from the spike, since upstream's own test of `create_group_info_with_extensions` is `#[ignore]`d at the tag); the golden sizes of §5.7 (G-M7); property tests with 10⁶ seeded inputs per CI run for every GHOST parser (never panics, never over-reads).
- **Kotlin (`android` job):** an independent JDK implementation replays `ch_derive.txt`, `ch_seal.txt` (outer layer and hints), `ch_content.txt` and `ch_invite.txt`; `MlsResult` exhaustiveness; migrations 6 and 7 (introspection, triggers, guards, REPLACE refusals); the `Funding` planner rule; the credential selection (a registration capability never reaches store, list or get).
- **RFC 9420 interoperability fixtures (spec Appendix A; revised §19.10).** (1) The MLS WG test vectors, as OpenMLS carries them in `openmls/test_vectors/` (`passive-client-welcome`, `passive-client-handling-commit`, `passive-client-random`, `tree-validation`, `treekem`, `message-protection`, `welcome`, `deserialization`, `storage-stability` and the others), run by OpenMLS's own KAT tests in `mls-vendor-tests` — **a git checkout of the openmls repository at commit `3a3e35de3feeca8f6605143c464d5452ae584d43`** (tag `openmls-v0.9.0`, the commit the crates.io package was built from, `.cargo_vcs_info.json`), pinned by hash: the crates.io package ships only `benches`, `examples`, `src` and `tests`, while the KATs read `../test_vectors/*.json`. (2) GHOST replays the three passive-client files **through `GhostKv` and the value-semantics call sequence** in a separate test-only crate `ghost-client-mls-kat` (outside every shipped graph, G-M2) with `openmls/test-utils` as a dev-dependency, because injecting the vectors' private keys needs `KeyPackageBundle::new` (`om:src/key_packages/mod.rs:752-756`, `test-utils` only). OpenMLS's own replay switches off lifetime validation through a crate-internal flag (`passive_client.rs:110,195`; "Some KATs use key packages that are expired by now", `validation.rs:875-881`) that no other crate can set. **Measured (V10-12, S0-13):** the subset is set by the provider's suites, not by the clock — every Add in the replayed commits carries the lifetime `[0, u64::MAX]` (1 550 of 1 550), and the joins need `JoinBuilder::skip_lifetime_validation()` (42 of 43 are refused without it), which the replay uses. GHOST's replay therefore runs **every vector of the libcrux provider's suites (0x0001, 0x0003): 43 of the 148 passive-client vectors, 252 epochs**, and names the 105 vectors of suites 0x0002, 0x0004 … 0x0007 as not applicable; it must reproduce every `epoch_authenticator` (V10-12: 252 of 252, with the reload oracle's fingerprint half after every call, 1 861 checks; also with 16 retained past epochs, split and unsplit byte-identical). **Coverage of GHOST's own suite is thin:** 0x0003 contributes 21 vectors and 26 epochs (the other 226 epochs are 0x0001, 200 of them one random vector), which OpenMLS's own suite at 0x0003 complements (below, `all-ciphersuites`); Q22 is re-asked with this. **What it does not prove:** GHOST's join path — the replay joins through OpenMLS's `JoinBuilder` with the vectors' configuration (mixed plaintext, 16 resumption PSKs), and every vector fails `welcomeJoin`'s structural checks and CO-2 (A) by design (§10.1). The vector files are committed with their SHA-256 and the upstream commit; their licence: `mlswg/mls-implementations` declares none, the copies are in the MIT-licensed openmls repository, test data never distributed (§13.3). (3) An optional differential peer (mls-rs, test-only; Q22).

### 12.3 EM-3: the exhaustive commit-arbitration model (the F9 EM-1 method)

An abstract model of CO-1 … CO-14 and §6.5–§6.7 in Rust, enumerated exhaustively: 2 committers (ranks 0 and 1), 2 members, epochs ≤ 4, message loss, duplication and reordering, a partition of either side for a chosen interval or for good, settle timers with store-hour anchors, process death at every step, one removal, one join and one rejoin. **Adversaries (added by §19.1):** a late but better commit (stored after every honest member settled); a malicious member that keeps an old epoch's state and grinds a self-update below a settled winner; a returning member that catches up across several epochs with and without successors on each candidate; a member flooding self-updates; a member or invite holder storing unopenable blobs; a relay withholding a removing commit from a subset of two members for good; a replayed KeyPackage re-wrapped as a rejoin or restore; a member whose system clock rejects a lifetime; **and (added by §19.13 … §19.15)** a committer whose own commit ends INDETERMINATE while others merge it, the owner on a dead branch (it must be readmitted), a resignation and a transfer of rank 0 followed by the former owner's leave, a moderator and the owner acting on the same aged request, a commit installing a leaf with a new signature key, and a member that misses a rotating commit for good (silent orphan); **and (added by S0-12, revised §19.29.1)** members with different device and trusted clocks, and a member of any rank storing a commit that carries a non-canonical (finite, expired) KeyPackage, which every member must judge invalid on both of OpenMLS's branches while the settle proceeds. Properties: **convergence** (T41 among members that held the same candidates and blobs at settle; no settled member ever leaves a branch because of a late or ground commit); **detection** (a member whose branch is dead reaches `resync_needed` within the CO-11 bound once the other branch's traffic reaches it; a subset cut off for good raises `partition_suspected` within 9 days, and the majority raises `split_suspected` within the retention plus 72 h when the subset posts; a silent orphan raises `stale_suspected` within 9 days); **no stranded committer** (a committer whose own commit others merged ends on their branch, by merging it or by a rejoin, whatever its rank); **no invalid merge** (no rank-255 commit with proposals or above the CO-14 rate, no removal of a lower rank, no Add without valid evidence, no swap without rejoin or restore evidence); **liveness** (with rank 0 online and no partition, every pending request is committed within two passes, and a flooding member never blocks a committer's pass); **catch-up latency** (a member back after N ≤ 30 epochs whose commits are still on the relays is current after one listing pass of one session, with no wall-clock wait); **Welcomes** (no joiner opens a Welcome of a commit that did not win); **removal hold** (no post encrypted in epoch e by an honest member after it held the removing candidate for e); **rotation** (a removal always yields a generation the removed member cannot name); **lifetimes** (revised §19.29.1: no member's verdict on a commit depends on its clocks; no commit carrying a non-canonical lifetime is merged; no lifetime refusal blocks a settle or a pass). Mutants MM-2, MM-3, MM-4, MM-16, MM-19, MM-21, MM-23, MM-24, MM-26, MM-32, MM-33, MM-34, MM-36 and MM-46 must fail EM-3 (MM-27 and MM-40 are withdrawn with Q42 (a), §19.29.1). Job `channel-state-models` (on pull requests that touch the arbitration code, weekly and on dispatch, §13.2).

### 12.4 Crash enumeration (CS-C1, CS-C2; the F7 §8.3 method)

| Scenario | Content |
|---|---|
| **SCN-CA** create and admit | create; invite; accept; join request; the committer's pass; Welcome; join |
| **SCN-CB** posts | 3 members, 30 posts with SHUFFLE and REPEATS across 3 relays, a burst of 40 from one sender (peek ordering), a RESURRECTed post, a COPY_NAMESPACE'd post |
| **SCN-CC** membership | an add and a remove in one pass, settle, rotation, the new generation's funding and registrations, drain |
| **SCN-CD** concurrency | ranks 0 and 1 commit on one epoch, **the losing one with an Add**; a rank-255 self-update on the same epoch; the loser clears, deletes its Welcome and re-passes; the joiner receives exactly one Welcome, of the winning commit, and its KeyPackage is consumed once (MM-19); **INDETERMINATE variant** (§19.13): the committer's store closes INDETERMINATE while the relays did keep a copy; the others merge it and post; the committer merges its own commit from their successors and never strands (MM-32) |
| **SCN-CE** offline | a member offline across 5 epochs and one rotation, then catch-up in order; **latency assertion**: current after one listing pass of its first session, with no wall-clock wait (CO-4 anchors, `reofferDeferred`); a variant across 30 epochs with the same assertion; a variant with EXPIRE_LOG → `resync_needed` → rejoin |
| **SCN-CF** ephemeral | 5 min, 1 h and 7 d channels; displayed, unread, pending; a timer change; a clock step back |
| **SCN-CG** fork | FORK mode splits members; both sides settle; a one-member side finds its branch dead (CO-11) and rejoins; a several-member side raises `split_suspected` and rejoins after the scripted fingerprint comparison; the owner's side unaffected |
| **SCN-CH** faults | `SQLITE_FULL` at every statement of RECV, PASS, SETTLE; `SimulatedNativeCrash` in `receive` and `settle` (poison rules of F9 §7.8) |
| **SCN-CI** migration | a v5 database with live DM and sync rows → v6 → v7, then SCN-CA |
| **SCN-CJ** reveal | a reveal to one member; others open nothing; the recipient adds the contact (source `link`, trust `unverified`); after the recipient's next leaf refresh and the old epoch's retention, its seed and state open no earlier REVEAL (PC-18) |
| **SCN-CK** leave and evict | a leave, an eviction at 63 days, a restore swap (it rotates; the replaced stack is a T38 subject) and a rejoin swap (it does not rotate); a moderator's resignation and leave; the owner's transfer of rank 0 and its leave (§19.13) |
| **SCN-CL** withheld removal (§19.1, PRV-H4) | WITHHOLD_COMMIT hides a removing commit **for good** from a subset S of two active members; S keeps posting; assertions: the majority raises `split_suspected` within the retention of S's epoch plus 72 h while the old namespace drains, S raises `partition_suspected` within 9 days, the removed member's reads of S's posts are counted and reported (the declared L8-C17 bound, not a zero), and S's `resync` after the fingerprint comparison restores it; **silent-orphan variant** (§19.15, SILENT_ORPHAN): one member misses a rotating commit for good; it raises `stale_suspected` within 9 days (MM-36) and its `resync` inside the drain restores it, after the drain it reaches `reinvite_needed` |
| **SCN-CM** adversarial members (§19.1, §19.3, §19.5) | LATE_COMMIT with a better rank after everyone settled (nobody moves; a returning member picks the branch with successors); GRIND by a member keeping an old state (nobody moves; catch-up picks the successors' branch); FLOOD_SELFUPDATE (CO-14 refuses the excess, committers still pass and remove the flooder); SPAM_UNOPENABLE in a quiet channel (a member may enter `resync_needed` and returns to `active` at the next progress; its rejoin is refused); REPLAY_JOIN (an old join KeyPackage as rejoin or restore is refused, a copied rejoin completes the requester's own rejoin, the victim is never evicted); a member whose system clock is 10 days slow (it processes every commit like the others: no stall, no divergence; revised §19.29.1); **added by S0-12, revised §19.29.1:** a member of any rank storing a commit that carries a finite-lifetime KeyPackage, expired for every clock (invalid for every member on both branches; the settle proceeds and the owner's next pass removes the member; MM-46); **added by §19.14 … §19.18:** SPAM_UNOPENABLE by a member removed at the end of the old generation in the draining namespace (nobody enters `resync_needed`, MM-37); RESEAL_ORDER (the victim's posts all display, MM-35); PROPOSE (dropped and counted; posting continues, MM-30); FORGE_OWN (dropped and counted; the victim's own-commit bookkeeping is unaffected); NEW_SIGNER (the commit is invalid everywhere, MM-31); REPLACE_BOOTSTRAP (the joiner refuses the substituted bootstrap and keeps waiting for a valid Welcome, PC-17) |

After every reboot: `integrity_check`, `foreign_key_check`, every trigger present; at most one current and one draining generation per channel; ≤ 8 candidates per epoch plus the own commit; no Welcome op for a commit that is not the merged winner; `mls_kv` rows open under the state key; the reload oracle; every `queued` post has an op or a pending row; epoch keys exist for exactly the retained epochs. At quiescence: CS-C2, CS-C3, CS-C6, T40 (every store maps to a non-inbound cause), liveness (one more post decrypts everywhere).

### 12.5 The exit scenario SCN-M20 ("20 clienți add/remove/resync")

**Setup:** 20 clients; 3 relays over 2 operators (one per scenario variant hostile); privacy mode STANDARD, and HIGH once the harness honours send delays (§19.10; S11's since the S1a re-scoping, §19.33, so SCN-M20 of S10 runs STANDARD only and the report says so); channel timer 7 d (a 1 h variant for the T25 clause); rank 0 = client 1, rank 1 = client 2; funders = clients 1 and 2.

**Script (virtual time ≈ 80 days, anchored so that its last day is the host day, §12.1, so that the 63-day eviction and a 30-day offline window fit):** client 1 creates the channel and invites 19 clients over three days (invites by link; batches bounded by the floor); ≈ 200 posts with reactions and replies; **removals:** a moderator removal, a leave, an idle eviction (a client silent 63 days — time is virtual), and a **device swap** (client 7 "restores": a new stack with the same seed rejoins through a new invite; the restore rotates the generation; its old stack keeps its state and is a seizure subject), with an **owner-restore variant** in which client 1 (rank 0) restores the same way and keeps rank 0 (§19.13, MM-33); two clients offline across ≥ 5 epochs and one rotation, then **resync** from the relays; one client offline past EXPIRE_LOG, which reaches `resync_needed` and rejoins; ranks 0 and 1 commit concurrently on one epoch (CO-3); a **FORK** variant in which the model relays serve two commits to two subsets for longer than the settle window: a subset of one member finds its branch dead (CO-11) and rejoins; a subset of several active members raises `split_suspected`, and its scripted users, whose fingerprint differs from the owner's, run `resync`; a removed client re-invited, returning with the same pseudonym (D12).

**Assertions:** at every epoch, every honest current member's `(winner_hash, epoch_authenticator)` is equal (T41); no duplicate display, one order everywhere; **the removed-member oracle opens 0** blobs of later epochs for each removed client and for the old stack of client 7, and 0 blobs of any later generation for that old stack (the plan's second clause, LIMITE:39; §19.16), and 0 posts composed after their author first held the removing commit (CO-13); the joiner oracle opens 0 for each joiner; T9 and T13 on every blob and post; every Welcome ≤ C(65536) and every commit within its floor (S0-5); the channel T25 cases pass (the plan's third clause); T37 and T40 on the world's relay view.

**Scale:** every PR runs SCN-M20 with one seed and crash singles on SCN-CA … SCN-CD; `channel-exit-gate` runs it with the full crash enumeration and **seeds pinned at S10's exit** from the measured per-seed time and the dispatch windows of V10-14 (target: 64, the number Phase 9's gate actually ran; S0-18: S0 could not measure a world that S10 builds).

### 12.6 Functional suites

| Suite | Cases |
|---|---|
| **RO-C reorder** | all orders of 6 posts of one sender; bursts of 40 over 3 relays with HIGH delays; posts after the next commit (REORDER_ACROSS_EPOCH) within and beyond retention; **re-seal** (RESEAL_ORDER: a member re-seals a victim's late post under an early `order`; the copy is dropped at `open`, every post of the victim displays, MM-35); a burst of 40 with 20 withheld past the hold (the declared ratchet-range loss is counted, §6.3.6); a committer posting while its own non-removing commit is pending (the posts decrypt everywhere as late posts of e, §19.13); **a batch with a replayed or undecryptable message in its middle** (S0-17: the rest of the batch decrypts, the failed item leaves no state, the reload oracle agrees; MM-43) |
| **RS-C restart** | process death at every lane item and actor step; a member offline 60 days in a 30-day channel |
| **RP-C replay** | each dedup layer isolated (§6.3.4); a resurrected commit; a copied join request; a member's proposal (dropped unprocessed; the receiver's next post is not refused, MM-30); a forged "own" message (dropped, counted) |
| **JN join** | every admission refusal of §6.2.4 (A) and (B); single use across two committers present since the invite; expiry; **an invite of a removed sponsor refused** (void at settle, and CO-2 refuses the Add if a committer admits it anyway); a committer that joined after the `INVITE_ISSUED` does not admit it; a joiner admitted after the `INVITE_ISSUED` accepts a later Add of that invite (evidence in the AAD); a join during a rotation (Welcome in the draining namespace); a rejoin by a current pseudonym with each freshness refusal of §6.7; a restore by invite for a current pseudonym (a swap); an old join KeyPackage re-wrapped as rejoin or restore refused; `GhostKpBind` of another channel or purpose refused; an invite for a channel already joined; **added by §19.16:** a WELCOME whose bootstrap a holder of `JK_g` replaced (refused, PC-17); a bootstrap naming the invite's generation with a different namespace (refused); **added by S0-4, S0-15:** FAKE_WELCOME with the forger's own committers list and with the real list copied (both refused by the anchor, nothing written, the KeyPackage intact, the real Welcome then joins; PC-19), a rejoin Welcome signed by a key outside the rejoiner's last committers (refused), an invite voided by a change of `committers` (not admitted; a commit carrying an Add and changing `committers` is invalid, MM-41), a KeyPackage of any non-canonical form or size (refused at the codec and at admission, MM-15); a rejoin and a restore Welcome with the old group's rows present (the rows are not passed; no `GroupAlreadyExists`); a restore of the owner and of a moderator by a lower-ranked committer (admitted, rank kept); **added by §19.27, §19.29:** the anchor checked before any tree validation (MM-44); a legitimate rejoin Welcome after the live branch added a committer accepted (signature under a last committer, no digest comparison; E-72) and a non-committer's forged rejoin Welcome refused; a forger's fresh group naming the real committers refused by §5.3's validity; the removed committer's fork recorded as the declared capture (L8-C25); **the password (§19.28, §19.29.3):** a password channel's invite admitted with the password; an open invite in a password channel, a stale `pw_version` and a wrong password refused (MM-PW2); an open invite admitted after the password is removed; the settle voiding invites of an older `pw_version`; with Q46 (b) a non-committer's invite in a password channel refused |
| **GV governance** | CO-2 rules (removal of a lower rank refused, context change by rank 1 refused, rotation flag from rank 255 refused, a swap without evidence refused, a rank-255 commit above the CO-14 rate refused, an update-path leaf with a wrong `refresh_epoch` refused; **added by §19.13, §19.14:** an update-path leaf with a new signature key, or with a credential identity other than its key, refused (MM-31); a resignation accepted, a resignation by the last committer refused, a transfer of rank 0 by rank 0 accepted and by rank 1 refused; a swap of rank 0 by rank 1 with rejoin or restore evidence accepted; a moderator's scheduled pass ignoring work younger than 48 h, and its user-action Remove committed at once (MM-34); rank read from the pre-commit context for a commit that reorders `committers`); **added by S0-5, S0-12, S0-16:** an update-path leaf with an extra extension or extra capabilities refused (the inflated self-update of V10-4, MM-38); an Add of a KeyPackage with any lifetime other than the constant one refused, on both of OpenMLS's branches (MM-39, MM-46; §19.29.1); a pass that removes a committer and swaps a member over the floor defers the swap and keeps the Remove with the drop of the committer's key (MM-42); moderator list changes; the S5 simulation over the threshold function |
| **FD funding** | renewal at the PRF time; rotation funding by the rotating committer at its switch hour; a non-rotating funder's first redemption for the new generation at its next EXPIRING renewal, not at the settle (PC-6); a funder without tokens (stores wait, then complete); registrations lapsing after the drain; periodic and invite-expiry rotations (Q28), never while a generation drains; a restore rotates, a rejoin does not (MM-18, MM-29); the drain's end with deferred junk rows still fetched (marked consumed, then `Namespaces.remove` succeeds, §6.5) |
| **FK fork** | FORK at a rotation (two generation secrets): recovery through the old generation during the drain; a late candidate for a settled epoch changes nothing but a fork signal; forged fork signals by a member removed at e + 1 change nothing |
| **EP-C ephemeral** | §8.5 |
| **RV reveal** | recipient-only opening; binding checks; a forged reveal (wrong pseudonym) refused; a reveal addressed to a box key the recipient has since replaced still opens within the old epoch's retention and not after (PC-18); the contact is added with source `link`; the `ch_reveal` rows disappear with the REVEAL post's timer |

### 12.7 The channel privacy world

A world of 60 clients, 12 channels (5–20 members; at most 9 per client), invites, removals and rotations, DMs present, modes STANDARD and HIGH (HIGH, JC-6's send-delay rules, NI-CH3 and the registration half of JC-3 need the harness extensions of §19.10 (S11's since the S1a re-scoping, §19.33): the redeem lane inside the world, send delays, circuit timing; until they exist each report line names what it compared); PR scale 28 virtual days, one seed; gate scale 56 days, seeds pinned at S11's exit from the measured per-seed time (S0-18). Exact searches over the effective relay view (every capture event, stored bytes, `keys.redb`, circuits):

| ID | Search | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| JC-1 | every relay-visible value recomputed from public material (channel ids known to members, pseudonym and box keys, KeyPackages, invite ids) | 0 matches |
| JC-2 | stored blob bytes scanned for group ids, epochs, KeyPackageRefs, pseudonym keys, `ChannelContent` headers | 0 |
| JC-3 | distinct store scopes and read scopes per (relay, generation namespace) | exactly 1 and 1; registration capabilities only in `register` |
| JC-4 | per (generation namespace, timer version) in 7-day, 30-day and off channels: distinct TTLs, and buckets of commits vs posts ≤ the floor (revised §19.22: a timer change between those values moves the TTL inside one namespace; join requests under an invite from an earlier timer version are counted and reported as the declared exception) | 1 TTL per timer version; commits only in the floor bucket |
| JC-5 | a removed member's credentials or secrets accepted in a later generation's namespace | 0 |
| JC-6 | the relay-visible time of any store equals, within the send-delay rules, the time of a non-inbound cause in `oracle_traffic` | all |
| JC-7 (added §19.20) | the model relay tries every value it was shown — every READ and DEPOSIT secret inside a presented credential, every verifier, every registration capability, and the namespace — as the key input K and as the recognition key R of §5.6 (every mode, every bucket) on every blob it stores | 0 hints match, 0 blobs open |
| NI-CH1 | twins differing only in what one client received (valid post, forged blob, replay, a join request addressed to it as committer, a commit, a rejoin request) | identical views of that client, except LC-6's pass outcome |
| NI-CH2 | twins where a committer stores a non-removing commit vs a post of equal length at the same minute | identical views (revised §19.22: since a non-removing own commit no longer holds the committer's posts, §19.13, the views stay identical after the store too; a **removing** commit is compared only up to its store, because the removal hold then pauses every member's posts, the committer's first, CO-13) |
| NI-CH3 | twins where a funder funds 1 vs 4 channels at fixed renewal times | identical per-namespace views; the count of funded namespaces is declared (LC-3) |
| NI-CH4 | twins where a client receives a rotating commit vs a non-rotating commit of equal length at the same minute (§19.8) | identical views except LC-4's declared difference: the first list of `ns_{g+1}` at the commit's switch hour (or the next session start), no redemption unless the client is the rotating committer or at its next renewal as a funder, the removed member's listing unchanged until the drain ends |
| NI-CH5 (added §19.29.3) | twins where one channel is password-protected and the other open, with the same invites, joins and posts (the password channel's invites sealed, its joiners opening them with the password) | identical relay views of every client; the declared difference: an invite that expires unconsumed (a sponsor's typo cannot be detected, and a password change voids outstanding invites) sets the rotation flag (§6.5), so a password channel with such invites rotates more often (LC-4), counted and reported |

### 12.8 Seizure, removal and joiner oracles

- **Removed-member oracle (T38):** dump the removed client's database, WAL, `mls_kv` and native context; try every stored 32-byte value, every retained `K_ep` and `JK_g`, every past-epoch secret (through `MlsGroup::load` of its stale state and `process_message`) and every KeyPackage key against every mode-0x11 blob of every epoch after its removal and every blob of every later generation (the join traffic of the draining generation, which it may open by design, L8-C5, excepted). Opened must be 0. Variants: removed while offline; removed while its own commit was pending; re-added (the epochs between removal and re-add open 0); **hold** (§19.6): every post composed by an honest member after its first sighting of the removing candidate opens 0 under the removed member's keys, and the posts it can open (composed before their author's sighting) are counted and reported against the L8-C18 bound.
- **Joiner oracle (T39):** the same over all blobs of epochs before the join epoch; the generation's earlier join traffic, which the joiner opens by design with the invite's `JK_g`, is counted and reported, not asserted to be 0 (§19.22).
- **Restore variant (T38, §19.16):** the replaced stack's dump against every blob of every generation after the restoring commit: 0.
- **Device seizure (AD-8, declared bounds; corrected §19.19):** a current member's dump opens exactly the current epoch's unconsumed messages from the epoch's start and the retained epochs' unconsumed messages (§8.3), measured against every recorded blob of the world (not only those still on relays), and the report prints the age of the oldest message opened against the L8-C10 bound; no expired post's plaintext remains (T25 physical scan).

### 12.9 Mutants (registered only with their killer; the count is the registered count at S11's exit, S0-18)

| ID | Mutation | Killer |

| MM-1 | change-set applied before OK (partial state) | SCN-CH, reload oracle |
| MM-2 | own commit merged without a verified copy | EM-3, SCN-CD |
| MM-3 | no settle window | EM-3, SCN-M20 FORK |
| MM-4 | tie-break by hash only | EM-3 |
| MM-5 | rank-255 commit with proposals accepted | GV, EM-3 |
| MM-6 | context change from rank ≥ 1 accepted | GV |
| MM-7 | no rotation on removal | T38 oracle, JC-5 |
| MM-8 | next generation derived from the pre-commit epoch | T38 oracle |
| MM-9 | past epochs kept beyond retention | seizure bound test |
| MM-10 | posts encrypted at compose while not current | RO-C (late posts lost beyond retention) |
| MM-11 | hash-order decryption (no peek ordering) | SCN-CB burst |
| MM-12 | unopenable blobs dropped instead of deferred | SCN-CE |
| MM-13 | invite single-use not enforced | JN |
| MM-14 | `invite_sig` not verified | JN (sock-puppet case) |
| MM-15 | KeyPackage checks skipped (a non-canonical KeyPackage — extra leaf extension, extra capabilities, another size — admitted; S0-4) | JN |
| MM-16 | finality not sticky: a settled member re-decides on a later (late or ground) commit | SCN-CM (LATE_COMMIT, GRIND), EM-3 |
| MM-17 | eviction ignores self-updates | CK |
| MM-18 | rejoin rotates the generation | FD, SCN-CK |
| MM-19 | Welcomes enqueued at the pass, not at the settle of a won commit | SCN-CD (losing commit with an Add), EM-3 |
| MM-20 | rejoin admitted without freshness (seen KeyPackage, stale day, live leaf, claimed epoch on the committer's branch) | JN, SCN-CM (REPLAY_JOIN) |
| MM-21 | CO-2 accepts a swap without rejoin or restore evidence | GV, EM-3 |
| MM-22 | an invite of a removed sponsor admitted (no void, no CO-2 sponsor check) | JN |
| MM-23 | CO-2 reads local history (an Add checked against the member's own `INVITE_ISSUED` records) | JN (a later joiner diverges; `oracle_epoch`), EM-3 |
| MM-24 | no successor preference at settle (plain `(rank, hash)`) | SCN-CM (catch-up after LATE_COMMIT), EM-3 |
| MM-25 | settle anchored at the local first sighting only (no store-hour anchor, no re-offer) | SCN-CE latency assertion |

| MM-27 | **withdrawn with Q42 (a) (§19.29.1):** its subject, the lifetime-pending candidate of S0-12, no longer exists (reinstated only if the owner chooses Q42 (b)) | — |
| MM-28 | past epochs deleted by `before_timestamp(day)` instead of by count | RO-C late post within retention; seizure bound test |
| MM-29 | a restore swap does not rotate the generation (§19.16) | T38 restore variant (the replaced stack opens a later generation's blobs), FD |
| MM-30 | a received proposal is stored (`store_pending_proposal`) instead of dropped (§19.16) | RP-C proposal case (the member's next post is refused `PendingProposal`) |
| MM-31 | CO-2 accepts an installed leaf whose signature key changed or whose credential identity differs from its key (§19.14) | GV (commits built with a changed signer and with a mismatched credential in the test-only builder) |
| MM-32 | the committer clears its own commit when the op closes INDETERMINATE (round 10b's rule, §19.13) | SCN-CD INDETERMINATE variant (the others merge the commit; the committer must merge it from its successors, not strand) |
| MM-33 | CO-2 refuses a swap of a committer by a higher rank (§19.13) | SCN-M20 owner-restore variant; SCN-CG with the owner on the dead side |
| MM-34 | a moderator's scheduled pass includes work younger than `r × 48 h` (§19.13) | GV (a fresh join request is not committed by rank 1 while rank 0 is active); EM-3 concurrency count |
| MM-35 | peek order taken from the seal's `order` without the `ChannelOrder` check (§19.18) | RO-C re-seal case (a member re-seals a victim's post with a false `order`; no post of the victim is lost) |
| MM-36 | no `stale_suspected` (§19.15) | SCN-CL silent-orphan variant (a member misses a rotating commit past EXPIRE_LOG; the flag rises within 9 days), EM-3 detection |
| MM-37 | CO-11 counts unopenable blobs in a draining namespace (§19.15) | SCN-CM spam from a member removed at the end of the old generation (no member enters `resync_needed`) |
| MM-38 | CO-2 accepts a non-canonical update-path leaf (an extra extension or extra capabilities; S0-5) | GV (V10-4's inflated self-update refused); SCN-M20's Welcome-size assertion |
| MM-39 | (retargeted §19.29.1) CO-2 (A) or the join's structural check admits a KeyPackage or KeyPackage-source leaf whose lifetime is not the constant one | GV, JN |
| MM-40 | **withdrawn with Q42 (a) (§19.29.1):** there is no trusted-day predicate left to apply | — |
| MM-41 | a commit carrying an Add or a restore swap may change `committers`, or the settle does not void invites whose `committers_digest` no longer matches (S0-15) | GV, JN (the joiner of such a commit refuses its Welcome) |
| MM-42 | §5.7's deferral order separates a Remove from the drop of its key (round 10c's order; S0-16) | GV (the committed commit is invalid everywhere) |
| MM-43 | a batched `receive` keeps a failed item's partial state (no per-item rollback; S0-17) | RO-C (a replay in the middle of a batch), reload oracle |
| MM-44 | the committers anchor checked only after `JoinBuilder::build()` validates the whole tree (§19.27 TR-7) | JN pre-check-before-build (a forged Welcome refused before any tree validation; the measured pre-check cost) |
| MM-45 | the committers digest over the ordered list, not the sorted set (§19.27 TR-6) | `ch_derive.txt` (a transfer of rank 0 must not change it; a committer add/remove must) |
| MM-46 | (retargeted §19.29.1) a `LifetimeError`-refused commit treated as anything but invalid — held as a candidate, ranked, merged, counted in CO-3, pending, or blocking a settle or a pass | SCN-CM (a member's commit carrying an expired finite-lifetime KeyPackage: invalid everywhere, the owner's next pass proceeds), EM-3, the E-69/E-70 probes' cases in Rust |
| MM-47 | **withdrawn (§19.29.2):** it tested round 10d's claim that a captured joiner reaches `reinvite_needed`, which an active forger defeats by design (E-71); the test obligation of Q49's answer replaces it (SCN-CL joiner-capture variants with a silent and an active forger; the report states the capture's duration) | — |
| MM-PW1 | the invite key derived without the password in a password channel (§19.28) | the leaked-link oracle (the link opens without the password) |
| MM-PW2 | the committer skips the `pw_version` check, or applies it in an open channel (§19.28.4, corrected §19.29.3) | JN password cases (a stale-`pw_version` invite admitted; an open invite refused after the password is removed) |
| MM-PW3 | (restated §19.29.3) a `kdf_code` profile below OWASP's floor pairs accepted by the codec | the profile-table assertion (every `kdf_code` passes a pair; a table entry below every pair fails the build; E-73) |
| MM-PW4 | (restated §19.29.3) the link length differs between open and password invites or between names, or a password channel is distinguishable at relays | the size-equality test (E-73); NI-CH5 |
| MM-PW5 | the password or the Argon2id output persisted at rest (§19.29.3) | a T25-style physical scan of the database and the WAL after an issue and a join (T25 row of §11.2; not T3, which scans process outputs) |
| PC-1 | raw MLS stored (no seal) | JC-2 |
| PC-2 | one seal key per channel instead of per epoch | joiner oracle, T38 oracle |
| PC-3 | bucket by type | JC-4, NI-CH2 |
| PC-4 | members store with token-bought capabilities | JC-3 |
| PC-5 | a committer's pass triggered by a received request | NI-CH1, JC-6 |

| PC-7 | namespace derived from `channel_id` | JC-1 |
| PC-8 | commit TTL ≠ post TTL in a 7-day channel | JC-4 |
| PC-9 | reveal names its recipient in content | RV |
| PC-10 | registration capability used for list or store | JC-3 |
| PC-11 | bootstrap readable under `JK_g` | T38 oracle (join-traffic variant) |
| PC-12 | second-level time in content or lifetimes | T13, T33 scan |
| PC-13 | `order` outside the AEAD | JC-2 |
| PC-14 | no removal hold (posts encrypted in e while a removing candidate is held) | T38 hold variant |
| PC-15 | namespace switch at the settle (fetch-time) instead of the switch hour, or the removed member stops listening at its merge | NI-CH4 |
| PC-16 | a seal, recognition or join key derived from a value the relays are shown (a READ or DEPOSIT secret, a verifier, a credential) (§19.20) | JC-7 |
| PC-17 | (redefined by S0-9) the joiner uses the bootstrap before `JoinBuilder::build()` verified the GroupInfo, or from a Welcome that failed the committers anchor | JN re-sealed-bootstrap and FAKE_WELCOME cases; T38 join-traffic variant |
| PC-18 | box keys derived from the pseudonym seed instead of drawn per leaf (§19.17) | RV forward-secrecy case (after a leaf refresh and the old epoch's retention, M's seed and state open no earlier REVEAL) |
| PC-19 | the joiner applies a Welcome without the committers anchor (S0-15) | JN FAKE_WELCOME (the forged group's Welcome is applied and the real one then fails) |
| ME-C1 | reactions not cascaded | EP-C |
| ME-C2 | unread deadline from content, not relay expiry | EP-C |
| ME-C3 | no checkpoint after a cascade | T25 physical scan |

| ME-C6 | `ch_epoch.anchor_minute` kept after the epoch is final (§19.21) | the schema trigger test; T33 introspection |

### 12.10 Fuzzing

`mls-fuzz` (weekly and on dispatch, with a budget counted in **target-hours**, not nights — at least 42 target-hours in total before the exit, e.g. seven 6-hour runs, §15 item 8, S0-14; dated pinned nightly toolchain, `client-core/mls/fuzz` outside the workspace with its own lock checked by cargo-deny): **new work** — the `dm-fuzz` arrangement F9 planned was never built (STATUS, Phase 9 row: "vendor-tests și fuzzing-ul neconstruite"), so S11 builds the arrangement once, generic over the DM and MLS crates (§19.10, F-4). Targets `open` (every mode), `contentDecode`, `inviteParse`, `joinVerify`, `receive` on arbitrary MLS bytes after a valid seal (the pre-authentication decoders of GHSA-rrmv and GHSA-w62v, reachable only by seal-key holders), `welcomeJoin`. OpenMLS's own decode targets run at the pinned tag in `mls-vendor-tests`.

### 12.11 Emulator and devices

`dm-emulator.yml` gains a channel smoke test: the release variant loads `libghost_client_net.so`, creates a channel, admits a second in-process client, exchanges posts, removes it; the host/Android equivalence test (F9 §15.9) gains deterministic MLS cases. **It has never run on the runner (no KVM; STATUS:18)**; the local Windows emulator run of F9 §22.32 is the fallback. A device checklist step (2 devices, one channel, add, post, remove, rejoin) joins F9 §15.10 and stays manual. **arm64 (added §19.23):** OpenMLS and the libcrux provider have never run on arm64 in GHOST — upstream builds Android targets without testing them (`om:README.md:34-47`), and the emulator image is x86_64 — so the device step on **two arm64 phones** is a named owner action before any distribution (§15 item 13, Q20); it is not a Phase 10 gate item, and no arm64 claim is made until it runs. **S0 status (V10-10, S0-11):** the stack ran on Android's bionic libc for the first time, on the x86_64 API 29 emulator (`device_probe`: a 3-member cycle with equal epoch authenticators; 20- and 100-member timings); the arm64 `device_probe` was built but not run (no arm64 device on this host). S2 builds its successor over `ghost-client-mls`; **if an arm64 phone is available at S2's exit, the owner runs it then** (Q20), so Q18's cache decision has a device figure before S3–S7 build on value semantics.

### 12.12 The exit-gate argument, and what it does not prove

| Claim | JVM (every PR; the gates on dispatch and weekly) | Emulator | Devices | Staging / external |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| add, remove, resync with 20 clients | SCN-M20, EM-3, crash enumeration | smoke | checklist (2 devices) | — |
| removed member decrypts no new epoch | T38 oracle | — | — | — |
| T25 in channels | EP-C, physical scans | T25 smoke | checklist | — |
| relay blindness (T36, T37, T40) | privacy world | — | — | **staging capture not run** (the F9 O1 runbook) |
| real SQLCipher, Keystore, Tor | — | partly (loopback relays) | checklist | — |
| performance on low-end phones | host timings, loaded host, medians (V10-5, V10-10): at 100 members a commit 22–36 ms, a settle 13 ms, a post 4–6 ms with 2 retained epochs, a received post 92 ms and a trial settle 300 ms with 64 (hence the cap of 16 and the batched calls, S0-6, S0-17); a 15–25× factor for low-end phones is an estimate | x86_64 emulator, API 29: at 100 members a commit 31 ms, a settle 25 ms, a post 8 ms | **not measured** (the arm64 probe is built, S0-11) | — |
| the composition's security | tests, EM-3 | — | — | **external review: not done; a release gate** |

| **G-M8** `mls-vendored-clock-log.sh` (the G-R8 method) | clock, logging and network sites in the compiled code of the `ghost-client-mls` graph equal a committed, justified baseline (the OpenMLS clock sites of §3.6 among them) | T3, T33 |

Every workflow that builds a client graph calls both (`ci.yml`, `dm-emulator.yml`, `dm-privacy-gate.yml`, `mls-vendor-tests.yml`, the channel gates): the inline rule already existed in three workflows with three package lists, and one script avoids five diverging copies; a negative fixture (a target directory first built without the variables) is in `self-test.sh`. `libcrux-aes` is the only provider crate with a SIMD build script (V10-7). **`libcrux-aes` joins the rule and every clean list (§19.23; confirmed by V10-7: simd256 → still simd256 after the variables changed → portable after a clean → portable):** the provider's `libcrux-aead` 0.0.9 is used with default features, which include `aesgcm128` and `aesgcm256` and so compile `libcrux-aes` 0.0.9, whose `build.rs` reads `LIBCRUX_DISABLE_SIMD128/256` and `LIBCRUX_ENABLE_SIMD128/256` without any `rerun-if-env-changed` line — the stale-cfg hazard F9 measured in V-6 for `libcrux-ml-kem`, `-sha3` and `-intrinsics`; the `cargo clean -p …` lines of `ci.yml:254`, `dm-emulator.yml:149,165,172` and `dm-privacy-gate.yml:114,197` gain `-p libcrux-aes`. **As built in S2a (a deviation recorded in §19.31 row 22):** the script checks (`--json`, `--target-dir`, `--with-aes`) and does not clean; the four-package clean list stays inline where each build runs | ADR-36 practice |

G-M4, G-M7, G-M8 and G-M9 are their MLS counterparts. **Decided by S0 (S0-18): each pair is built once, generic over the DM and MLS graphs** (a script parametrised by the graph and its baseline; the DM graph now, the MLS graph from S2). Phase 9 work in progress builds G-R3, G-R4, G-R7 and G-R8 on their own; S2 extends whichever version is on `main` rather than writing a second one (F-4).

| **`channel-privacy-gate.yml`** (new; dispatch and weekly, on another day than the exit gate) | the privacy world at gate scale |
| **`channel-state-models.yml`** (new; on pull requests that touch `client-core/mls` or the arbitration code, weekly and on dispatch) | EM-3 |
| **`mls-vendor-tests.yml`** (new; on pin changes and weekly, ≈ 15 min) | OpenMLS's test suite with its KAT vectors from a git checkout of the openmls repository at commit `3a3e35de3feeca8f6605143c464d5452ae584d43` (tag `openmls-v0.9.0`), verified by hash; the crates.io package has no `test_vectors/` (§12.2); `cargo test --locked -p openmls --lib --features test-utils,libcrux-provider,all-ciphersuites` — without `all-ciphersuites` the parametrised tests run suite 0x0001 only (`openmls_test/src/lib.rs:8-19`); with it, run in S0: 1 134 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored, of which suite 0x0003 with the libcrux provider 213 passed and 3 ignored (the ignored ones include upstream's own test of `create_group_info_with_extensions`); the GHSA watch of G-M1; `test-utils` only here and in `ghost-client-mls-kat` |
| **`mls-fuzz.yml`** (new; weekly and dispatch, a target-hour budget, §12.10) | §12.10 |
| `t2-exit-gate.yml` | §7.9 |
| `dm-emulator.yml` | §12.11 |

**Runner budget (Q20; revised §19.11).** The four runners live on the owner's laptop and run only while he is logged in (`docs/ci-self-hosted.md` §1, §3). The nightly load already scheduled, by job timeout: `dm-emulator` 150 min, `dm-privacy-gate` 300 + 90, `dm-state-models` 60, `entitlement-exit-gate` 12 shards × 120, `messaging-exit-gate` 480, `monero-regtest` 45, `signer-timing` 60, `t2-exit-gate` a 10-pair matrix × 240 (+120), whose pair 0 has already hit its timeout four times (all on 2026-09-20: one scheduled and three push-triggered runs, confirmed by V10-14's crawl) (`.github/workflows/*.yml`, `cron` and `timeout-minutes`; `pq-model` and `pq-bump` run on push, not nightly). Phase 10 adds three gates (one of them a 60-client, 56-day world with real OpenMLS), fuzzing and weekly vendor tests. **Measured by S0 (V10-14, S0-14; every job of every trigger on the four runners, 2026-09-17 … 09-25, crawled after the nights ended: `mls-spike/scripts/v10-14-crawl.sh` and `v10-14-aggregate.awk`):**
- On days without pushes to `main` (09-17, 09-18) the scheduled workflows used **19–21 %** of the four runners' time. On development days (09-20, 09-21, 09-23, 09-24) the runners were busy **87–97 %** of the day, and **54–67 %** of that was push-triggered: on every push to `main`, `t2-exit-gate` runs pair 0 and its mutants (2–4 h each; 6 983 runner-minutes over the period), `entitlement-exit-gate` its 12 shards (4 177), `ci` (3 213) and `messaging-exit-gate` (2 418); 38 pushes in ten days, 5 of them documents only.
- Scheduled runs are created **275–340 minutes after their cron times** (07:18–07:56 UTC, the owner's morning), so 89 % of the scheduled runner-minutes fell between 07:00 and 20:00 UTC and 1.2 % between 02:00 and 07:00: the "nightly" gates run in the day, next to the development pushes. The long waits of the first crawl (19–23 h) are scheduled jobs queued **behind push-triggered jobs**, not an idle laptop: in nine days a job waited with no runner busy for one minute, and all four runners were busy 8 052 of 12 960 minutes.
- The scheduled work alone: 1 111–2 735 runner-minutes a day (09-24: 40 jobs, 2 735 minutes, first run created to last job ended 21.9 h, longest queue wait 1 226 minutes), `t2-exit-gate` 61–92 % of it. `dm-privacy-gate`'s T32 world jobs ran 28–43 minutes each on 09-25 (they had failed within 2 minutes before), so its designed load (10 × 300 + 90 minutes by timeout) is arriving; `dm-emulator` still fails at once (no KVM).

**Therefore (normative):** `channel-exit-gate` and `channel-privacy-gate` run **on dispatch, at hours the owner chooses when the laptop is otherwise idle, and weekly** (on different days); `channel-state-models` (EM-3) on pull requests that touch `client-core/mls` or the arbitration code, weekly and on dispatch; `mls-fuzz` weekly and on dispatch against a **target-hour budget** (§12.10); `mls-vendor-tests` weekly and on pin changes (≈ 15 min); **no channel gate runs on every push**. A green gate at the pinned seed count therefore takes weeks unless push-triggered capacity is freed; how is Q20 (re-asked with the options: keep the push triggers of the existing gates, path-filter them, move `t2-exit-gate` and the entitlement shards to dispatch and weekly with a light smoke on push, or rotate T2's seed pairs). The laptop's availability is a declared dependency of every gate claim (R-7).

**Effort, stated in two parts (revised §19.11).**

*Implementation.* F9's observed pace is the only calibration there is: the code of its slices landed in about seven active days (2026-09-16 and 2026-09-19 … 09-24, `git log`) against an estimate of 10–12 weeks, while about two thirds of its registered mutants (30 of 97 run), its fuzzing, its vendor tests, its seeded randomness and its HIGH mode stayed unbuilt. That pace is therefore a lower bound for writing code and says nothing about closing a gate. At that pace this phase's slices sum to **about 40.75 working days** (the column above: round 10c's 34.5, which added about 2.5 days in S4, S6, S7 and S9 for the corrections of §19.13 … §19.22, plus **6.25 days for the S0 corrections**, §19.26: S2 +3.25 — the stub and GHSA watch, the size re-measure, the split rows and the cap, G-R2's getrandom clause, the extracted portable script, the probe's successor, batched calls; S4 +0.75 — canonical admission checks, the committers anchor, batch receive, less the withdrawn `K_boot`; S5 +1.75 — canonical leaves, the lifetime predicate and its EM-3 adversaries, the anchor's CO-2 rule and voiding, the deferral order, `trialCount`; S10 and S11 +0.25 each — the dispatch-and-weekly schedules), **plus 3.0 days for rounds 10d and 10e** (§19.27 … §19.29): S2 +1.0 (the `ChannelInviteV2` codec, the seal, Argon2id in Rust under Q47 (a) and the RFC 9106 vector, and the Argon2id time and memory measured on the emulator and on a phone at S2's exit), S3 +0.25 (the password columns of migration 7), S4 +1.0 (the `pw_version` policy field and its conditional check, `INVITE_ISSUED` TLV 0x0015, the voiding at settle and the rotation on a password change, the leaked-link and password tests, the anchor pre-check and the rejoin rule, the set digest), S5 ±0 (round 10d's +0.5 for the refusal-branch rule is replaced by "a lifetime refusal is invalid" and the insider adversary, +0.25, and the removal of S0-12's lifetime-pending machinery, −0.25, is netted now that Q42 (a) is the baseline), S9/S11 +0.75 (the password mutants, the fuzz target, the NI-CH5 twin and the password scan) — **43.75 working days in all**, roughly 8–9 weeks — at the upper end of the plan's 6–9 weeks for implementation only. S5, S2 and the harness debts (S11's since the S1a re-scoping, §19.33) carry most of the uncertainty. Q49's and Q50's options are not costed until the owner chooses. **Not included:** the channel-media slice (§5.10), which needs Phase 11's engine and is sized when Q38 is answered.

*Closure blockers (calendar, not effort).* (1) **CI restored** — the artifact quota and a KVM runner or the local emulator fallback; owner (Q20); needed by the S10 and S11 exits, not before. (2) **Gate capacity** — measured by V10-14 (§13.2): the four runners are saturated on development days, mostly by push-triggered gates, and scheduled runs start in the owner's morning; the channel gates run on dispatch and weekly, so a green gate at the pinned seed count takes **weeks**, not nights, unless Q20's options free push-triggered capacity; the owner's laptop hours bound it (R-7). (3) **The fuzz budget** — at least 42 target-hours after S11's arrangement lands, at 6 hours a week about seven weeks unless dispatched more often (§15 item 8). (4) **Owner decisions** Q1–Q41 before S0's freeze, among them the ADRs this phase builds on (ADR-25, ADR-30′ … ADR-39; Q39) — answered on 2026-09-27 with the defaults — and **Q42 … Q50** of rounds 10d and 10e (§19.27 … §19.29), open: Q42 before S2 (the canonical KeyPackage), Q43 … Q48 and Q50 before S2 and S4 (the password), Q49 before S7. (5) **Phase 9's open gate** (R-8): defects found under channels are fixed in Phase 9 code; whether Phase 10 may close first is Q39. (5a) **Two arm64 phones** for the device step, before distribution (§12.11). (6) The external review stays a release gate, outside this phase. The calendar to the exit gate is therefore implementation plus **several weeks of dispatched and weekly gate runs and the fuzz budget** once (1) is done (at the measured load, more like seven weeks than two), and it is not shortened by Q3 or Q15: Q3 saves about one working day and Q15's default is already assumed in the column; Q20's options on push triggers change CI calendar time, not effort. Phase 9's record says a design's test counts slip; §12 pins none before S10 and S11 (S0-18).

## 15. Exit-gate checklist (all on the closing commit)

1. SCN-M20 green in `channel-exit-gate` with the pinned seeds and the full crash enumeration (plan clause 1, FR-2.2, FR-3.2, FR-3.5).
2. The removed-member oracle opens 0 in every variant, including the device-swap stack (later epochs and later generations: a restore rotates) and the removal-hold variant (plan clause 2, TM S4, LIMITE:39); the posts it opens under the declared L8-C18 residue are reported, not hidden.
3. The channel T25 cases green, including the physical scans (plan clause 3).
4. EM-3 green with the §19.1 and §19.13 adversaries; T41 in SCN-M20 and its owner-restore variant; SCN-CL (with the silent-orphan variant) and SCN-CM green with their declared bounds.
5. The privacy world at gate scale: JC-1 … JC-7 as expected, NI-CH1 … NI-CH4 equal up to their declared differences (T36, T37, T40), on the harness of §19.10.
6. Every registered mutant detected; `ch-report-check.sh` accepts the reports.
7. The WG test vectors pass in `mls-vendor-tests` (the openmls checkout at `3a3e35de…`, with `all-ciphersuites`); the passive-client replay through `GhostKv` passes on the 43 vectors (252 epochs) of the provider's suites, and the report names the 105 vectors of other suites as not applicable (spec Appendix A; §12.2, S0-13).
8. Fuzzing ran at least 42 target-hours in total (for example seven 6-hour runs, weekly or dispatched; S0-14) with no open crash.
9. Gates G-M1 … G-M9 green with fixtures (G-M7 with the golden sizes); `cargo deny` green and the GHSA watch clean; notices and MPL sources updated; the library within the size budget on both ABIs (§3.8).
10. The emulator smoke test ran (on the runner, or the local fallback recorded); the device step recorded or recorded as not executed.
10a. The harness features the gate lines rely on (§19.10: seeded native randomness or record/replay, the redeem lane, send delays and HIGH mode, circuit timing) are built, or each report line names what it did not compare.
11. Appendix B applied to INVARIANTS, THREAT_MODEL, LIMITE, the plan, STATUS and the ADR index; ADR-40 … ADR-43 filed as "propus".
12. The phase's adversarial review done and its findings corrected or recorded.
12a. **FR-3.4's media half** ("media up to 50 MiB", "boundary-size, invalid-media … tests") is either met by the channel-media slice or recorded as not met, according to Q38; the closing report does not claim FR-3.4 in full otherwise.
13. **Not required for the gate, required before distribution:** the external audit of OpenMLS as configured and of the GHOST composition (plan row 15), staging captures (T1), device performance, and the device step on two arm64 phones (§12.11; OpenMLS and libcrux have never run on arm64 in GHOST).

## 16. Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood / impact | Mitigation | Residue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | Commit arbitration has a subtle bug (divergence, lost admissions, liveness); the review rounds found such defects in round 10a (§19.1, §19.2, §19.5) and more in round 10b's text (a stranded committer, an owner that could neither leave nor be restored, moderators racing the owner, §19.13) | medium / high | EM-3 exhaustive model with adversarial members and relays, FORK, WITHHOLD_COMMIT, LATE_COMMIT, GRIND, FLOOD and the §19.13 cases, reload oracle, `oracle_epoch` at every epoch, MM-2 … MM-4, MM-16, MM-19 … MM-28, MM-31 … MM-34 | forks under AD-1 partitions remain possible by design: detected when the branches' traffic meets, bounded and flagged when it does not (L8-C17, L8-C21) |
| R-2 | OpenMLS's serde layout changes at a bump (the day rewrite, the per-epoch rows, the storage format); a bump changes an encoding the canonical forms and golden sizes pin; canonical leaves freeze the KeyPackage and leaf shape for a channel's life (S0-4) | medium / medium | exact pins; G-M7 golden states, golden sizes and upgrade tests; `STATE_CORRUPT` instead of silent defaults | a bump costs a migration drill; a new capability or extension is a flag day or a new channel (§3.8) |
| R-3 | Value semantics cost on low-end phones: every call parses the channel's whole state — measured (V10-5, S0-6): 47 KB at 32 members and 134 KB at 100 with no past epoch, and about 18.5 / 53 KB more per retained past epoch; medians on a loaded host at 100 members: a received post 2.6 ms with 2 retained epochs, 16.5 ms with 8, 92 ms with 64; a trial settle 13 / 40 / 300 ms | medium / medium | the cap of 16 past epochs; per-epoch rows (I/O); batched `receive` and `trialCount` (one load per batch, S0-17); the cache seam and Q18's version-checked cache; the device step (§12.11) | unmeasured on devices until the device step; a 15–25× factor is an estimate |
| R-4 | APK size: **measured +2 488 200 B (arm64-v8a) and +2 916 384 B (x86_64)** in the shipped library (V10-3, S0-3) | certain / low on arm64-v8a (21 % headroom); **medium on x86_64** (7.3 % headroom, and the probe lacks most of `ghost-client-mls`'s own code) | budget ≤ +3 MiB per ABI, enforced per ABI; S2's exit re-measures with the real crate; Q16's alternative would remove 0.42 / 0.53 MB of unused KEM code | NFR-4 (< 80 MiB) not at risk by itself |
| R-5 | Token budget and funding stalls (rotations cost 3 tokens; funders absent) | medium / medium | ≥ 2 funders recommended in the Phase 13 UI; registrations tolerate 2–3 weeks; stores wait, never fail silently | L8-C6 |
| R-6 | Pre-authentication decoding bugs in OpenMLS | low / medium | the seal limits who can reach the decoders to seal-key holders; `catch_unwind`; `mls-fuzz`; exact pins with the **GHSA watch** of G-M1 (OpenMLS's advisories are not in RustSec, V10-2) | members and invite holders can still reach them |
| R-7 | CI capacity: gates that never run (Phase 9's lesson); four runners on the owner's laptop, only while he is logged in | **certain** / high (V10-14: saturated on development days, mostly by push-triggered gates; scheduled runs start in the owner's morning) | channel gates on dispatch and weekly, never on push (S0-14); CI restoration required at the S10/S11 exits (Q20); Q20's options for the existing push triggers; report checks refuse unmeasured claims | a green channel gate at the pinned seed count takes weeks, not nights; the exit calendar depends on the laptop's hours |
| R-8 | Phase 9's infrastructure is not closed; channel work may expose its defects | high / medium | S0 re-ran the Phase 9 suites the channel engine depends on (2026-09-26, S0-18: the JVM unit tests of `:storage`, `:sync`, `:crypto-bridge` and `:messaging` with the `ghost-dm-host` library, 865 tests, all passed; evidence §15); defects found later are fixed in Phase 9's code with their own tests | schedule; Phase 9's exit gate stays open (Q39) |
| R-9 | Upstream churn (`ExportedSecret<T>` on `main`; Android "built but not tested" upstream) | medium / low | pins; the emulator equivalence test (x86_64); the device step on two arm64 phones before distribution (§12.11); upstream issues filed (F-5) | arm64 unexercised until the owner runs the device step |
| R-10 | The classical suite: channels exposed to harvest-now-decrypt-later; a later PQ migration needs new channels | certain / medium | declared (L8-C1, §4.5); suite-agile invites; triggers in §4.4 | owner decision Q2 |
| R-11 | Expectations of ADR-13's full governance in Phase 10 | medium / low | D17 and Q15 state the split | — |
| R-12 | GHOST uses OpenMLS in ways its examples do not (no member proposals, by-value adds, `swap_members` for rejoin, the pinned-schema day rewrite) | medium / medium | each is public API except the day rewrite (recorded deviation); golden tests; the external audit scope names them | — |
| R-13 | Loss of the owner's seed, or an owner who vanishes, freezes the policy (committer list) and leaves a leaf nobody can evict (§19.13) | low / medium | resignation and transfer let an owner hand over before leaving; a restored owner keeps rank 0 (any-rank swaps); moderators keep membership moving; the UI (Phase 13) urges naming a moderator | L8-C16; succession of an absent owner is Q37 |
| R-14 | OpenMLS judges KeyPackage lifetimes on the device's system clock, not GHOST's trusted clock, and its refusal masks every other defect of the commit (§19.4; V10-11) | **closed by Q42 (a)** (§19.29.1): with the constant lifetime a refusal means a non-canonical KeyPackage and an invalid commit for every member (E-69, E-70); open again under Q42 (b), where no convergent rule is known (a stall any member can cause, or forks of honest members) | the constant lifetime in every KeyPackage and in the creator's leaf; "a lifetime refusal is invalid" in CO-2; the constant form checked by GHOST (OpenMLS never checks the span); G-M7 pins OpenMLS's acceptance at every bump (`has_acceptable_range`, uncalled at 0.9.0); an upstream request for an injectable time source (F-5) | an OpenMLS version that wires in `has_acceptable_range` refuses every GHOST KeyPackage until GHOST adapts (caught by G-M7) |
| R-15 | The Phase 9 harness debts (seeded randomness, redeem lane, HIGH mode, circuit timing) are larger than sized, or V10-6 fails | medium / medium | sized in S2 and S11 (§14; S1's share moved to S11 with the S1a re-scoping, §19.33); record/replay as the fallback; report lines name what they compared (§15 item 10a) | privacy and crash claims narrower until built |
| R-16 | The fork rules (successor counts, branch death, split flags) confuse users or are gamed by colluding members | medium / medium | EM-3 adversaries; `split_suspected` with a fingerprint rather than silent moves; Q27 lets the owner prefer the RFC 9750 copy-based option | L8-C21 |
| R-17 | More rotations (invite expiry, 28-day age, restores) raise token use and read pairs | medium / low | at most one non-removal rotation per 14 days and never during a drain; at most two generations listened; 9 channels per client (Q4, Q28) | +3 tokens per rotation for the rotating committer; the read-pair cap can be passed during the six weeks after a slot-table change (L8-C24) |
| R-18 | Channel media (spec FR-3.4, P0) depends on Phase 11's engine, and the two designs had each left it to the other (C-17) | high / medium | §5.10 reserves the contract in Phase 10's formats now; Q38 decides whether Phase 10's exit waits; §15 item 12a forbids claiming FR-3.4 in full otherwise | the channel media namespace, its read pairs and quota are open until the slice |
| R-19 | A single-proposal commit exceeds the small class's 4 KiB floor at 32 members | **closed by measurement with canonical KeyPackages** (V10-4, §5.7: every single-proposal commit fits in 32-, 64- and 128-leaf trees; tightest margin 172 B); with round 10c's 1 024-byte KeyPackage bound an Add exceeds it from 27 members (26 in a 128-leaf tree) | canonical KeyPackages and leaves (S0-4, S0-5); §5.7 splits multi-proposal commits in the corrected order (S0-16); G-M7's golden sizes fail a bump that eats the margin; Q3 re-asked with both options | a future OpenMLS encoding change could eat the 172 B, caught by G-M7 |
| R-20 | A forged Welcome captures a joiner: a holder of the generation's join key builds a group under the channel's id and Welcomes the joiner's KeyPackage; OpenMLS verifies the GroupInfo under the forger's own leaf (V10-8, measured) | **closed for non-committers** by the committers anchor (S0-15) | the invite's committers digest; the joiner's check before writing, on the unverified GroupInfo; §5.3's validity of the joined context; FAKE_WELCOME, PC-19 | a committer named at the invite's issue (or among a rejoiner's last committers), even one removed since, can capture the joiner in a fork of the real group and keep it captured for as long as it sustains the fork — measured, E-71; only an out-of-band comparison of the fingerprint shows it, and no in-band recovery exists (L8-C25, Q41, Q49, §19.29.2) |
| R-21 | Argon2id at join on a low-end phone is too slow or runs out of memory (§19.28.5, §19.29.3) | medium / medium | measured on the x86_64 emulator: native `argon2` 125–128 ms, BouncyCastle on ART 197–269 ms (the review: 87 vs 250 ms), BouncyCastle needing more than 64 MiB of Java heap (OutOfMemoryError at -Xmx64m on ART, -Xmx72m on HotSpot) against 96 MiB of app heap on AOSP's 1 GB profile; Q47 (a) runs it natively, off the Java heap; S2's exit measures time and memory on the emulator and on a phone; a lighter profile is a new `kdf_code` | ≈ 1.3–3.2 s natively and ≈ 3–7 s with BouncyCastle on a low-end phone by the ×15–25 estimate over the two emulator runs (not measured on a device) |
