## 17. Owner questions (the proposed default applies unless the owner objects)

**S0 status (2026-09-26; S0-18).** The S0 exit requires every question frozen or re-asked. **Re-asked with S0's evidence:** Q2 (measured PQ sizes; default unchanged), **Q3** (both options measured), **Q16** (the unused KEM code and the x86_64 headroom), **Q18** (the measured cost of value semantics; batched calls), **Q20** (the measured load by trigger), **Q22** (the thin 0x0003 coverage of the WG vectors), **Q26** (the actor time of per-call reloads). **New (S0):** **Q40** (the lock-resolution stub), **Q41** (the committers anchor). **Frozen with their defaults** (no S0 evidence bears on them): Q1, Q4 … Q15, Q17, Q19, Q21, Q23 … Q25, Q27 … Q39.

**Owner decision (2026-09-28).** The owner approved Q42 … Q50 with their proposed defaults (constant KeyPackage lifetime; the password-sealed invite with only committers inviting in a password channel; the app-generated 5-word passphrase and Argon2id at 64 MiB / t=1 / p=4, re-measured on a device; rotation on a password change; Argon2id in Rust; rejoin without the password, restore with it; Q49 declared; nothing of the link or the password kept on the sponsor's device).

**Round 10d status (2026-09-28; §19.27, §19.28).** The targeted protocol review of S0-12 and S0-15 (§19.27) confirmed seven findings against OpenMLS 0.9.0 (probes E-61 … E-64) and added **Q42** (the constant KeyPackage lifetime, which removes the device-clock stall); the corrections to the anchor (set digest, pre-check-before-build) and the lifetime refusal branch are folded normatively. The owner's channel password (§19.28) is designed as a password-sealed invite (Argon2id, probes E-65 … E-68) and adds **Q43 … Q48**.

**Round 10e status (2026-09-28; §19.29).** The adversarial review of round 10d (27 findings, every one re-run, E-69 … E-76) **re-asks Q42 … Q48** — round 10d's lifetime refusal rule forked honest members and let any member freeze a channel, so Q42's constant lifetime is the normative baseline and the finite option is re-asked with its cost; the password does not bind insiders, its checks and formats are corrected, and Q47's proposed default moves to Rust — and **adds Q49** (a removed committer's capture of an invitee: declared, and how a captured member returns) and **Q50** (what the sponsor's device keeps of a password invite). Q42 … Q50 are open; their proposed defaults are what §19.27 … §19.29 write as normative.

| # | Question | Proposed default | Pe scurt (RO) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Approve ADR-40 … ADR-43 (Appendix A) as "propus", and this design as the basis for Phase 10 code? | **yes** | Aprobarea ADR-40 … ADR-43 și a designului |
| Q2 | Ciphersuite: classical 0x0003 for every P0 channel, with no PQ claim for channels and the §4.4 triggers; or the X-Wing suite 0x004D now (experimental code point; **measured with GHOST's extensions (V10-9): 64 KiB blobs from 20 members, a cap near 38–46 members**, outside the audit, not migratable in place; 0x004F "works" only because OpenMLS maps it to the same X-Wing KEM and the provider does not check the KEM)? | **0x0003** | Canalele folosesc suita clasică; suita post-cuantică doar când e stabilă și revizuită |
| Q3 | **Re-asked (V10-4, S0-4).** Two size classes (small ≤ 32 members with 4 KiB blobs; large ≤ 100 with 16 KiB blobs) — and how the small class fits its floor: **(a)** every KeyPackage and leaf in one **canonical form** (395 bytes; every single-proposal commit then fits at 32 members in every tree shape, tightest margin 172 B), at the price of no GREASE and no extensibility inside a channel (a new capability is a flag day or a new channel, §3.8); or **(b)** keep round 10c's bound of 1 024 bytes per KeyPackage with at most one leaf extension and **lower the small class to 26 members** (an Add of such a KeyPackage exceeds C(4096) from 27 members in a 32-leaf tree and from 26 in a 128-leaf one), which still needs S0-5's canonical update-path leaves (inflated self-updates made 100-member Welcomes unsendable) and so buys little extensibility; or **(c)** only the small class in Phase 10 (saves about one working day, §14)? | **both classes, (a) canonical KeyPackages** | Două clase: până la 32 de membri (4 KiB) și până la 100 (16 KiB); pachetele de chei au o singură formă fixă (395 de octeți), altfel clasa mică ar coborî la 26 de membri |
| Q4 | At most **9** channels per client (revised §19.8: 6 read pairs per channel while its old generation drains must fit the Phase 7 budget of 64 pairs, or a co-member who leaves every shared channel slows the DM inbox), beyond which the app refuses to join? **Declared (§19.23):** in the six weeks after a slot-table change the union relay sets can double the pairs, so the bound can be passed then (L8-C24); channel media with its own namespace would lower the limit to about 5 (§5.10, Q38). | **9** | Cel mult 9 canale pe dispozitiv (altfel un membru ostil poate încetini citirea DM-urilor) |
| Q5 | Invites as single-use bearer links or QR codes that carry the generation's namespace and access secrets, rather than invitations only through DMs? **Corrected (§19.7):** admission is automatic, so whoever presents a leaked unused invite first **becomes a member** and reads every later post until removed; every holder also sees the channel's traffic volume and can fill its quota until the next rotation (L8-C4). | **links and QR** (sendable inside a GHOST DM; QR in person recommended) | Invitația e un link sau QR de unică folosință; cine o folosește primul intră în canal — o invitație scursă înseamnă un membru nedorit |
| Q6 | A new namespace and new access secrets at every removal (3 tokens for the removing committer), so a removed member sees nothing more at the relays; or keep the namespace and declare that a removed member keeps seeing volume and timing? | **rotate** | La fiecare scoatere canalul trece pe o adresă nouă; cel scos nu mai vede nimic |
| Q7 | Funders: the owner by default, up to 3 named members; each pays 3 tokens per week per channel; non-funders pay nothing? **Declared (§19.9, LC-8):** under AD-1 the weekly redemptions single out the funders' clients, so the default makes the owner's client identifiable to the relays as the channel's funder; naming a non-owner funder moves that mark to the funder (the owner stays marked at the rotations it commits, Q31). | **yes** (the owner funds) | Proprietarul plătește releele canalului (3 token-uri pe săptămână); releele pot astfel recunoaște clientul care plătește |
| Q8 | Committers commit at scheduled passes only when work is pending (plus an empty weekly commit), declaring that relays can infer "a request was pending" (LC-6); or commit at every pass (more epochs, more storage writes, no LC-6)? | **when pending** | Moderatorul confirmă cererile la momente programate, nu imediat ce sosesc |
| Q9 | Members refresh their keys every 28 days; members idle 63 days are removed? | **yes** | Cheile membrilor se reîmprospătează la 28 de zile; cine lipsește 63 de zile e scos |
| Q10 | A 20-minute settle window before a membership change takes effect, anchored at the earlier of the member's own sighting and the relay's store hour (so a returning member catches up at once, §19.1)? | **20 min** | O schimbare de membri intră în vigoare după 20 de minute; cine revine după o absență se sincronizează imediat |
| Q11 | History policy "none" only (joiners see no content from before they joined; they do see earlier join requests of their generation, the rotation count and the epoch number, §8.4), no history transfer until a later ADR? | **yes** | Membrii noi nu văd conținutul de dinaintea intrării lor (văd doar cererile de intrare recente și numărul de rotații) |
| Q12 | Channels with timers ≤ 1 day: commits, join traffic and the administrative posts (`INVITE_ISSUED`, `LEAVE`, §19.21) live 7 days on relays (relays can tell them from posts there), or the whole channel at 7 days, or no timers below 7 days for channels? | **commits 7 days** | În canalele cu timer scurt, mesajele de administrare stau 7 zile pe relee |
| Q13 | One pseudonym per channel for life (a removed and re-added member returns with the same pseudonym; THREAT_MODEL:81 corrected)? | **yes** | Același pseudonim în același canal, pentru totdeauna |
| Q14 | Reveal to one member by encryption to its channel box key (fresh per leaf refresh, so a later theft of the recipient's seed opens no earlier reveal, §19.17); other members see that you revealed to someone, not to whom? (What the recipient can prove to others is Q35.) | **yes** | Dezvăluirea identității e vizibilă doar celui ales; ceilalți văd doar că ai făcut o dezvăluire |
| Q15 | Governance in Phase 10 limited to roles, removal, leave, eviction, sponsor records and the S5 threshold simulation; flags, quarantine, strikes, exclusion lists and verified channels with the Phase 13 UI? | **yes** | Rapoartele, carantina și strike-urile vin în Faza 13 |
| Q16 | **Re-asked (V10-3).** Accept `hpke-rs` 0.7.0 (MPL-2.0, three crates, file-level copyleft) unmodified with published sources, or write a GHOST crypto provider on RustCrypto's `hpke`? Measured: the stack adds +2.49 MB (arm64-v8a) and +2.92 MB (x86_64), of which about 0.42 / 0.53 MB is KEM code suite 0x0003 never runs (P-256, ML-KEM-512/1024), linked because `hpke-rs-libcrux` dispatches over every KEM at run time; x86_64 keeps 7.3 % headroom under the +3 MiB budget. A GHOST provider would drop that code and the MPL crates, and add GHOST-written crypto glue to the audit scope. | **accept** (the budget holds; S2 re-measures) | Acceptăm `hpke-rs` (MPL-2.0) nemodificat, cu sursa publicată; costul măsurat e în buget |
| Q17 | No external commits and no published GroupInfo (joins only through a committer)? | **yes** | Intrarea în canal doar prin aprobarea unui moderator |
| Q18 | **Re-asked (V10-5, V10-10; S0-6, S0-17).** Past epochs kept 2 days (1 day in short-timer channels), **at most 16** (was 64), deleted by count (§8.3); posts encrypted only when the channel is current; and the storage adapter keeps **value semantics** (every call re-reads the channel's rows) with past epochs in rows of their own and **batched calls** (a receive pass and each trial settle is one native call, one load), or a version-checked **native state cache** now? Measured on a loaded host (medians of 9) at 100 members: a received post 2.6 ms with 2 retained epochs, 16.5 ms with 8, 92 ms with 64; a trial settle 13 / 40 / 300 ms; OpenMLS rewrites every retained epoch on each post (3.4 MB at 64; 5.5–5.7 KB with the per-epoch rows); a low-end phone is estimated 15–25 times slower (not measured). A cache would remove the per-call parse but adds a second copy of the state that must never diverge from SQLCipher (the reason for value semantics, D5). | **value semantics, per-epoch rows, cap 16, batched calls, a cache seam**; the cache is decided after the device step (§12.11; at S2's exit if a phone is available) | Cheile vechi se șterg după 2 zile (cel mult 16 epoci); fără cache nativ deocamdată: apelurile se grupează, iar decizia pe cache vine după măsurarea pe telefon |
| Q19 | Device loss: a restored device returns through a new invite and replaces its old leaf (a restore), **which rotates the channel's address so the lost device keeps no relay access** (+3 tokens for the committer, §19.16), whatever the member's rank; a running old device then shows "replaced"; a stolen seed can only be excluded by leaving the pseudonym and a new identity, and PCS does not heal against a seed thief (declared, L8-C9)? | **yes** | Un dispozitiv restaurat își reia locul printr-o invitație nouă, iar canalul își schimbă adresa ca dispozitivul pierdut să nu mai vadă nimic; un seed furat cere identitate nouă |
| Q20 | **Re-asked (V10-14, S0-14).** Owner actions: restore the CI artifact quota and provide a KVM runner (or accept the local emulator fallback) **before the S10 and S11 exits** (not before S0, §19.11); accept that gate claims depend on the laptop's hours; run the device step on two arm64 phones before any distribution, and the arm64 `device_probe` at S2's exit if a phone is at hand (§12.11). **And the capacity the channel gates need.** Measured (§13.2): on development days the four runners are 87–97 % busy, 54–67 % with push-triggered gates (`t2-exit-gate` pair 0 and mutants, the 12 entitlement shards, `messaging-exit-gate`, `ci`; 38 pushes in ten days), scheduled runs start in the owner's morning (cron + 4.6–5.7 h), and queued scheduled jobs wait up to 23 h behind push jobs. Options: **(a)** keep the existing push triggers; channel gates on dispatch and weekly (a green channel gate takes weeks); **(b)** move `t2-exit-gate` and the entitlement shards from every push to dispatch and weekly (or path-filter them), with a light smoke on push, freeing most of a development day for dispatched channel gates; **(c)** rotate T2's ten seed pairs (e.g. two per night) on top of (a) or (b). | **(a) now, with the owner deciding (b) and (c) as Phase 8's and Phase 9's gate owner; channel gates never on push** | Refacerea CI înainte de porțile S10/S11; porțile canalelor rulează la cerere și săptămânal; proprietarul decide dacă porțile existente mai rulează la fiecare push |
| Q21 | NFR-2 (p95 < 8 s) declared unmet by the 30-second listing interval, measured in Phase 12? | **yes** | Ținta de latență NFR-2 nu e atinsă cu programul actual; se măsoară în Faza 12 |
| Q22 | **Re-asked (V10-12, S0-13).** An mls-rs differential peer in tests? The WG passive-client vectors give GHOST's own suite 0x0003 only **21 vectors and 26 commit epochs** (252 epochs in all, 226 of them 0x0001), and they test the adapter, not GHOST's join path; OpenMLS's own suite at the tag with `all-ciphersuites` adds 213 passing tests at 0x0003 with the libcrux provider (run in S0). | **no** in Phase 10 (the vendor suite at 0x0003 and the WG vectors; revisit before the external audit) | Fără a doua implementare MLS în teste; acoperirea suitei 0x0003 vine din testele OpenMLS |
| Q23 | A formal model (ProVerif) of the channel layer before distribution? | **not in Phase 10**; the external review decides | Fără model formal al canalelor acum |
| Q24 | No public channels, no discovery, no content readable by non-members in Phase 10; any of them needs its own ADR? | **yes** | Fără canale publice sau descoperire |
| Q25 | **Revised (§19.13):** moderators handle requests and evictions the owner has left pending for `rank × 48 h` (48 h for the first moderator, 96 h for the second, …), remove at their own action at any time, and make their own weekly PCS commit; only the owner changes the policy (and transfers rank 0); any committer may resign? (Round 10b's rule counted 48 h without an owner *commit*, which in a quiet channel made the first moderator active about five days in seven and raced the owner routinely.) | **yes** | Moderatorii preiau cererile lăsate de proprietar 48 h; pot scoate oricând; doar proprietarul schimbă regulile și își poate preda rolul |
| Q26 | **Re-asked (V10-5).** One messaging actor for DMs and channels (one writer)? Channel calls parse the whole channel state each time (§3.4); with the batched calls of S0-17 a receive pass of 128 blobs is a handful of loads and a settle with 8 candidates 8 `trialCount` calls, but on a low-end phone a large channel's catch-up can still hold the actor for seconds, delaying DMs by as much. | **yes**, with batched calls; the device step measures the actor time | Un singur fir de lucru pentru DM și canale, cu apeluri grupate |
| Q27 | Fork policy (§19.1): a settled epoch is final and **sticky** (a later commit never moves a member); concurrent candidates are decided by successor senders, then rank and hash; a member leaves its branch only when it is dead (CO-11); live splits and relay partitions are flagged with a fingerprint for comparison out of band (CO-12). Or RFC 9750 §5.2.2's other option: merge at once and keep a copy of the previous group state for a few days so late commits can be judged — at the cost that a device seized in that window re-derives every later epoch, read posts included? | **sticky finality** | O epocă odată acceptată nu se mai schimbă; bifurcațiile se semnalează cu o amprentă de comparat în afara aplicației |
| Q28 | Rotate the channel's address and access secrets also when an invite of the generation expired unused, and when a generation is 28 days old (at most once per 14 days, never while an old address drains), so leaked or old invites stop giving metadata access and removals hide among routine rotations; cost +3 tokens per rotation for the committer (§19.7)? | **yes** | Canalul își schimbă adresa și după invitații expirate nefolosite și la 28 de zile, nu doar la scoateri |
| Q29 | Leave the channel name out of the invite link (the joiner sees it after admission; a leaked link does not name the channel), or carry it for a better accept screen (declared, L8-C4)? | **no name in the link** | Numele canalului nu apare în link-ul de invitație |
| Q30 | A sponsor-confirmed admission step (the sponsor sees the join request's key fingerprint and confirms it, compared with the invitee out of band, before a committer admits), which makes a leaked invite useless without the sponsor? | **not in P0**; flag bit 1 reserved for a per-channel choice in Phase 13 | Confirmarea de către sponsor a celui care intră: nu acum; bitul de politică rămâne rezervat |
| Q31 | Who funds a new generation after a rotation: the rotating committer at the switch hour of its commit (stores start at once; relays single out the committer, LC-8), or only the listed funders at their next renewal (stores wait up to a week; the committer stays unmarked unless it funds)? | **the rotating committer** | Cine plătește adresa nouă după o rotație: comitentul care a făcut-o (releele îl pot recunoaște) |
| Q32 | Split Welcomes into floor-sized fragments so they look like posts (hides the committer's Welcome store and the join count per blob), or keep one larger Welcome blob per joiner (declared LC-5)? | **not in P0**; Phase 11's blob fragmentation is the candidate | Welcome-urile rămân blob-uri mai mari (vizibile ca intrări) până în Faza 11 |
| Q33 | Rejoin after a fork or an expired log by a pseudonym-signed, fresh request admitted by a committer under the §6.7 rules (in-band, no new invite), or require every swap to be authorized by a new invite (restore only)? | **signed rejoin** | Reintrarea după o bifurcație se face cu o cerere semnată cu pseudonimul, fără invitație nouă |
| Q34 | The removal hold: while a commit that removes someone is settling (≤ 20 minutes), posts wait and are encrypted after it, so the removed member cannot read them; or post through the window (declared: the removed member reads those posts)? A committer's own non-removing commit holds nothing (§19.13). | **hold** | Cât se aplică o scoatere (≤ 20 de minute), postările așteaptă, ca cel scos să nu le poată citi |
| Q35 | **Reveal proof (§19.17).** The reveal carries `sig_id` (the `ghost1` identity key signs the pseudonym binding) and `sig_ps`: the recipient can prove to anyone — an operator, a court, other members — that this pseudonym is that identity. Keep it and declare it (L8-C8; Phase 13 warns before a reveal), or a deniable reveal: no identity signature in the channel, the binding confirmed afterwards inside the recipient's first DM with the card (Olm messages carry no signatures, so the recipient can show nothing a third party must believe), at the cost of a second step and a new design in Phase 13? Either way the MLS signature on the REVEAL post proves which pseudonym sent that ciphertext. | **keep, declared**; the deniable variant designed with Phase 13's reveal UX | Cel căruia îi dezvălui identitatea poate dovedi oricui legătura (semnătură): declarat acum; varianta fără dovadă transferabilă, în Faza 13 |
| Q36 | **Seizure window in short-timer channels (§19.19).** A seized device opens every unconsumed message of its current epoch from the epoch's start (up to 7 days with the weekly PCS commit, for anyone who recorded the relays). In channels with timers ≤ 1 d, make the committers' PCS commit daily (about one epoch a day, one 20-minute settle, no tokens), so the window stays near the timer, as ADR-27 promises? | **yes** (daily in channels with timers ≤ 1 d; weekly otherwise) | În canalele cu timer scurt, cheile se reîmprospătează zilnic, ca un telefon confiscat să nu deschidă mesaje mai vechi de o zi |
| Q37 | **Owner absence (§19.13).** An owner who vanishes without transferring rank 0 freezes the policy for the channel's life and keeps a leaf nobody can evict (a PCS hole if its device state leaked). Accept and declare it, or add a succession rule (rank 1 takes rank 0 after the owner's silence)? Without a server there is no trusted time: an epoch-count rule can be pumped by a moderator's empty commits, and a commit minute is the committer's claim, so any rule lets a moderator take over an active but quiet owner. | **accept, declared** (L8-C16); the UI urges naming a moderator and transferring before leaving | Dacă proprietarul dispare fără să-și predea rolul, regulile canalului rămân înghețate; fără succesiune automată (ar permite unui moderator să preia canalul) |
| Q38 | **Channel media (C-17, §5.10).** Spec FR-3.4 requires media up to 50 MiB in posts; Phase 11's design leaves channel media to Phase 10 ("designed and gated with Phase 10", its Q17), and channel media needs Phase 11's engine. Does Phase 10's exit wait for a channel-media slice built after Phase 11's engine lands, or does Phase 10 close on its plan row with the contract reserved and FR-3.4's media half recorded as not met until that slice (owned jointly, gated then in `channel-exit-gate`)? The slice also decides the media namespace (a separate one per generation costs about 4 of the 9 channels, §5.10). | **close Phase 10 without it**; the channel-media slice runs after Phase 11's engine, FR-3.4 stays partly open until then, and Phase 11's Q17 text is aligned at merge | Media în canale: formatul e rezervat acum; implementarea după motorul de media al Fazei 11; Faza 10 se închide fără ea |
| Q39 | **Order of decisions (FEA-M6, §19.23).** This design builds on ADR-25 and ADR-30′ … ADR-39 (proposed, awaiting the owner as Phase 9's Q4) and on Phase 9's code, whose exit gate is open. May S0 start now (evidence only), S1 onwards only once those ADRs are decided or explicitly accepted as the basis, and may Phase 10 close before Phase 9 with every claim marked as inheriting Phase 9's open gate? A green T2 sweep is not a Phase 10 exit item (§7.9). | **yes** (S0 now; S1 after the ADR decision; closure before Phase 9 allowed, marked) | Faza 10 poate începe cu S0 acum; restul după decizia pe ADR-urile Fazei 9; se poate închide înaintea Fazei 9, cu mențiune |
| Q40 | **New (V10-1, S0-1).** OpenMLS 0.9.0 does not resolve into the ghost lock: its optional SQLite provider (which GHOST never enables) needs an older `libsqlite3-sys` than Arti's, and `links = "sqlite3"` must be unique. Accept **(a)** a manifest-only `[patch.crates-io]` stub of that crate (never compiled; removed when upstream relaxes its requirement, F-5); or **(b)** pin `ghost-client-net`'s `libsqlite3-sys` back to 0.35 so Arti uses `rusqlite` 0.37 (Arti 0.46 allows it; measured to resolve), shipping SQLite 3.50.2 instead of 3.51.3 in Arti's directory cache (CVE-2025-7709, an FTS5 defect fixed in 3.50.3, needs control of the database file); or **(c)** wait for upstream? | **(a) the stub** | Un înlocuitor gol, niciodată compilat, pentru stocarea SQLite opțională a OpenMLS, ca să se rezolve dependențele lângă SQLite-ul lui Arti |
| Q41 | **New (V10-8, S0-15).** A joiner accepts only a Welcome whose GroupInfo is signed by one of the committers named in its invite (a 32-byte digest in the invite and in `INVITE_ISSUED`): without it, any holder of the generation's join key (an invite holder, a member removed at the end of the generation) can move a joiner into a group of its own under the channel's id, and the real Welcome then fails (measured). Accept the anchor with its costs — a change of committers voids every outstanding invite, a committer named at the invite's issue (even one removed since) can still capture the joiner until the invite expires (L8-C25, sharpened by §19.27 TR-4 and corrected by §19.29.2: once a committer is **removed** it is the only party that answers the invites its removal voided, it captures by forking the real group from its last state, and it can keep the captured member live, detectable only out of band; the recovery is Q49), a rejoiner accepts only committers it last knew — or require the sponsor-confirmed admission of Q30 instead (a second online step)? The anchor's digest is now over the committer **set** (TR-6) and is checked on the unverified GroupInfo before the tree is validated (TR-7). | **the anchor** (Q30 stays reserved) | Cel invitat acceptă doar un Welcome semnat de unul dintre moderatorii numiți în invitație; o schimbare a moderatorilor anulează invitațiile în curs |
| Q42 | **Re-asked (§19.29.1; new in round 10d, §19.27 TR-1 … TR-3).** The canonical KeyPackage's lifetime: **(a)** the **constant `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]`** (still 395 bytes; accepted by the committer, every receiver and `JoinBuilder`, E-62): no clock ever refuses a canonical KeyPackage, so an OpenMLS lifetime refusal means a non-canonical one and the commit is invalid for every member, and every member judges every commit alike whatever its device clock (measured, E-69, E-70); S0-12's trusted-day predicate, `clock_blocked`, L8-C20 and R-14 go; freshness stays on `GhostKpBind.day` and the trusted clock; or **(b)** the finite `[d − 4, d + 80]`, for which **no convergent rule is known**: on OpenMLS's refusal branch the rank is hidden, so a member either waits — then any member freezes a channel with one commit carrying an expired KeyPackage (measured: every correct clock refuses it, every member blocks, and no pass can remove the attacker) — or chooses without the rank — then honest members whose device clocks straddle an honest KeyPackage's bound fork (measured); round 10d's rule was the second; (b) needs a further design round. | **(a) the constant lifetime** | Pachetul de chei are o durată de viață constantă; cu o durată finită, fie orice membru poate bloca un canal, fie membrii cinstiți se despart după ceasul telefonului (măsurat) |
| Q43 | **Re-asked (§19.29.3; new in round 10d, §19.28).** How the password protects joining: **(a)** the **password-sealed invite** (Argon2id, RFC 9106, from the password and a salt in the link): no verifier on members' devices; a leaked link is an Argon2id-priced guessing oracle; it does **not** bind insiders — nothing derived from the password reaches `INVITE_ISSUED`, the join request or the commit, so a member allowed to invite mints a working invite without knowing the password (measured; Q46 limits who may invite); **(b′)** a **verifier in the context** — an Ed25519 public key derived from Argon2id of the password — and, in every join request, a signature by the derived key over SHA-256(key_package), checked by every member in CO-2 (A): no one who lacks the password, committers included, can admit anyone, and a change is one policy commit; but every member's device holds the verifier (offline guessing from any seized member device) and every holder of `JK_g` sees other joiners' signatures, the same oracle a leaked link gives; **(c)** a PAKE over relays (interactive, a relay reflex, no vetted current crate). | **(a)**, with Q46 (b) | Parola sigilează invitația (Argon2id); nu oprește un membru care poate invita, de aceea într-un canal cu parolă invită doar proprietarul și moderatorii (Q46) |
| Q44 | **Re-asked (§19.29.3; new in round 10d, §19.28.5).** The Argon2id profile **m = 64 MiB, t = 1, p = 4** (`kdf_code` 1): below RFC 9106's second recommended option (t = 3 at 64 MiB), above OWASP's first pair (46 MiB, t = 1); the floors are OWASP's parameter pairs (normative, §19.29.3); the device cost depends on Q47 (x86_64 emulator: native 125–128 ms, BouncyCastle on ART 197–269 ms; ×15–25 on a low-end phone ≈ 1.3–3.2 s and ≈ 3–7 s), re-measured at S2's exit. **And the password's strength:** a leaked link allows ≈ 154–160 guesses per second on this one 24-thread host (E-73: a 6-digit PIN in about an hour, 3 words of a 2 048-word list in about a year, 5 words in millions of years), and nothing checks a password's strength at use: **(a)** the app generates it — 5 words of a 2 048-word list, ≈ 55 bits — and the owner cannot set a weaker one; **(b)** the owner chooses, above a minimum estimated strength; **(c)** a recommendation only (round 10d). | **64 MiB / t=1 / p=4; (a) an app-generated 5-word passphrase** | Parola o generează aplicația (5 cuvinte, ≈ 55 de biți); Argon2id la 64 MiB, o trecere, 4 benzi; se remăsoară pe dispozitiv |
| Q45 | **Re-asked (§19.29.3; new in round 10d, §19.28.4).** A password set or change bumps `pw_version`; a committer refuses, and the settle voids, every invite of an older `pw_version` (so a link opened before the change yields a join request that is refused, and the sponsor sees it void). **And the generation:** whoever opened a link before the change keeps the generation's namespace, READ and DEPOSIT secrets and join key until the next rotation (≤ 28 days) — it can list and deposit in `ns_g` and open the generation's later join requests and Welcome blobs: **(a)** the set or change also carries the rotation flag (+3 tokens for the owner; while a generation drains, at the owner's first pass after the drain ends, Q28); **(b)** no rotation (declared). | **(a) rotate** | Schimbarea parolei anulează invitațiile vechi și mută canalul pe o adresă nouă (+3 token-uri) |
| Q46 | **Re-asked (§19.29.3; new in round 10d, §19.28.4).** Who may issue invites in a password channel. Round 10d's "a member without it cannot mint invites" is **withdrawn**: a member allowed to invite that never knew the password mints a working invite with a password it makes up, or an open one, and every check passes (measured). **(a)** keep `members_may_invite`: the password is a second factor against leaked links from honest sponsors only (declared); **(b)** flag bit 2 forces `members_may_invite = 0` (a §5.3 validity rule, so CO-2 (A) refuses an Add sponsored by a non-committer in a password channel): only committers — the owner and moderators, who can admit anyone anyway — invite there; **(c)** Q43 (b′). | **(b) only committers invite in a password channel** | Într-un canal cu parolă invită doar proprietarul și moderatorii |
| Q47 | **Re-asked (§19.29.3; new in round 10d, §19.28.5; the proposed default changes).** Where Argon2id runs: **(a)** in Rust (`argon2` 0.6.0, +4 names to G-M1: `argon2`, `blake2`, `password-hash`, `phc`; its 64 MiB off the Java heap; 125–128 ms on the x86_64 emulator; the password crosses JNI once as NFKD UTF-8 bytes and is zeroized after the call); **(b)** in Kotlin with BouncyCastle (`:identity` and `:network` ship it, `:messaging` only in its instrumented tests; the 64 MiB matrix on the Java heap — OutOfMemoryError at -Xmx72m on HotSpot and at -Xmx64m on ART, against 96 MiB of app heap on AOSP's 1 GB phone profile; 197–269 ms on ART, 1.6–2.1× the native time in the two emulator re-runs (2.9× in the review's run); the one Kotlin key derivation, outside §10.1's rule). | **(a) Rust** (round 10d proposed (b)) | Argon2id rulează nativ, în Rust: BouncyCastle cere peste 64 MiB din heap-ul Java și e mai lent pe ART |
| Q48 | **Re-asked (§19.29.3; new in round 10d, §19.28.4).** A rejoin (Q33) involves no invite, so it needs no password. A restore goes through a fresh invite (§6.12): **(a)** in a password channel it needs the password like any join (a member who forgot it asks the owner out of band); **(b)** a restore-only invite: an open link whose `INVITE_ISSUED` carries a restore marker (a new TLV) and that a committer admits only as a swap of a current member's pseudonym. Round 10d's "a rejoin/restore carries `pw_version` 0" is withdrawn (the join request has no such field). | **(a)** | Reintrarea nu cere parola; restaurarea pe un dispozitiv nou o cere, ca orice intrare |
| Q49 | **New (§19.29.2).** A committer named in an invite (or among a rejoiner's last committers), even one removed since, can capture the invitee in a fork of the real group and keep it captured for as long as it sustains the fork (measured: the fork passes the anchor and §5.3 and stays live); only comparing the fingerprint out of band shows it, and a captured member has no way back in-band (a new invite is refused while it is `active`; at `reinvite_needed` its restore-purpose KeyPackage is refused because its pseudonym never was a member). **(a)** declare it, with the UI obligations of §19.29.2 and a way back: after an out-of-band comparison the user leaves the captured copy and accepts a fresh invite, and a committer admits a restore-purpose KeyPackage whose pseudonym is not a current member as an Add (a join-purpose KeyPackage still never becomes a swap); **(b)** (a) plus `ANCHOR_CONFLICT`: accepting an invite whose committers digest differs from the held channel's raises a conflict for the user instead of `ALREADY_MEMBER` — a capture shows as soon as a fresh invite arrives, but a forged conflicting invite can lure a member into a forger's group if the user accepts; **(c)** a subset anchor: the Welcome's GroupInfo carries the committer set of the invite's issue (its digest must equal the invite's), the signer must be in it and the joined committers must be a subset of it, so a committer's removal no longer voids invites and capture becomes a race — which the forger, acting at once, usually wins against committers acting at scheduled passes; **(d)** Q30's sponsor-confirmed admission (a second online step; it does not by itself stop a named committer's fork). | **(a)** | Un moderator scos poate captura un invitat într-o copie a canalului cât timp o întreține; se vede doar comparând amprenta; utilizatorul revine părăsind copia și acceptând o invitație nouă |
| Q50 | **New (§19.29.3).** What the sponsor's device keeps of a password invite: **(a)** neither the link nor the password: `ch_invite_own` keeps the invite id, the expiry and its state for the UI, the link is shown once at issue, and the sponsor types the password at each issue; **(b)** the link until its expiry (a seized unlocked sponsor device is then an Argon2id-priced password oracle for up to 7 days after each issue; declared); **(c)** also the password in the OS keystore at the user's opt-in (Phase 13; a seized unlocked device yields the password). | **(a)** | Telefonul celui care invită nu păstrează nici linkul, nici parola |

---

## 18. Findings outside Phase 10 (proposed as separate tasks)

- **F-1.** STATUS:14 and `relay/README.md:20` assign "Noise gossip" to Phase 10; that is spec v2.0 numbering (its Phase 10 was multi-relay metadata protection). The v2.1 plan places relay-to-relay work nowhere explicitly; the owner should assign it (Phase 12 or operations). Erratum in Appendix B.4.
- **F-2.** The guarantee matrix (plan:241), TM:50 (AD-2 "nu vede … apartenență") and TM:38 (AS-7 "doar în capetele MLS") say relays do not see channel membership; under AD-1 session co-occurrence rebuilds it (L7-D2). Text corrected in Appendix B; decoupling is Phase 12.
- **F-3.** Spec FR-5.5 (90-day default TTL) is superseded for channel and DM blobs by ADR-27/ADR-32; the spec erratum should say so.
- **F-4.** Phase 9's G-R3, G-R4 (graph half), G-R7 and G-R8 are not built; **decided by S0: each is built once, generic over the DM and MLS graphs** (§13.1, S0-18); Phase 9 work in progress builds them, and S2 extends that version.
- **F-5.** Upstream OpenMLS requests (no fork): a storage transaction hook (begin/commit around an operation), which would let GHOST drop the change-set copy; a sender-data peek for ordering; a documented time source for past-epoch stamps; **an injectable time source for leaf and KeyPackage lifetime validation** (today `SystemTime::now()`, switchable only by a crate-internal test flag, §19.4), which would let GHOST judge lifetimes on its trusted clock and let the JVM worlds run on virtual time; **(added by S0)** relaxing `openmls_sqlite_storage`'s `rusqlite` requirement or dropping that optional dependency from `openmls` (V10-1: it blocks the ghost lock; Q40's stub goes when it lands); storing past epochs' secret trees apart from the `MessageSecretsStore` (V10-5: every post rewrites every retained epoch); calling `Lifetime::has_acceptable_range` (V10-11: never called); un-ignoring the test of `create_group_info_with_extensions` (V10-8); and publishing advisories to RustSec (V10-2).
- **F-11 (added by S0; F-10 is left unused here because the text cites Phase 9's F-10 carry-over).** RustSec files RUSTSEC-2026-0209 and -0211 under `libcrux-aesgcm`, the former name of `libcrux-aes`, with no patched version, so no tool maps them to the crate GHOST links; a note to the RustSec maintainers (or a `libcrux-aes` entry) closes the gap for every user (V10-2).
- **F-6.** The v1 `media` table has `owner_operation_id` without a foreign key (`Schema.kt:211-219`); Phase 11 should replace it with the migration-7 style. (The Phase 11 design's migration M drops `media` and `media_blobs` behind a fail-closed guard, `faza11-media.md` D21; migration 7 of this design leaves them to it, so the two agree.)
- **F-9.** The plan's FR-3.4 row reads "Timestamp rotunjit la minut" (plan:262), while spec FR-3.4 reads "Posts MUST support text, media up to 50 MiB and an authenticated client timestamp", accepted by "Boundary-size, invalid-media and timestamp-signature tests pass" (spec p.8). The plan row should carry the media clause; which phase closes it is Q38 (C-17).
- **F-7.** Phase 9's harness debts that Phase 10 must build (§19.10): the seeded native randomness (never built; F9 §22 item 13), the redeem lane inside the world, HIGH mode with send delays, circuit timing, and the fuzz and vendor-test arrangements. Built once, generic over the DM and MLS worlds, they also close parts of Phase 9's own open gate; STATUS should say so when they land.
- **F-8.** THREAT_MODEL S3 ("MLS transcript detects gaps") overclaims against a delivery service that partitions the group: RFC 9750 §5.2 says such a partition "can be detected only by out-of-band comparison". The wording is corrected in Appendix B.2.

---

## 19. Normative corrections after review (round 10b)

**The review.** Round 10a (commit `a79b7ce`) was reviewed adversarially under three lenses: protocol and cryptographic soundness (findings `P10-Rn`), privacy, metadata and threat coverage (`PRV-Hn`), and feasibility, testability, scope and consistency (`FEA-Hn`, `FEA-Mn`). Every finding received was checked against the OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources in the local cargo registry (`om:`; the crate's `.cargo_vcs_info.json` names commit `3a3e35de3feeca8f6605143c464d5452ae584d43`, which is the tag `openmls-v0.9.0`), against RFC 9750 (text fetched 2026-09-25 and quoted below) and against the repository at `f244b80` (`InboxStore.kt`, `Inbox.kt`, `Schema.kt`, `PairSchedule.kt`, `TrafficPolicy.kt`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `docs/ci-self-hosted.md`, F7, F9). **No finding received was rejected as wrong** (round 10c rejects no finding in full; it rejects parts of two — P10-R8, P10-R13 — narrows two — P10-R9, PRV-M2 — and declines two optional suggestions — P10-R5, PRV-M5 — each with its reason in §19.0a). Eight proposed fixes are narrowed, replaced or left to the owner, each with its reason in its subsection: P10-R1's "late commit loses at the source" and its optional pre-merge copy (§19.1, Q27); P10-R3's ±1-day window and "no fork evidence" test (§19.3); FEA-H3's "authenticated commit minute" anchor (§19.1); PRV-H3's sponsor-confirmed admission (§19.7, Q30) and its rotation after every expired invite (spaced to one per 14 days, §19.7); PRV-H5's switch at "the first session after the commit's day plus 1" (replaced by the commit's switch hour, which is commit-determined without a day-long pause in posting, §19.8); PRV-H6's reconsidered funder default (kept, with the leak declared and the alternatives offered, §19.9, Q7, Q31).

**What each round read (revised in round 10c).** A tooling defect cut each review at 10 000 characters before round 10b's author saw it: the protocol lens inside P10-R4's correction (P10-R5's title and everything after it unread; P10-R3 referred to a P10-R15 whose text was not delivered), the privacy lens inside PRV-H7's fix (PRV-M1 … PRV-M6 and PRV-L1 … PRV-L4 unread), the feasibility lens inside FEA-M1 (its second half, FEA-M2 … FEA-M8, six low findings, a table of eight proposed owner questions and a table of claims "verified but not true" unread). Round 10b corrected P10-R5 from a reconstruction (§19.5), P10-R15 from P10-R3's reference (§19.3) and FEA-M1 from its first half (§19.6). **Round 10c read the three reviews in full** — `p10-review-protocol.md` (22 935 characters), `p10-review-privacy.md` (15 808), `p10-review-feasibility.md` (16 947), kept under `.claude/scratch/reviews/` outside version control — and checked **every** finding, before and after the cut, against the round-10b text, the repository at `d0fddf8`, the OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources and, where a finding touched it, the Phase 11 design on branch `p11-design` at `116cfcd`. The result is §19.0a: 55 findings (17 protocol, 17 privacy, 21 feasibility) and the feasibility lens's 8 proposed questions; **none remains unread or unanswered**. Round 10b's reconstruction of P10-R5 matched the reviewer's text except for one optional suggestion, which §19.13 declines with its reason. The corrections of round 10c are §19.13 … §19.24; §19.25 is their bookkeeping.

**This section is normative: where it and an earlier section disagree, this section wins.** Earlier sections were corrected in place where the change is local; those places cite "(§19.n)".
