### 19.27 Targeted protocol review of S0-12 and S0-15 (2026-09-28)

**Precedence (normative).** This section wins over §19.1 … §19.26 (and over the sections they touch) for the two rules it reviews — the trusted-day KeyPackage-lifetime predicate (S0-12) and the committers anchor (S0-15) — and for nothing else. **§19.29 (round 10e) wins over this section**: its adversarial review withdrew TR-1's refusal-branch rule and TR-2's "the junk never blocks" (they fork honest members and let any member freeze a channel, E-69, E-70), made Q42's constant lifetime the baseline together with the rules it replaces (§6.2.4 (B), fn 4, §19.4 rules 1 and 3, MM-39, T13, ADR-40 point 4), withdrew TR-3's "residual honest-skew split", TR-4's recovery claim and its joiner timer, and TR-7's set-digest equality for rejoins; the paragraphs below are corrected in place where they state those, citing §19.29. It is the targeted review §19.26 records as owed before S1 ("**S0-12 and S0-15 are new protocol rules and go to the protocol lens of the next adversarial review** before Q1 is answered"). Each rule was attacked against OpenMLS 0.9.0's real behaviour at the pinned tag `3a3e35de…`; the spike carries a probe for every finding (`ghost/client-core/mls-spike/core/tests/r10d_s0_12_lifetimes.rs`, `r10d_s0_15_anchor.rs`; run 2026-09-28, evidence E-61 … E-64). Findings are `TR-n`.

**Sources verified from primary material (accessed 2026-09-28).** RFC 9420 §7.2 (`https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9420.txt`): "Applications MUST define a maximum total lifetime that is acceptable for a LeafNode, and reject any LeafNode where the total lifetime is longer than this duration. In order to avoid disagreements about whether a LeafNode has a valid lifetime, the clients in a group SHOULD maintain time synchronization (e.g., using [NTP])." §7.3: sending → "the client MUST verify that the current time is within the range of the lifetime field"; **receiving** → "it is RECOMMENDED that the client verifies … (This check is not mandatory because the LeafNode might have expired in the time between when the message was sent and when it was received.)". §12.4.3.1: "Verify the signature on the GroupInfo object. … The public key is taken from the LeafNode of the ratchet tree with leaf index `signer`." OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources: `key_packages/key_package_in.rs:206-208` (`life_time.validate()` → `lifetime.rs:87` → `validate_with_time(SystemTime::now())`); `lifetime.rs:116` `has_acceptable_range` **called nowhere**; `MlsGroup::unprotect_message`, `PublicGroup::parse_message` and `UnverifiedMessage::verify` are all `pub(crate)` (no public API returns decrypted-but-unvalidated content); `ProcessedWelcome::unverified_group_info()` and `VerifiableGroupInfo::verify_no_out` are **public**; `keys_for_welcome` deletes the joiner's KeyPackage inside `ProcessedWelcome::new_from_welcome` (`creation.rs:706-713`).

#### S0-12: the trusted-day lifetime predicate

**TR-1 (the "both branches" rule is not computable on the refusal branch; corrects CO-2, CO-4).** S0-12 has the member evaluate "one predicate on the member's trusted day, **on both branches of OpenMLS's answer**." A received commit is a PrivateMessage; OpenMLS decrypts and validates it in one `pub(crate)` step (`process_message`), and its KeyPackage-lifetime check runs **before** the update path, the proposal set and everything GHOST checks (V10-11, E-52). The two branches are not symmetric:

- **Accept branch** (device clock within the KeyPackage's range): `process_message` returns the staged commit, so GHOST reads each Add's KeyPackage and its bounds and applies its trusted-day predicate. **Measured (E-61):** the staged commit exposes the sender leaf and the Add count. This branch works as S0-12 says.
- **Refusal branch** (device clock outside the range): `process_message` returns only `Err(ValidationError(KeyPackageVerifyError(LifetimeError(Expired|NotValidYet{not_after|not_before, now}))))`. **Measured (E-61):** the error carries **one** `not_after` — the first failing KeyPackage of the commit — and **no sender, no proposal list, no rank, no second bound**. There is no public OpenMLS API that returns the decrypted-but-unvalidated framed content (`unprotect_message`/`parse_message`/`verify` are `pub(crate)`), and the seal's `open` recovers only the PrivateMessage's cleartext header (epoch, content type) and its authenticated data (`GhostCommitAad`, which names each Add's `kp_ref` but **not** the KeyPackage bytes or their bounds, and carries **no** MLS sender). So on the refusal branch GHOST can compute **neither the trusted-day predicate** (no bounds) **nor the candidate's rank** (no sender) **nor CO-1's "no proposal" test** (no proposal list). S0-12's "lifetime-pending: ranked by `(rank, hash)`, blocks the settle while it would win, becomes invalid when the trusted day stops accepting the KeyPackage" is therefore only partly implementable: the rank is unknown, and "the trusted day stops accepting" can never be observed (the bounds are never learned), so the escape to "invalid" is dead.

*Round 10d's correction — **withdrawn by §19.29.1**.* Round 10d evaluated the trusted-day predicate only on the accept branch and made a `LifetimeError` candidate lifetime-pending at the lowest rank (255), blocking the settle (`clock_blocked`) only when no decidable valid candidate was held, and claimed "this converges". It does not: an honest rank-0 commit adding an honest KeyPackage and an honest rank-255 self-update for the same epoch are merged differently by a member whose device clock accepts the KeyPackage (the rank-0 commit) and one whose clock is past its bound (the self-update) — same epoch, different epoch authenticators (E-69); and a lone junk commit blocks every member (E-70). *What replaces it (§19.29.1, CO-2):* with Q42's constant lifetime a `LifetimeError` (the typed error still discriminates it from every other refusal) can only come from a non-canonical KeyPackage, so the commit is **invalid** on both branches for every member.

**TR-2 (an insider stall the refusal branch opens; new mutant MM-46).** Because the refusal branch hides the sender and the proposal list, a **member of any rank** (rank 255 included) can build a commit carrying an Add of an **expired** KeyPackage, seal it under `K_ep(e)` (mode 0x11, which every member can produce), and deposit it. Every member whose device clock is past the KeyPackage's `not_after` reaches the refusal branch and cannot tell this junk (a rank-255-with-proposal commit, invalid by CO-1) from a valid committer commit its own clock happens to reject. Under round 10c's / S0-12's "blocks the settle while it would win," a low-ground hash on the junk commit would stall the epoch. Round 10d claimed its lowest-priority rule closed this and "the junk never blocks"; **corrected by §19.29.1:** every member with a correct clock, the owner included, reaches the refusal branch on an expired KeyPackage (E-70), so as the sole candidate the junk made **every** member `clock_blocked` — not current (§6.3.1) — and CO-7 then forbade every pass, the owner's Remove of the attacker included, until some member's scheduled self-update gave a decidable candidate, and the attacker could repeat it every epoch. With Q42's constant lifetime the junk is invalid for every member and blocks nothing (E-70). The blob is not "unopenable foreign" (it opens the seal, then MLS refuses), so CO-11/CO-12 do not fire on it; it is dropped and counted as an invalid commit.

**TR-3 (the constant lifetime removes OpenMLS's device-clock dependency entirely; Q42, recommended default).** OpenMLS validates a received Add's KeyPackage lifetime against the device's **wall clock** (`SystemTime::now()`), **not** GHOST's trusted clock, and — against RFC 9420 §7.3, which makes the receive-side check RECOMMENDED "because the LeafNode might have expired … between when the message was sent and when it was received" — OpenMLS makes it **mandatory** (switchable only by a crate-internal test flag). The trusted clock is bounded (σ = 3 d, from Arti/NTP, F7:58); the device **wall** clock has no such bound, and under Tor-only, with NTP often unreachable, an honest phone's wall clock can be days wrong — so an honest member can reach the refusal branch on a perfectly valid commit and stall (TR-1). GHOST does not otherwise use the lifetime field: freshness and expiry are enforced by `GhostKpBind.day` (checked on the **trusted** day by the admitting committer and, through the AAD, by every member, §6.2.4) and by idle eviction. The lifetime field's only effect in GHOST is to arm OpenMLS's unavoidable wall-clock gate.

**Measured (E-62):** a canonical KeyPackage built with the **constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]`** is **395 bytes** — identical to the day-aligned one (`Lifetime` is two fixed `u64`s), so the canonical size (S0-4) and G-M7's golden are unchanged; it is accepted by the committer's `KeyPackageIn::validate`, by every receiver of the Add at any later time, and by the **default** `JoinBuilder` (lifetime validation on) over a tree whose KeyPackage leaves — the creator's included — all carry it; a 4-member channel built this way has equal epoch authenticators, and beside it a finite-lifetime Add is still refused once expired. With the constant lifetime **OpenMLS's lifetime gate never fires on a canonical KeyPackage**, so the undecidable refusal branch (TR-1), the `clock_blocked` state, the L8-C20 malicious-committer split and R-14 all disappear; freshness stays with `GhostKpBind.day`, checked on the committer's trusted day in (B) and, in CO-2 (A), against the commit's own `commit_day`, which is the same for every member — **so no member-dependent split remains** (corrected §19.29.1: round 10d described a "residual honest-skew split" here, but CO-2 reads no member's clock). A non-canonical (finite) KeyPackage can still be refused by OpenMLS; under the constant lifetime that refusal makes the commit invalid for every member (§19.29.1, E-69, E-70). (E-62's default-`JoinBuilder` test shows OpenMLS's own join accepts constant-lifetime trees; GHOST's join path keeps `skip_lifetime_validation()` and checks the constant form itself, §6.2.5.) RFC 9420 §7.2's "maximum total lifetime" is an application obligation GHOST discharges through `GhostKpBind` (its own reject-if-too-old rule), consistent with the RFC leaving the mechanism to the application; GHOST is a closed, suite-locked deployment with no MLS interop other than the passive-client vectors (which build no KeyPackages), so no peer implementation rejects the wide lifetime.

**Q42 (default yes; re-asked by §19.29.1): adopt the constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]` for the canonical KeyPackage** (§6.2.4 (B), ADR-40 points 4 and 7), removing OpenMLS's device-wall-clock dependency, the `clock_blocked` state, L8-C20 and R-14, with freshness left to `GhostKpBind.day` on the trusted clock. **Round 10e:** this is the normative baseline, because it is the only convergent rule found; round 10d's "if the owner keeps finite lifetimes, TR-1's refusal-branch correction is the baseline" is withdrawn — with finite lifetimes no convergent rule is known (§19.29.1). Either way S0-12's "ranked as valid" and "becomes invalid when the trusted day stops accepting" are withdrawn (unimplementable on the refusal branch, TR-1).

#### S0-15: the committers anchor

**TR-4 (a committer-set change makes capture guaranteed, not a race; sharpens L8-C25, Q41).** S0-15's anchor names the committers **at the invite's issue**; L8-C25 declares that a committer named then, even one removed since, can still forge a Welcome the invitee accepts. The review makes the exposure concrete and worse than "can still": when a commit **removes a committer** it (i) changes `committers`, so CO-5 **voids the invite** and the real committers **never issue its Welcome**, and (ii) opens generation g + 1 (a Remove rotates, D6) while the removed committer keeps `JK_g` and can deposit into the draining `ns_g` for 14 days. The invitee, whose invite names generation g and who listens on `ns_g`, therefore receives **only** the removed committer's forged Welcome — the real side produces none — so capture is **guaranteed** for anyone who acts on the (now-voided) invite, not a race. **Measured (E-63):** the removed moderator, from a group of its own under the channel id with the anchored committers list, produces a Welcome whose signer is itself (an anchored committer) and whose digest matches the invite's; the invitee's anchor comparison **accepts** it and OpenMLS consumes its KeyPackage. (*Corrected by §19.29.2:* this fresh-group form fails §5.3's validity, which `welcomeJoin` checks — the owner it names is not a leaf of the forger's group — so E-63 showed the anchor comparison only; the capture that passes every check is the removed committer's **fork of the real group from its last state**, E-71.)

*Round 10d's correction — **withdrawn by §19.29.2**.* Round 10d took a captured invitee for a silent orphan and named its recovery: a shorter joiner timer, `stale_suspected`, then `reinvite_needed` and a fresh restore invite — "not silent forever". None of it holds: the forger's fork carries the real members' leaves and the forger can keep it live with committer commits and posts, which the captured joiner merges and reads (E-71), so neither `stale_suspected` nor `reinvite_needed` fires; and even a silent forger's victim cannot come back in-band — at `reinvite_needed` §6.2.2 builds a restore-purpose KeyPackage, which CO-2 (A) admits only as a swap of a current leaf, and the captured pseudonym never was a member; while `active`, a new invite is refused as `ALREADY_MEMBER`. The joiner timer is withdrawn with the claim. *What stands (§19.29.2):* the residue is declared as measured (L8-C25) — capture lasts as long as the forger sustains it and only an out-of-band comparison of the fingerprint shows it; the sponsor's client tells its user when an invite is voided; how a captured member returns is **Q49**.

**TR-5 (the same for a rejoiner's anchor).** A rejoiner's anchor is the committers of its **last final epoch**; a committer removed on the live branch since the rejoiner went offline is still in that set, so it can forge a rejoin Welcome the rejoiner accepts. **Measured (E-63):** a removed moderator forges a rejoin Welcome whose signer is among the stale member's last-known committers, and the anchor accepts it (in the fresh-group form, which §5.3 refuses; in the fork form, which passes the rejoin pre-check and §5.3, E-71). Same class as TR-4, same residue: no in-band recovery (§19.29.2, Q49); L8-C25 covers it.

**TR-6 (the digest must be over the committer *set*, not the ordered list; corrects §5.2, §5.9; new mutant MM-45).** The committers digest of §5.2 is computed over the committers **in list order**. The anchor uses only **set membership** ("the GroupInfo signer is one of the named committers"), so the order carries no security. But an **ordered** digest changes when the list is merely **reordered** — a **transfer of rank 0** (§19.13) reorders `committers` without changing the set — so CO-5 **voids every outstanding invite** on a transfer of rank 0, an availability cost with no security benefit. **Measured (E-64):** reordering two committers changes the ordered digest and leaves the set digest (over the committers sorted bytewise) unchanged, while dropping a committer changes both.

*Correction (normative; §5.2, §5.9, §6.2.4).* The committers digest is `SHA-256("ghost/v1/ch/committers" ‖ channel_id ‖ u8(k) ‖ sort(committers))` — the committer keys **sorted bytewise ascending**, so the digest binds the **set** of keys that may sign a Welcome, order-independent. A transfer of rank 0 (a reorder) no longer voids invites; an add or removal of a committer (a set change) still voids them (CO-5) and still requires an admitting commit to leave the set unchanged (CO-2). This is a change to a compatibility-contract derivation (§5.2), pinned by the `ch_derive.txt` vectors; it supersedes §5.2's ordered digest.

**TR-7 (the anchor must be checked on the *unverified* GroupInfo, before the ratchet-tree validation; corrects §6.2.5, fn 5; new mutant MM-44).** S0-15's anchor as written reads the joined context's committers and the GroupInfo signer from a **built** `StagedWelcome`, i.e. after `JoinBuilder::build()` has validated the **entire ratchet tree** (every leaf signature, parent hashes). A holder of `JK_g` — an invite holder, a member removed at the end of g — can deposit forged Welcome blobs into the generation namespace; the invitee opens each (mode 0x13) and, without an earlier check, pays a full tree validation per forgery. **Measured (E-64), forged Welcome refused: pre-check vs full `build()`:** at 32 members **0.84 ms vs 6.02 ms**, at 100 members **1.52 ms vs 24.48 ms** (medians of 9; forged and real Welcomes the same size, 9 135 B and 26 143 B) — a 7–16× CPU amplification on the invitee per forged blob, ×(15–25) on a low-end phone (≈ 0.4–0.6 s each). The pre-check verifies the GroupInfo signature against ≤ 8 anchored committer keys and does no tree validation.

*Correction (normative; §6.2.5, §10.2 fn 5).* `welcomeJoin` checks the anchor on the **unverified** GroupInfo before `JoinBuilder::build()`: after `ProcessedWelcome::new_from_welcome` (which HPKE-opens the group secrets and AEAD-opens the GroupInfo — no tree validation), it reads `unverified_group_info().group_context()`'s `GhostChannelContext.committers`, for a join or restore checks their set digest equals the invite's `committers_digest`, and verifies the GroupInfo signature with `VerifiableGroupInfo::verify_no_out` (public) under each anchored committer key — for a rejoin, under each committer of its last final epoch, **with no digest comparison** (corrected §19.29.2: round 10d compared the set digest with the last committers', which refused a legitimate rejoin Welcome once the live branch had added a committer and bought nothing, a forger copying the set; E-72); only if one verifies does it call `build()` and apply the join. `new_from_welcome` deletes the joiner's KeyPackage (`creation.rs:706-713`), so the whole call runs under value semantics and its change-set is discarded on any failure — the KeyPackage survives a refused Welcome (the V10-8/S0-15 property is unchanged; only the order of the checks moves the tree validation after the cheap anchor). The measurement (E-64) confirms the pre-check refuses both forgeries and accepts the real Welcome with the KeyPackage intact.

#### Disposition, tests, mutants

| Finding | Verdict | Correction | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| TR-1 lifetime "both branches" not computable on the refusal branch | CONFIRMED (E-61) | round 10d: trusted-day predicate on the accept branch only, a `LifetimeError` candidate lifetime-pending at the lowest rank — **withdrawn by §19.29.1** (it forks honest members, E-69); now: under Q42's constant lifetime a `LifetimeError` makes the commit invalid | CO-2, CO-4, T41, CS-C3 |
| TR-2 insider expired-KeyPackage stall | CONFIRMED (E-61, E-70) | round 10d: "closed by TR-1's rule" — **false (§19.29.1)**: as the sole candidate the junk blocked every member; now invalid for every member | CO-2, CO-4, §12.3, MM-46 |
| TR-3 constant lifetime removes the device-clock gate | CONFIRMED (E-62) | Q42 (a) is the normative baseline (§19.29.1), with the rules it replaces; the "residual honest-skew split" withdrawn | §3.5, §3.6, §6.2.4, CO-2, ADR-40 points 4 and 7, Q42 |
| TR-4 committer-set change → guaranteed capture | CONFIRMED (E-63; the fork form E-71) | round 10d's recovery (`stale_suspected` → `reinvite_needed`, restore invite) **withdrawn by §19.29.2**: capture lasts while the forger sustains it, no in-band recovery; declared; Q49 | §6.2.5, L8-C25, Q41, Q49, MM-47 (withdrawn) |
| TR-5 rejoiner anchor captured by a since-removed committer | CONFIRMED (E-63, E-71) | same residue; L8-C25, Q49 | §6.7, §6.2.5, L8-C25 |
| TR-6 ordered digest voids invites on a transfer of rank 0 | CONFIRMED (E-64) | set digest (committers sorted bytewise) | §5.2, §5.9, §6.2.4, MM-45 |
| TR-7 anchor checked after full tree validation (CPU DoS) | CONFIRMED (E-64) | pre-check on the unverified GroupInfo before `build()`; for a rejoin the signature only (corrected §19.29.2, E-72) | §6.2.5, §10.2 fn 5, MM-44 |

**Mutants (registered with killers; extend §12.9).** **MM-44** the anchor checked only after `JoinBuilder::build()` (killer: the TR-7 probe / JN, a forged Welcome is refused before any tree validation, measured pre-check cost). **MM-45** the committers digest over the ordered list, not the sorted set (killer: the TR-6 vector — a transfer of rank 0 must not change the digest, a committer add/remove must). **MM-46** (retargeted by §19.29.1) a `LifetimeError`-refused commit treated as anything but invalid (killer: SCN-CM's insider expired-KeyPackage commit; EM-3; the E-69/E-70 probes). **MM-47** (withdrawn by §19.29.2; its claim does not hold against an active forger, E-71). **Tests:** the probes above and round 10e's (§19.29) move into `ghost-client-mls`'s S2/S4/S5 suites and G-M7; EM-3 (§12.3) gains different device clocks and the insider's non-canonical-lifetime commit; the `ModelRelay` FAKE_WELCOME mode (§12.4) gains the removed-committer fork and the CPU-amplification measurement; JN (§12.6) gains the pre-check-before-build assertion, the rejoin signature-only rule and the set-digest reorder case.

### 19.28 Channel password (owner requirement 2026-09-27)

**Precedence (normative).** This section wins over §19.1 … §19.27 and over the sections it touches for what it names: the channel password that protects **joining**. It designs the owner's 2026-09-27 requirement — the channel administrator (owner, rank 0) can set, change or remove a password so that joining needs **both** the single-use invite (D10, unchanged) **and** the password, shared out of band; the password does **not** lock local viewing. Design only; the evidence is measured in the spike (`ghost/client-core/mls-spike/core/tests/r10d_password.rs`, run 2026-09-28, E-65 … E-68; re-built on the specified construction and extended by `r10e_password.rs` in round 10e, E-73 … E-75), which adds `argon2` 0.6.0 as a **dev-dependency of the spike's own `[workspace]`** only (never a shipped or host graph). **§19.29.3 (round 10e) wins over this section** and its corrections are made in place below, citing §19.29.

#### 19.28.1 Mechanism comparison and choice

Three mechanisms, judged on offline guessing (leaked link, seized device, relays), who issues invites, the effect of a password change, interaction with the committers anchor / `INVITE_ISSUED` / admission evidence / rejoin (Q33) / restore (Q19), relay metadata, CP-09, licences, low-end cost, and the Phase 13 UX.

| Criterion | (a) password-sealed invite (Argon2id) | (b) proof in the join request vs a stored verifier | (c) PAKE over relays (CPace / SPAKE2 / OPAQUE) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Offline guess from a **leaked link** | yes — the link carries the salt and the sealed secrets; Argon2id-bounded (measured below) | no — the link carries no password material | no — the link carries no password material |
| Offline guess from a **seized member/committer device** | **no verifier on members' devices** (corrected §19.29.3: the sponsor's device keeps each sealed link until its expiry unless Q50 (a) — a password oracle; every member knows the password anyway) | **yes** — the verifier is in the policy every member holds; any one seized member device leaks it | augmented (OPAQUE): **yes** (server-compromise dictionary, precomputation-resistant); balanced (CPace/SPAKE2): no verifier, but every member needs the password |
| Offline guess from **relays** | no — the invite is out of band; the join request is a normal sealed blob | no (but every `JK_g` holder sees other joiners' proofs, an oracle) | PAKEs resist passive observation by design |
| Who issues; does a sponsor need the password | the sponsor seals under the password it types; **nothing checks it**, so a member allowed to invite mints a working invite without the real password (corrected §19.29.3, measured E-73; Q46 limits who may invite) | the sponsor issues **without** the password; the joiner proves it, checked at admission by every member — no one lacking it can admit anyone | the invitee runs the PAKE; the committer (server) needs the verifier / the password |
| A password change | affects **future** invites; invites of an older `pw_version` refused at admission and voided at settle; with Q45 (a) the generation rotates (§19.29.3) | a clean policy commit; applies to all future joins at once | a new verifier / re-registration |
| Committers anchor / `INVITE_ISSUED` / admission evidence | **composes unchanged** — the password gates reading the link; admission is by `invite_sig` as in D10/§19.2 | the proof is the committer's admission **policy** (B), like invite freshness (§19.2) | interactive; a PAKE transcript in the commit's authenticated data or a committer-only check |
| rejoin (Q33) / restore (Q19) | a rejoin involves no invite and needs no password; a restore goes through a fresh invite and, in a password channel, needs the password (corrected §19.29.3, Q48) | same | same |
| Relay metadata / indistinguishability | out-of-band invite → a password channel is **not** distinguishable at relays (NI-CH5); every link is 335 B whatever its kind and name (measured, E-73), but its cleartext `kdf_code` tells any link holder whether it needs a password, and an open link opens from its salt (corrected §19.29.3) | the join request carries a proof; pad to one size | new relay round trips → a **reflex** (T40) and new observable |
| CP-09 (no novel crypto, vetted crates) | Argon2id (RFC 9106) + HKDF + ChaCha20-Poly1305 — all standard, all already present | standard too (corrected §19.29.3): a MAC or signature under a password-derived key over SHA-256(key_package) — the binding `invite_sig` uses — cannot be replayed for another KeyPackage, so no challenge is needed; with a MAC every member holding the verifier can also mint proofs, with a signature (a public verifier) it cannot | a PAKE is a large, **interactive** addition; the only audited Rust crate is `opaque-ke` (NCC Group, 2021, at an old version) |
| Licences (permissive, pin by hash) | `argon2`/`blake2`/`password-hash`/`phc` MIT OR Apache-2.0 (Q47 (a)), or BouncyCastle on Kotlin (Q47 (b)) | as (a) plus the verifier scheme | `spake2` 0.4.0 "have not yet received any formal … security reviews" (verified 2026-09-28); `pake-cpace` 0.1.7 unaudited; `opaque-ke` 4.0.1 Apache-2.0/MIT |
| Standards maturity | Argon2id: **RFC 9106** (IRTF, Informational, 2021; widely deployed) | — | CPace: draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21, "Sent to the RFC Editor," **not yet an RFC** (verified 2026-09-28); SPAKE2: RFC 9382 (IRTF, Informational; "was not selected" in the CFRG competition); OPAQUE: RFC 9807 (IRTF, Informational, 2025) — the three RFCs share one category (corrected §19.29.3) |
| Low-end cost | one Argon2id at join: on the x86_64 emulator 125–128 ms native, 197–269 ms BouncyCastle on ART; ≈ 1.3–3.2 s native, ≈ 3–7 s BouncyCastle estimated on a low-end phone (×15–25, not measured; corrected §19.29.3) | one Argon2id (if the verifier is Argon2id) | several round trips, each a relay session |

**Proposed decision (Q43, re-asked by §19.29.3): (a), the password-sealed invite with Argon2id.** It (i) obeys CP-09 with standard primitives and adds **no** interactive protocol, **no** new relay message and **no** reflex (T40); (ii) puts **no** verifier on members' devices (the sponsor's stored link is Q50), so a seized member device gives no guessing oracle (unlike (b) and augmented (c)); (iii) composes unchanged with D10, the S0-15 committers anchor, `INVITE_ISSUED` and the admission-evidence split (§19.2) — the password gates only the reading of the link; and (iv) leaves a password channel indistinguishable at relays (NI-CH5), though not to a link holder. Its costs: offline guessing from a **leaked link** (a bearer secret shared with few people, single-use, expiring ≤ 7 days; Argon2id-priced; declared, L8-C26), and — corrected by §19.29.3 — **it does not bind insiders**: nothing derived from the password reaches `INVITE_ISSUED`, the join request or the commit, so any member allowed to invite admits whoever it likes, password or not (Q46 (b) lets only committers invite in a password channel; committers could already admit anyone). (b) enforces the password against everyone who lacks it, committers included, but moves the guessing oracle to every member's device and to every `JK_g` holder; (c) adds an interactive PAKE that fights the async, batched, no-reflex admission model and has no vetted-and-current audited crate.

#### 19.28.2 What the password does NOT protect (normative)

- A **member can still share content** — screenshots, forwarding, re-posting; the password restrains no member.
- A **committer can admit anyone** — the password gates the link, not admission; a malicious committer admits any KeyPackage regardless (CO-2 is the committer's policy), and any holder who decrypts an invite can pass the decrypted secrets to a non-password-holder.
- **A member allowed to invite can admit anyone** (added §19.29.3): nothing derived from the password reaches `INVITE_ISSUED`, the join request or the commit, so such a member — even one that never knew the password, having joined before it was set — mints a working invite under a password it makes up, or an open one, with an unmodified client, and every check passes (measured, E-73). Q46 (b) lets only committers invite in a password channel.
- **Every member, and every removed member, knows the password** (every joiner had to), and one recovered password opens every link of the channel until the owner changes it; nothing forces a change when a member is removed (Phase 13 prompts one).
- **The sponsor's device** keeps each sealed link until its expiry unless Q50 (a) — on a seized unlocked device an Argon2id-priced password oracle for up to 7 days after each issue — and the keystore opt-in (Q50 (c)) would keep the password itself.
- A **weak password falls to offline guessing from a leaked link**: about 154–160 guesses per second on one 24-thread host at `kdf_code` 1 (E-73), so a 6-digit PIN falls in about an hour; hence Q44's app-generated passphrase.
- **A link holder learns whether the link needs a password** (its cleartext `kdf_code`; an open link opens from its own salt anyway).
- **A typo cannot be detected**: no verifier exists, so a sponsor that mistypes the password produces a link nobody can open, which expires unconsumed and sets the rotation flag (§6.5).
- It does **not** lock local viewing (the requirement is explicit) and adds **no** cryptographic access control to posts (every member reads every post; the password gates only joining).

#### 19.28.3 Byte formats (normative; TLV numbers do not clash with §5)

**`ChannelInviteV2` (`ghost://channel/`, replaces V1 for every invite, open or password; version byte 2; corrected §19.29.3).**

```
header = version(1) = 0x02 || kdf_code(1) || salt(16)                                    // 18 bytes, in clear
sealed = ChaCha20-Poly1305(k, nonce = 0^12, AD, inner)                                  // 301 + 16 bytes
inner  = the V1 fields of §5.9 from suite to name (253 + name_len bytes), zero-padded to 301
AD     = "ghost/v1/ch/invite-v2" || header                                              (not transmitted)
k      = HKDF-SHA256(ikm, salt = salt, info = "ghost/v1/ch/invite-seal" || kdf_code, L = 32)
ikm    = Argon2id(NFKD(password) as UTF-8, salt, profile(kdf_code), T = 32)   for kdf_code >= 1 (a password invite)
       = salt (16 bytes)                                                      for kdf_code = 0  (an open invite: §5.2 rule (1)'s
                                                                                                 declared exemption, OpenInviteSalt)
link   = "ghost://channel/" || base32(header || sealed), lowercase, unpadded             // 335 bytes; 16 + 536 characters
```

- **One size for every invite:** 18 + 301 + 16 = **335 bytes** whatever the `kdf_code` and the name. **Measured (E-73, `r10d_password.rs` on this construction):** open and password invites with names of 0 and 48 bytes are all 335 bytes; the link is 552 characters, a QR code of version 18 at level M in byte mode (V1's 254-byte link: 423 characters, version 16). **What a link shows** (corrected §19.29.3): the version, `kdf_code` — so any holder learns whether the link needs a password — and a random salt; an open link opens from its own salt; a password link's body does not appear in it. The right password opens it; a wrong password, a flipped body bit and a flipped `kdf_code` are refused (the header is in the AD). *Round 10d* specified a 20-byte header carrying `pw_version` and a 303-byte body (339 bytes, computed, not measured), claimed that a link "does not reveal password-vs-open", and its probe measured another format (a 17-byte header, t = 3, no HKDF salt, labels of its own, no `kdf_code`).
- `kdf_code`: 0 = open; 1 = the P0 Argon2id profile (Q44); any other code is refused (`NON_CANONICAL`), so a future profile is a new code without a format change.
- **No `pw_version` in the link** (corrected §19.29.3): the invitee needs none and the committer reads it from `INVITE_ISSUED`; in round 10d's AD it added nothing (the key already depends on the password), and in clear it told a link holder how many times the password had changed, which links two leaked links of one channel.
- **The AD label is the link's own** (`"ghost/v1/ch/invite-v2"`): round 10d reused `"ghost/v1/ch/invite"`, the domain string of `sponsor_sig` (§5.5).
- The password is normalized to NFKD and encoded in UTF-8 before it crosses JNI (§10.2 fn 14).

**`GhostChannelContext` (§5.3) gains a password policy — no verifier.** `flags` bit 2 = **join requires a password** (0 in an open channel; set/cleared by the owner); a new `uint16 pw_version` after `idle_evict_days`. **The password itself and any value derived from it are never in the context** (that is the whole point of (a) over (b)); the context records only *that* the channel is password-protected and *which* password epoch. Setting a password = set the flag and bump `pw_version`; changing = bump `pw_version` (the flag stays set); **removing = clear the flag, `pw_version` unchanged** (corrected §19.29.3: round 10d's step 1 bumped it on removal too). Each is a GroupContextExtensions commit from rank 0 that every member processes (ADR-27 point 8's "the policy is itself a message shown to everyone"); the validity rules are in §5.3 (a non-decreasing `pw_version`, +1 exactly at a set or change, rank 0 only; with Q46 (b), bit 2 forbids bit 0).

**`INVITE_ISSUED` (§5.5, kind 0x03) gains TLV `0x0015 pw_version` (u16 ≥ 1, authenticated by the post's MLS signature): present, with the context's `pw_version`, exactly when the sponsor issued in a password channel, and absent otherwise** — the one encoding of "open" (corrected §19.29.3: round 10d's "absent = 0" beside an optional TLV gave two encodings of 0, against §5.5's canonical rule). 0x0015 is free (§5.5 uses 0x0010 … 0x0014). No other TLV changes.

#### 19.28.4 Protocol steps (normative)

1. **Owner sets, changes or removes** the password: a policy commit (§19.28.3; a set or change bumps `pw_version`, a removal only clears the flag). With Q45 (a) a set or change also carries the rotation flag (§6.5), so whoever opened a link before it loses the generation's namespace and secrets; the settle voids every unconsumed invite of an older `pw_version` (CO-5), and each sponsor's client tells its user. With Q44 (a) the app generates the password; the owner distributes it out of band. Removing makes future invites open. Phase 13 prompts a change when a member is removed (the removed member knows the password).
2. **Sponsor issues** in a password channel (`CH-INVITE`): a committer (Q46 (b); any member with `members_may_invite` under (a)) types the password — which its client **cannot check** (no verifier), so the UI asks for it twice against typos and reminds the sponsor to share it out of band, separately from the link; Rust draws `salt`, derives `ikm = Argon2id(password, salt, profile)` (Q47 (a)), builds `ChannelInviteV2` with `kdf_code` 1 and the sealed body, and the client posts `INVITE_ISSUED(…, pw_version = context.pw_version)`; the link is shown once (Q50 (a)).
3. **Invitee accepts** (`CH-ACCEPT`): parses the V2 header; for `kdf_code ≥ 1` it prompts for the password, runs Argon2id over `salt`, derives `k` and opens the sealed body (a wrong password or a tampered link → AEAD failure → `WRONG_PASSWORD`, nothing written); then proceeds exactly as §6.2.2 with the decrypted V1 fields. The join request (§5.6) is **unchanged** — the password leaves no trace in it.
4. **Committer admits** (§6.2.4): unchanged, plus one policy check (B), **only while flag bit 2 is set at admission**: `INVITE_ISSUED` carries TLV 0x0015 equal to the context's `pw_version`; an invite without it, or of an older `pw_version`, is refused. In an open channel no password check applies, so an open invite is admitted after the password has been removed (corrected §19.29.3: round 10d's unconditional equality with "absent = 0" refused every open invite after a removal, measured in the model of E-73). A rejoin involves no invite and needs no password; a restore's invite is a password invite in a password channel (Q48 (a); round 10d's "a rejoin/restore carries `pw_version` 0" named a field the join request does not have).

#### 19.28.5 KDF placement, parameters, schema, JNI

- **Where Argon2id runs (Q47; the proposed default is now Rust, §19.29.3).** **(a) Rust:** `argon2` 0.6.0 inside `ghost-client-mls` behind fn 14 (+4 names to G-M1, all MIT OR Apache-2.0); its 64 MiB matrix lives in native memory, off the Java heap, and is zeroized; the password crosses JNI once (§10.2 fn 14). **Measured (E-65):** the RustCrypto `argon2` 0.6.0 reproduces the RFC 9106 §5.3 tag `0d640df5…6b01e659` exactly; **(E-75)** 125–128 ms at `kdf_code` 1 on the x86_64 API 35 emulator. **(b) Kotlin/BouncyCastle** (round 10d's default): `Argon2BytesGenerator` reproduces the same vector on HotSpot and on ART (E-74, E-75), but keeps the whole matrix on the Java heap — OutOfMemoryError at -Xmx72m on HotSpot and at -Xmx64m on ART, against 96 MiB of app heap on AOSP's 1 GB phone profile (minSdk 29; no `largeHeap`) — takes 197–269 ms on ART (1.6–2.1× the native time in the two emulator re-runs, 2.9× in the review's run), would add bcprov to `:messaging` at runtime (today only `androidTestImplementation`, `messaging/build.gradle.kts:77`; round 10d's "already a dependency of `:messaging`" was wrong), would be the one Kotlin key derivation against §10.1's rule and a new site for G-DM3's Kotlin allowlist, and would leave the key on a managed heap nothing scans.
- **Parameters (Q44).** RFC 9106 §4 profiles, **measured on this host (Intel Core Ultra 9 275HX, single-threaded; medians of 7, E-66; re-run in round 10e under a different load, so each is a range):** 64 MiB/t=3/p=4 (RFC second-recommended) **120–354 ms**; 64 MiB/t=1/p=4 **50–126 ms** (p = 1: 46–66 ms); 32 MiB/t=3/p=4 **68–143 ms**; 19 MiB/t=2/p=1 (an OWASP pair) **30–62 ms**; 256 MiB/t=1/p=4 **261–472 ms**. **The lanes (corrected §19.29.3):** single-threaded, p lanes do the same total work (m blocks per pass), and p = 1 and p = 4 took the same time in both implementations (E-66, E-74, E-75); p = 4 lets a multi-core device — or an attacker — finish one evaluation sooner; memory-hardness is set by m (round 10d wrote that p raises it at a compute cost). **Default: m = 64 MiB, t = 1, p = 4** (`kdf_code 1`) — below RFC 9106's second recommended option (t = 3 at 64 MiB), above OWASP's first pair (46 MiB with t = 1). Joining is a rare, deliberate, foreground action, so a KDF of a few seconds is acceptable: the ×15–25 estimate over the emulator figures gives ≈ 1.3–3.2 s natively and ≈ 3–7 s with BouncyCastle on a low-end phone (not measured; round 10d's "2–3 s" scaled the host's Rust figure while proposing BouncyCastle; R-21). Bitwarden's 64 MiB warning concerns iOS autofill; what matters on Android is the Java heap, which only Q47 (b) uses. S2's exit measures time and memory on the emulator and on a phone; a lighter profile is a new `kdf_code`. **Floors (normative, corrected §19.29.3):** OWASP's equal-strength Argon2id pairs — m ≥ 46 MiB with t ≥ 1, m ≥ 19 MiB with t ≥ 2, 12 MiB with 3, 9 MiB with 4, 7 MiB with 5 — and p ≥ 1, as the codec's profile table, which a test asserts (MM-PW3; round 10d's per-parameter floor "m ≥ 19 MiB, t ≥ 1" admitted 19 MiB with one pass). **Strength (Q44):** with (a) the app generates the password (5 words of a 2 048-word list, ≈ 55 bits); a leaked link allows ≈ 154–160 guesses/s on one 24-thread host (E-73).
- **Schema (migration 7, slice S3).** `ch_channel` gains `pw_required` (0/1, flag bit 2 mirrored for the UI) — **not** `pw_version` (corrected §19.29.3: a plaintext per-channel counter of password changes breaks §9's "no counters per channel"; the sponsor reads it from the native state); `ch_invite_issued` gains `pw_version` (NULL or ≥ 1) and voids on a bump; `ch_invite_own.invite_env` is NULL for a password invite (Q50 (a)). **No password, verifier or Argon2id output is stored at rest under Q50 (a)** — the sponsor types the password per issue; under Q50 (b) the stored link is a password oracle until its expiry, under (c) the keystore holds the password. MM-PW5's killer is a T25-style physical scan of the database and the WAL for the password and the Argon2id output after an issue and after a join (round 10d named T3, which scans process outputs, not stored data); native copies are zeroized after each call; what a managed heap may hold is declared, not scanned (a `String` cannot be zeroized, so the facade takes a `CharArray`).
- **JNI (the closed `MlsNative` surface; corrected §19.29.3).** Only fn 14 changes; with Q47 (a): `inviteBuild(h, channelId, gEnv, fields, kdfCode, password?)` and `inviteParse(bytes, password?)` (§10.2), the salt drawn in Rust (the seeded stream in the host build, §12.1), with the codes `PASSWORD_REQUIRED`, `WRONG_PASSWORD`, `NON_CANONICAL`, `UNSUPPORTED_SUITE` and `INVALID_POLICY`; the derivation takes the typed `InvitePasswordKey` or `OpenInviteSalt` (G-M3). With Q47 (b): `inviteHeader(bytes)` returns `kdf_code` and the salt, parsed natively (Kotlin never parses the link), a sponsor's salt is drawn by a native call before the KDF (so it stays in the seeded stream), and the BouncyCastle output crosses as `pwKey32`, a raw key in Kotlin (declared); an absent or zero key for a `kdf_code ≥ 1` link is refused, never used (round 10d's "null/zero for an open invite" was ambiguous, and it reported a wrong password as `NON_CANONICAL`). The surface list (G-M6) grows by these arguments (and `inviteHeader` under (b)); the facade gains the password parameters and results of §10.3.

#### 19.28.6 Tests and mutants

**Tests (corrected §19.29.3).** Vectors: the RFC 9106 §5.3 Argon2id vector in Rust (E-65; and in BouncyCastle under Q47 (b), E-74); `ch_invite.txt` extended to V2 (open and password invites with names of 0 and 48 bytes, all 335 bytes; a wrong password, a tampered body and a tampered `kdf_code` refused; a password link without a password `PASSWORD_REQUIRED`, never opened with an absent or zero key). A **leaked-link oracle (E-67, E-73):** a password link without the password yields no `ns`, `read_base`, `deposit_base` or `join_key`, hence no join request and **no membership and no generation secret** (the inner body does not appear in the link). **Password JN cases:** a stale `pw_version` refused and voided at settle; an open invite refused in a password channel and admitted after the password is removed; with Q46 (b) a non-committer's invite refused by CO-2 (A); with Q45 (a) the set or change rotates. **NI-CH5** (§12.7): password and open channels give the same relay views, with the declared rotation-rate effect. The **T25 password scan** (§11.2). **Fuzzing** of `inviteParse` (arbitrary bytes and arbitrary passwords: never panics, wrong length refused, AEAD failure clean) joins `mls-fuzz` (§12.10).

**Mutants (registered with killers; extend §12.9; MM-PW2 … MM-PW5 restated by §19.29.3).** **MM-PW1** the invite key derived without the password in a password channel (e.g. from the salt only) — killer: the leaked-link oracle (the link opens without the password). **MM-PW2** the committer skips the `pw_version` check, or applies it in an open channel — killer: the password JN cases. **MM-PW3** a `kdf_code` profile below OWASP's floor pairs accepted by the codec — killer: the profile-table assertion (the RFC 9106 vector, at 32 KiB, is below every floor and tests the primitive only; t = 0 is refused by both libraries, so it is no mutant). **MM-PW4** the link length differs between open and password invites or between names, or a password channel is distinguishable at relays — killer: the size-equality test (E-73) and NI-CH5. **MM-PW5** the password or the Argon2id output persisted at rest — killer: the T25-style physical scan of the database and the WAL. A password channel adds no relay observable (the invite is out of band; the join request and `INVITE_ISSUED` are the existing shapes), so JC-1 … JC-7 and NI-CH1 … NI-CH4 are unchanged, and NI-CH5 compares password and open channels directly (round 10d folded that comparison into JC-2, a byte scan, and NI-CH1, whose twins differ only in what one client received, neither of which compares the two kinds of channel).

#### 19.28.7 Effort and identifiers

Effort (F9 pace, §14; restated by §19.29.5): the password adds **+2.75 working days** (S2 +1.0: the V2 invite codec, the seal, Argon2id in Rust with the RFC 9106 vector, and the Argon2id time and memory measured on the emulator and a phone; S3 +0.25: the columns of migration 7; S4 +0.75: `pw_version` in the context, `INVITE_ISSUED` and the conditional committer check, the voiding at settle and the rotation on a change, the JN and leaked-link tests; S9/S11 +0.75: the mutants, the fuzz target, NI-CH5 and the password scan), and the §19.27 review corrections as revised by §19.29 **+0.25 day** (S4 +0.25: the anchor pre-check, the rejoin rule and the set digest; S5 ±0: the refusal-branch rule is replaced by "a lifetime refusal is invalid" and the insider adversary, +0.25, and S0-12's lifetime-pending machinery is removed, −0.25) — **+3.0 days in all, §14's 40.75 → 43.75**. New identifiers: TR-1 … TR-7; Q42 … Q50; `ChannelInviteV2`, `kdf_code`, `pw_version`; `INVITE_ISSUED` TLV 0x0015; `GhostChannelContext` `flags` bit 2 and `pw_version`; MM-44 … MM-46, MM-PW1 … MM-PW5; NI-CH5; R-21; L8-C26; the typed inputs `OpenInviteSalt` and `InvitePasswordKey`; the codes `PASSWORD_REQUIRED`, `WRONG_PASSWORD` and `INVALID_COMMIT(lifetime)`; the findings R10D-P1 … P13 and R10D-V1 … V14; E-61 … E-76. Withdrawn: `clock_blocked`, `ch_candidate.lifetime_pending`, `LIFETIME_PENDING`, MM-27, MM-40, MM-47, L8-C20, the joiner-side liveness timer of TR-4. The residues go to L8-C25 and L8-C26 (Appendix B.3).

### 19.29 Adversarial review of round 10d (round 10e, 2026-09-28)

**Precedence (normative).** This section wins over §19.27 and §19.28, and over the sections they touch, for what it names; each correction is also made in place, citing "(§19.29)". Round 10d (`fd8910d`) was reviewed adversarially under two lenses: protocol and cryptographic soundness (findings **R10D-P1 … P13**) and privacy, metadata, feasibility and consistency (**R10D-V1 … V14**). **Every finding was re-run before its disposition:** the reviews' probes were rebuilt in the spike (`core/tests/r10e_protocol.rs`, `r10e_password.rs`, the rewritten `r10d_password.rs` over `tests/support/invite_v2.rs`, and `r10d_s0_15_anchor.rs` extended), BouncyCastle 1.85.2 was re-run on the host JVM, and BouncyCastle on ART and the native `argon2` on the x86_64 API 35 emulator (evidence **E-69 … E-76**). No finding is rejected; four of their claims are narrowed where the re-run disagreed in degree (R10D-P1 twice, R10D-V1, R10D-V3), and one correction is applied in another form than proposed (R10D-P9) (§19.29.4). **Decisions that need the owner are recorded, not taken:** Q42 … Q48 are re-asked and Q49, Q50 are new (§17); where a question is open, the normative text follows its proposed default and says so.

#### 19.29.1 The KeyPackage lifetime: only the constant lifetime converges (R10D-P1, P7; R10D-V9)

**Findings (confirmed).** (a) *Freeze.* A member of any rank deposits a commit adding a KeyPackage whose finite lifetime has expired. Every member with a correct clock, the owner included, reaches OpenMLS's refusal branch (E-70); under TR-1's rule it is the sole candidate, so every member is `clock_blocked` — not current (§6.3.1) — and CO-7 forbids every pass, the owner's Remove of the attacker included. TR-2's "the junk never blocks" is false. (Narrowed: the block ends when a decidable candidate appears — a member's scheduled self-update, CO-9 — but the attacker can repeat it every epoch.) (b) *Fork.* An honest rank-0 commit adding an honest finite-lifetime KeyPackage and an honest rank-255 self-update for the same epoch: a member whose device clock accepts the KeyPackage merges the first, a member whose clock is past its bound ranks it lowest and merges the second — same epoch, different epoch authenticators (E-69). TR-1's "this converges" and CS-C3's "stalls instead of choosing differently" are false. (c) *Loose ends:* `ch_kp_seen` kept "until the KeyPackage's `not_after`" is forever under the constant lifetime, against a 1 024-row cap; TR-3's "residual honest-skew split" does not exist (CO-2 (A)'s window is relative to the commit's own `commit_minute`); §6.2.4's "the same day d as the lifetime" cannot hold; OpenMLS 0.9.0 ships an uncalled `Lifetime::has_acceptable_range` (84 d + 1 h, `lifetime.rs:113-118`) that would refuse every constant-lifetime KeyPackage if a later version called it. (d) *Precedence:* §19.27 changed the lifetime "for nothing else", leaving §6.2.4 (B), fn 4, §19.4 rules 1 and 3, MM-39, T13 and ADR-40 point 4 requiring the day-aligned form, under which GHOST's own checks would refuse every constant-lifetime KeyPackage (E-62's "default `JoinBuilder`" test bypassed GHOST's join path).

**Why only the constant lifetime converges.** On the refusal branch OpenMLS hides the candidate's sender and so its rank (TR-1, E-61), so a member can only wait — the freeze — or choose without the rank — the fork; S0-12's and round 10c's rules had the same dilemma. A rule converges only if a refusal implies a verdict every member shares. With the constant lifetime no clock refuses a canonical KeyPackage, so a `LifetimeError` means a non-canonical one and the commit is invalid; on the accept branch `KeyPackage::life_time()` shows the non-canonical lifetime, invalid too — **one verdict on both branches, whatever the clock** (E-69, E-70). (Narrowed: the review's "no convergent rule exists with finite lifetimes" is exact for `[d − 4, d + 80]`, which honest device clocks can fall outside; a finite lifetime wide enough that no clock Tor can run with falls outside it — say ±10 years — would behave like the constant one. The constant form is simpler and carries no time at all.)

**Correction (normative under Q42 (a), the proposed default; made in place).** The constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]` is the canonical KeyPackage's and the creator's leaf's (§3.5, §6.2.4 (B), fn 4, ADR-40 points 4 and 7); the KeyPackage's day travels only in `GhostKpBind.day`. CO-2 reads no clock: every Add must carry the constant lifetime, and a `LifetimeError` makes the commit invalid (`INVALID_COMMIT(lifetime)`), never a candidate (CO-2, CO-4, CS-C3, T41, C-15). Withdrawn: S0-12's trusted-day predicate, round 10d's refusal-branch rule, the lifetime-pending state, `clock_blocked` (§6.3.1, §6.11, `ch_channel`), `ch_candidate.lifetime_pending`, `LIFETIME_PENDING` (fns 6, 7, 20), L8-C20, R-14 (closed), MM-27 and MM-40; MM-46 is retargeted to "a `LifetimeError` treated as anything but invalid" and MM-39 to "any lifetime but the constant one admitted". Joins keep `skip_lifetime_validation()` and GHOST checks the constant form on every KeyPackage-source leaf (§6.2.5; §19.4 rules 1 and 3 replaced). `ch_kp_seen` rows live until `GhostKpBind.day + 11` (the last day (B)'s window admits the KeyPackage, d + 7, plus σ and one day; §6.2.4 (B), §6.3.5, §9). G-M7 pins, at every bump, OpenMLS's acceptance of the constant lifetime and its typed refusal of a finite one. T13 reads "constant". Consequence: OpenMLS no longer reads the device clock at any GHOST KeyPackage, so the worlds need no host-clock anchoring for lifetimes (§12.1). **Recorded (Q42 re-asked):** option (b), finite lifetimes, has no known convergent rule and would need another design round.

#### 19.29.2 The committers anchor: capture by a removed committer's fork (R10D-P2, P8; R10D-V2, V10)

**Findings (confirmed).** (a) E-63's forgery was a fresh group naming the real committers; §5.3's validity, which `welcomeJoin` checks on the joined context, refuses it — the owner it names is not a leaf of the forger's group — so E-63 showed only the anchor comparison. The capture that passes every check is a removed committer's **fork of the real group from its last state**: the anchor holds, every committer is a leaf, the joiner applies it (E-71), and likewise a rejoiner (the rejoin pre-check and §5.3 pass, E-71). (b) An **active forger keeps the captured member live**: the joiner merges the forger's committer commits and reads its posts (E-71), so neither `stale_suspected` (CO-12) nor `reinvite_needed` ever fires; its posts, and any REVEAL it sends to a puppet (a DM card and a transferable proof, §6.9), go to the member the channel expelled. (c) Even a silent forger's victim cannot come back in-band: at `reinvite_needed` §6.2.2 builds a restore-purpose KeyPackage, which CO-2 (A) admits only as a swap of a current leaf, and the captured pseudonym never was a member; while `active`, a new invite is refused as `ALREADY_MEMBER`. TR-4's "recovery is `reinvite_needed` — not silent forever" and its joiner timer are withdrawn; T39's "joins only the channel it was invited to" and R-20's "can still capture" understated the residue, and the probe's `ANCHOR_CONFLICT` comment named nothing the design defined. (d) TR-7 compared the joined committers' set digest with the rejoiner's last committers': a legitimate rejoin Welcome is refused once the live branch has added a committer (E-72), while S0-15, §5.4 and §6.7 ask only that the signer be among the last committers; the equality buys nothing, since a forger copies the set.

**Correction (normative; made in place).** (1) The rejoin pre-check verifies the unverified GroupInfo's signature under one of the rejoiner's last committers, with no digest comparison (§5.4, §6.2.5, fn 5; E-72); the join and restore pre-check is unchanged; §5.3's validity of the joined context is stated as part of `welcomeJoin`. (2) The residue is declared as measured (L8-C25, R-20, P-C5, T39, Q41, ADR-43): a committer named in an invite, or among a rejoiner's last committers, even one removed since, can capture the member in a fork of the real group and keep it captured for as long as it sustains the fork (committing, posting, funding the old namespace once its drain ends); only an out-of-band comparison of the fingerprint shows it; once removed, it is the only party that answers the invites its removal voided. (3) UI obligations (Phase 13; no protocol change): the sponsor's client tells its user when a settle voids one of its invites (CO-5, or a password change), so the invitee can be told not to act on the old link; the accept screen asks a joiner to compare the fingerprint with its sponsor after joining. (4) MM-47 is withdrawn with the claim it tested. **Recorded (Q49):** how a captured member returns, and whether to narrow the capture itself — (a) declare it and make the way back explicit: after an out-of-band comparison the user leaves the captured copy and accepts a fresh invite (a join, its pseudonym never having been a member, which works today), and a committer admits a restore-purpose KeyPackage whose pseudonym is not a current member as an Add, so that the `reinvite_needed` path of a silent forger's victim works too; (b) (a) plus an `ANCHOR_CONFLICT` outcome at `CH-ACCEPT`; (c) a subset anchor, which turns the capture into a race the forger usually wins; (d) Q30's sponsor-confirmed admission. The test obligation of the answer replaces MM-47 (SCN-CL joiner-capture variants with a silent and an active forger; the report states the capture's duration).

#### 19.29.3 The channel password (R10D-P3 … P6, P9 … P13; R10D-V1, V3 … V8, V11, V12)

**Findings (confirmed).**
- **Insiders (P3, V1).** Nothing derived from the password reaches `INVITE_ISSUED`, the join request or the commit, so a member allowed to invite — even one that never knew the password, with an unmodified client — mints a working invite under a password it makes up, or an open link with the context's `pw_version` in its TLV; every check passes (E-73: a real V2 link sealed under a made-up password, and the admission rules modelled). "A member without it cannot mint invites" (§19.28.1, Q46) is withdrawn. (Narrowed: the privacy lens's "only (b) or (c) enforce the password against inviting members" holds for (b) only in its signature form — with a MAC verifier every member can mint proofs.)
- **No rotation (P4).** A password set or change left the generation in place, so whoever opened an old link kept `ns_g`, `RB_g`, `DB_g` and `JK_g` until the next rotation (≤ 28 days): listing and depositing in `ns_g` and opening the generation's later join requests (joiners' pseudonym keys) and Welcome blobs.
- **Removal and restore (P5, V6).** After a removal `pw_version` stays ≥ 1 and an open `INVITE_ISSUED` carries no TLV ("absent = 0"), so the check `0 == N` refused every later open invite (E-73's model); "absent = 0" beside an optional TLV gave two encodings of 0; §19.28.3 (removal clears the flag) and §19.28.4 step 1 (every change bumps) disagreed; a restore goes through a fresh invite, sealed in a password channel, so it needs the password against Q48, and "a rejoin/restore carries `pw_version` 0" named a field the join request does not have.
- **Stored secrets (P6, V7).** "No verifier is stored anywhere" is false: the sponsor keeps each sealed link in `ch_invite_own.invite_env` until its expiry — on a seized unlocked device an Argon2id-priced password oracle for up to 7 days after each issue; Phase 13's keystore opt-in stores the password itself; every member and removed member knows the password, and one recovered password opens every link of the channel until it changes.
- **The link (P9, V4, V5).** The cleartext `kdf_code` tells any link holder whether a password is needed (it must: the client has to know whether to prompt), and an open link opens from its public salt anyway; the cleartext `pw_version` added how many times the password changed, linking two leaked links of one channel, and its AD binding added nothing. MM-PW4's "distinguishable link" half was vacuous. ADR-43, D10 and §5.9 still described V1 (254 B) for open invites beside V2 for password invites — password channels visible by size.
- **The probe and the derivation (P12).** The probe measured another construction (t = 3, no HKDF salt, labels of its own, a 17-byte header, no `kdf_code`), so round 10d's 339 bytes were computed, not measured; the AD label reused `sponsor_sig`'s domain string; the open-mode key derives from the public salt against §5.2 rule (1), T36 and G-M3 without an exemption; `pwKey32` was an untyped raw key in Kotlin.
- **Parameters and strength (P10).** m = 64 MiB, t = 1 is below RFC 9106's second recommended option (t = 3); the per-parameter floor "m ≥ 19 MiB, t ≥ 1" admits 19 MiB with one pass, which OWASP pairs with t = 2 (E-73); strength was only "recommended", and a leaked link allows about 154–160 guesses per second on this one host (E-73).
- **Where Argon2id runs (P11, V3).** Q47's default (BouncyCastle) was costed with the other implementation: it keeps the whole matrix on the Java heap — OutOfMemoryError at -Xmx72m on HotSpot and at -Xmx64m on ART (E-74, E-75), against 96 MiB of app heap on AOSP's 1 GB profile — and took 197–269 ms on ART against 125–128 ms natively on the same emulator (E-75; narrowed: 1.6–2.1× in this re-run, 2.9× in the review's run, 250 vs 87 ms), so ≈ 3–7 s on a low-end phone, above round 10d's own drop threshold; `:messaging` has BouncyCastle only as `androidTestImplementation` (`messaging/build.gradle.kts:77`); the Kotlin derivation breaks §10.1's rule and would be a new site for G-DM3's allowlist; a password `String` cannot be zeroized and MM-PW5 did not cover the managed heap. "p raises memory-hardness at a compute cost even without threads" is wrong: single-threaded, p = 1 and p = 4 take the same time (E-66 re-run, E-74, E-75).
- **Killers (V8).** MM-PW3's killer cannot work (the RFC vector's 32 KiB is below every floor; t = 0 is refused by both libraries); MM-PW4's (JC-2 is a byte scan; NI-CH1's twins differ in what one client received) compares no password channel with an open one; MM-PW5's (T3 is the log and crash canary, not a scan of stored data; its all-canary test is still "Neconstruit", `INVARIANTS.md:11`).
- **Interfaces (V11) and UX (V12).** The JNI, facade and §10.1 changes were underspecified (the header parse, an ambiguous "null/zero" key, a wrong password reported as `NON_CANONICAL`, no password parameter or result in the facade, the salt drawn outside the seeded stream); a sponsor's typo cannot be detected and yields a link that expires unconsumed and sets the rotation flag; invites made stale by a password change were not voided at settle; the link grows from 423 to 552 characters (QR version 16 → 18 at level M, E-73).
- **The comparison (P13).** RFC 9106 is an IRTF Informational RFC like RFC 9382 and RFC 9807 (verified); mechanism (b) needs no challenge — a MAC or signature under the verifier over SHA-256(key_package), the binding `invite_sig` uses, cannot be replayed for another KeyPackage — so what rules it out is the verifier on every member device, not CP-09.

**Corrections (normative; made in place).** §19.28.1's table and decision text (the rows on seized devices, who issues, a change, rejoin and restore, relay metadata, CP-09, licences, maturity and cost); §19.28.2's residues (insiders, every member knows the password, the sponsor's stored link, guessing, what a link shows, typos); **the formats** — an 18-byte header without `pw_version`, the AD label `"ghost/v1/ch/invite-v2"`, the V1 fields as a 301-byte body, **every invite a 335-byte V2 link** (measured on the specified construction, E-73; §5.9 superseded; D10, ADR-43), the open seal's salt-derived key as the one declared exemption of §5.2 rule (1) through the typed `OpenInviteSalt` (G-M3); `INVITE_ISSUED`'s TLV present exactly in a password channel; **the check** — only while flag bit 2 is set at admission, TLV present and equal (E-73), removal clearing the flag without a bump, invites of an older `pw_version` voided at settle (CO-5) with the sponsor told; the floors as OWASP's pairs asserted in the profile table; the lanes sentence; the device estimates (R-21, an S2 exit measurement); `ch_channel.pw_version` withdrawn (§9); fn 14, the facade and §10.1 (§10.2, §10.3); the password and the Argon2id output added to T3's canaries and to a T25-style scan (MM-PW5); MM-PW2 … MM-PW4 restated with killers that exist; NI-CH5 (§12.7). **Recorded for the owner:** Q43 (the mechanism, with the corrected comparison), Q44 (strength: an app-generated 5-word passphrase proposed), Q45 (rotation on a set or change, proposed), Q46 (only committers invite in a password channel, proposed), Q47 (Rust proposed instead of BouncyCastle), Q48 (a restore needs the password, proposed), Q50 (the sponsor keeps neither the link nor the password, proposed).

#### 19.29.5 Bookkeeping and effort (R10D-V13, V14)

- §17's round-10d line said the password added "Q42 … Q48"; it added Q43 … Q48. The closure blocker listed only Q1–Q41; it now names Q42 … Q50 and when each is needed (§14). The Revision row's "proposed, not approved" is replaced by the approval of 2026-09-27 and the proposed status of rounds 10d and 10e.
- **The context is 448 bytes** with `pw_version` (folded into §5.3). Every GroupContextExtensions proposal and every Welcome's GroupInfo grows by exactly 2 bytes (one `uint16` inside an opaque vector whose varint length prefix stays 2 bytes), so §5.7's context-change rows, the Remove-with-context unit and the composite rows grow by 2 B and every Welcome by 2 B; the tightest margin (the restore swap, 172 B) carries no context change and is unchanged. G-M7's goldens are fixed in S2 with the real crate; the spike's V10-4 goldens stay as measured (its encoder is round 10c's).
- The spike now builds the specified construction (`r10d_password.rs`, `support/invite_v2.rs`); the header comment "R-PW formats" became "§19.28.3". TR-4's joiner timer is withdrawn with its claim. Appendix B.1 (T39, T41), B.2, B.3 (L8-C20, L8-C25, L8-C26) and ADR-40, ADR-42, ADR-43 (decisions, consequences, tests) follow. The schema's `ch_channel.pw_version` is withdrawn; the password's columns are booked to S3 (migration 7) and NI-CH5 to S11. RFC 9106 is IRTF Informational. The STATUS row no longer says "no stored verifier", calls E-68 a check of sources, and says that §19.27 … §19.29 and Q42 … Q50 await the owner.
- **Effort:** +0.25 working day over round 10d — S2 +0.25 (the Argon2id device measurement), S3 +0.25 and S4 ±0 (the columns moved to S3; the conditional check, the voiding, the rotation on a change and the rejoin rule added to S4), S5 −0.5 (round 10d's refusal-branch rule replaced by "a lifetime refusal is invalid", and S0-12's machinery removed), S9/S11 +0.25 (NI-CH5 and the password scan) — **43.75 working days** (§14, §19.28.7). Q49's and Q50's options are not costed.
- **Checked and consistent (the privacy lens's list, re-checked):** TLV 0x0015 and flag bit 2 are free; migration 7 is the right place; round 10d's §14 arithmetic held; E-61 … E-68 were present; G-M1's "+4 crates" under Q47 (a) (E-76); BouncyCastle reproduces the RFC 9106 vector on HotSpot and on ART (E-74, E-75).

### 19.30 Forward notes (S2a implementation, 2026-09-29)

**Precedence.** These notes record slice S2a: the native crate `ghost-client-mls` as a Rust API without its JNI export, the test-only `ghost-client-mls-kat`, the seeded stream `ghost-host-rng`, the gates G-M1 … G-M4, G-M8 and G-M9, and the `mls-vendor-tests` workflow. They win over §0 … §19.29 for the items they name, and for nothing else. **No wire format, derivation or protocol rule of §5, §6 or §19.26 … §19.29 changes:** the committed vectors `protocol/test-vectors/ch_*.txt` are the formats as specified, generated by the crate and replayed by a second implementation written from this document. Every deviation below is an implementation choice where the design is silent or a signature the JNI of S2b maps, each with its reason; every finding is evidence, with the command or test that shows it. Written on branch `p10-s2a` from `197b2b4`.

**The one thing to read first.** (1) The crate runs the channel protocol end to end on OpenMLS 0.9.0 with the libcrux provider — create, admit (evidence and the canonical 395-byte KeyPackage with the constant lifetime of Q42 (a)), post, batched receive, settle, remove with rotation, rejoin and restore swaps, the password invite, the deferral order of §5.7, CO-2 on both sides, the committers anchor of `welcomeJoin` — through `GhostKv`'s value semantics, with the reload oracle's two halves green. (2) The upstream evidence reproduces with the product code: OpenMLS's own suite at the tag **1 134 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored** (E-59); the WG passive-client replay through `GhostKv` **43 vectors, 252 of 252 epochs, 1 861 reload checks** (V10-12); the golden sizes of V10-4 **byte for byte**, plus the 2 bytes of `pw_version` §19.29.5 predicted and the 48 bytes of the bootstrap in every Welcome (F-4). (3) **V10-6 needs a fourth precondition** (F-2): OpenMLS stores its proposal queue in a hash map's order, so two processes with identical messages wrote different rows until `GhostKv` fixed the order. (4) E-49 is wrong about `MlsGroup::tree_hash` (F-1). (5) The JNI export, the host library and the device measurements are S2b's (item 38).

The **53 storage methods** of the feature set are mapped (41 group-scoped, 12 global), and a test compares the list with the implementation. The **day rewrite** stamps `added_at` with the call's day; **past epochs** are one row each (split), reassembled byte-identically; retention cap 16; a **batched receive** checkpoints before each item, so a failed item rolls back alone; `trialCount` writes nothing. |

| 15 | deviation | §5.4, §5.2 rule (2) | **A rotating commit's bootstrap.** The committer builds the Welcome before it merges, so it cannot export `G_{g+1}` from the new epoch. S2a carries `generation = g + 1`, `open_epoch = e + 1` and **G = 32 zero bytes**; the joiner, which joins at epoch e + 1, exports `G_{g+1}` itself. A bootstrap that opens at the joined epoch with a non-zero G, or opens later, is `INVALID_BOOTSTRAP`. The joiner's `ns(G)` check is unchanged. |
